For a chart $\varphi$ and an open $s$ inside its source, $\varphi(s) = O \cap W$ for an open $W$ ⟦Chapter2/MPolyfold · MChart.exists_isOpen_image_eq⟧, so the model of the restricted chart is a tame retract when that of $\varphi$ is ⟦Chapter3/SolutionGermRecentre · MChart.isTameRetract_image⟧.

These are used in §III.34 (the chart $\psi \circ \varphi$ of Theorem 3.6.8 (preprint 3.54) has the tame model $\psi(O)$) and in §§III.36–III.37 (the shrunk data of Definition 3.2.1 (preprint 3.25) keep tame models). The text's sentence "$X' = \psi(X)$ is tame" in Proposition 3.2.2 (preprint 3.26) is not needed there (§III.24): the tameness Definition 3.2.1 (preprint 3.25) asks for is that of the chart models, which the transport of charts leaves unchanged.

### III.28 Corollary 3.4.3 (preprint 3.39): composition with a given conjugation

**Corollary 3.4.3 (preprint 3.39)** (text): if the sc-germ $g$ is equivalent, by a strong bundle isomorphism $\Phi$, to a basic germ and $s$ is an sc⁺-germ, there is a strong bundle map $\Psi$ such that $((\Psi \circ \Phi)_*(g + s))^1$ is a basic germ. The text's proof: $\Phi_*(g) = h$ is basic, $t = \Phi_*(s)$ is an sc⁺-germ with $t(0) = 0$, $\Phi_*(g + s) = h + t$, and Theorem 3.4.2 (preprint 3.38) gives $\Psi$ with $(\Psi_*(h + t))^1$ basic; take $\Psi \circ \Phi$. Four things are silent: that Theorem 3.4.2's (preprint 3.38) coordinate change is a strong bundle *isomorphism*; that the index raise of a strong bundle isomorphism is one between the shifted structures; that strong bundle isomorphisms and push-forwards compose; and that $\Phi_*(g + s) = h + t$ holds only where the conjugation identity holds. The formalization proves all four and states the corollary with the composite explicit.

*Linear strong bundle isomorphisms.* Two sc-operators $S : E \to E'$, $T : F \to F'$ give the sc-operator $S \oplus T$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicClassComposition · ScOperator.prodMap⟧, and if $S(U) \subseteq U'$ the map $(u, k) \mapsto (Su, Tk)$ is a strong bundle map $U \triangleleft F \to U' \triangleleft F'$ (Definition 2.6.1 (preprint 2.47) as formalized ⟦Chapter2/Tame · IsStrongBundleMap⟧): its base component does not depend on $k$, it is linear in $k$, it preserves the double filtration because sc-operators preserve the levels, it is the sc-operator $S \oplus T$ on $(U \triangleleft F)[0]$, and on $(U \triangleleft F)[1]$ it is $S \oplus T^1$ with $T^1$ the shifted operator ⟦Chapter3/BasicClassComposition · ScOperator.isStrongBundleMap_prodMap⟧. With inverse sc-operators $S'$ (inverse to $S$ on $U$, $U'$) and $T'$ it is a strong bundle isomorphism ⟦Chapter3/BasicClassComposition · ScOperator.isStrongBundleIso_prodMap⟧.

*Theorem 3.4.2's (preprint 3.38) coordinate change as an isomorphism.* In §III.18 the change of coordinates of Theorem 3.4.2 (preprint 3.38) is the pair of linear sc-isomorphisms $\psi = $ `split`, $\Psi = $ `fiber` (inverses `glue`, `fiberInv`) on the index-raised spaces ⟦Chapter3/BasicClass · StabilityData.split⟧ ⟦Chapter3/BasicClass · StabilityData.fiber⟧. Put $\Lambda((a, w), (q, w')) = (\psi(a, w), \Psi(q, w'))$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicClassComposition · StabilityData.linIso⟧. For every $U$ relatively open in the shifted quadrant $(\widetilde C \oplus W)^1$, $\psi(U) = \mathrm{glue}^{-1}(U)$ is relatively open in $(\widetilde C \oplus C) \oplus X^1$ (`glue` is continuous and preserves the quadrant coordinates) ⟦Chapter3/BasicClassComposition · StabilityData.isRelOpen_glue_preimage⟧, and $\Lambda : U \triangleleft (\mathbb R^N \oplus W)^1 \to \psi(U) \triangleleft ((\mathbb R^N \oplus Z) \oplus X^1)$ is a strong bundle isomorphism by the preceding paragraph ⟦Chapter3/BasicClassComposition · StabilityData.linIso_isStrongBundleIso⟧. Before the index raise, the same construction with the level-$0$ operators `split₀`, `fiber₀` gives $\Lambda_0$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicClassComposition · StabilityData.linIso₀⟧, a strong bundle isomorphism $U \triangleleft (\mathbb R^N \oplus W) \to \psi(U) \triangleleft ((\mathbb R^N \oplus Z) \oplus X)$ — the text's "strong bundle map covering $\psi$" ⟦Chapter3/BasicClassComposition · StabilityData.linIso₀_isStrongBundleIso⟧ — and its index raise is $\Lambda$, because `split` and `fiber` are `split₀` and `fiber₀` on the levels $m + 1$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicClassComposition · StabilityData.strongShift_linIso₀⟧.

*The index raise.* For $\Psi : E_0 \times F_0 \to E'_0 \times F'_0$ let $\Psi^1$ be its level-$1$ map on $E_1 \times F_1$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicClassComposition · strongShift⟧. For a strong bundle map it exists on $U_1 \oplus F_1$ and satisfies $\iota\Psi^1(p) = \Psi(\iota p)$, because $\Psi$ maps $U_1 \oplus F_1$ into $U'_1 \oplus F'_1$ (the filtration with $k = 1 \le m + 1$) ⟦Chapter3/BasicClassComposition · IsStrongBundleMap.toBase_strongShift⟧; its base map is the level-$1$ map of the base map of $\Psi$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicClassComposition · IsStrongBundleMap.toBase_base_strongShift⟧. **$\Psi^1$ is a strong bundle map $U^1 \triangleleft F^1 \to U'^1 \triangleleft F'^1$** of the shifted structures ⟦Chapter3/BasicClassComposition · IsStrongBundleMap.shift⟧: independence of the base component and fibre linearity are read through the injective inclusions; the double filtration $(U^1 \triangleleft F^1)_{m,k} = U_{m+1} \oplus F_{k+1}$ is preserved because $k + 1 \le (m + 1) + 1$ whenever $k \le m + 1$, so the filtration property of $\Psi$ applies; $(U^1 \triangleleft F^1)[0]$ is the shift of $(U \triangleleft F)[0]$, so $\Psi^1$ is sc-smooth there by Proposition 1.2.2 (preprint 1.17) ⟦Chapter1/Levels · IsScSmooth.shift⟧; and $(U^1 \triangleleft F^1)[1]$, with levels $E_{m+1} \oplus F_{m+2}$, is the shift of $(U \triangleleft F)[1]$, on which $(\Psi^1)[1]$ agrees with $(\Psi[1])^1$ (the lift of the fibre component to $F'_2$ is the same element, by the injectivity of $F'_2 \to F'_1 \to F'_0$), so Proposition 1.2.2 (preprint 1.17) applies again. For a strong bundle isomorphism with inverse $\Psi'$, $\Psi'^1$ is the inverse of $\Psi^1$, again checked after the inclusion into level $0$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicClassComposition · IsStrongBundleIso.shift⟧. The text uses this whenever it writes $((\Psi \circ \Phi)_*(\cdot))^1$.

*Composition.* The base map of $\Lambda \circ \Phi$ is the composite of the base maps on $U$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicClassComposition · IsStrongBundleMap.base_comp⟧, and $(\Lambda \circ \Phi)[1] = \Lambda[1] \circ \Phi[1]$ on $U \oplus F_1$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicClassComposition · IsStrongBundleMap.shiftFiber_comp⟧; so the composite is a strong bundle map — fibre linearity because over $u$ it is the fibre map of $\Lambda$ over $\varphi(u)$ after that of $\Phi$ over $u$, sc-smoothness on $[0]$ and $[1]$ by the chain rule ⟦Chapter3/BasicClassComposition · IsStrongBundleMap.comp⟧ — and the composite of strong bundle isomorphisms is one, with the inverses composed in the opposite order ⟦Chapter3/BasicClassComposition · IsStrongBundleIso.comp⟧. Push-forwards compose: $(\Lambda \circ \Phi)_*g = \Lambda_*(\Phi_*g)$ on the target of $\Lambda$, since the base of $(\Lambda \circ \Phi)^{-1}$ is $\varphi^{-1} \circ \lambda^{-1}$ and $\Phi(\varphi^{-1}(w), \cdot)$ lies over $w$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicClassComposition · IsStrongBundleIso.pushforward_comp⟧ ⟦Chapter3/GermPushforward · pushforward⟧. The index raise commutes with composition, $(\Lambda \circ \Phi)^1 = \Lambda^1 \circ \Phi^1$ on $U_1 \oplus F_1$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicClassComposition · IsStrongBundleMap.strongShift_comp⟧.

**Corollary 3.4.3 (preprint 3.39) as formalized** ⟦Chapter3/BasicClassComposition · corollary_3_4_3⟧. Let $U$ be relatively open in a partial quadrant $C$ of $E$ with $0 \in U$, $g : U \to F$ sc-smooth (the text's "sc-germ"; sc-smoothness is what makes $(g + s)^1$ take values on level $1$), $d$ conjugation data of $g$ (§III.30: a strong bundle isomorphism $\Phi : U \triangleleft F \to U' \triangleleft (\mathbb R^N \oplus W)$ covering $\varphi$ with $\varphi(0) = 0$ and a basic germ $h$ on a germ $G$ inside $U'$, with $(\Phi(u, g(u)))_2 = h(\varphi(u))$ for $u \in U$ near $0$; the text's "equivalent by a strong bundle isomorphism to the basic germ $\Phi_*g$"), and $s : U \to F^1$ sc-smooth into $F^1$ with $s(0) = 0$ (the text's sc⁺-germ $s : O(C, 0) \to (F, 0)$). The proof, in the order of the Lean:

1. *The sc⁺-germ $t = \Phi[1]_*s$.* Put $t(u') = (\Phi[1](\varphi^{-1}u', s(\varphi^{-1}u')))_2$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicClassComposition · ConjugationData.pushPlus⟧. It is sc-smooth from $U'$ into $(\mathbb R^N \oplus W)^1$: the pairing of $\varphi^{-1}$ with $s \circ \varphi^{-1}$ maps $U'$ into $U \oplus F^1$, and $\Phi[1]$ is sc-smooth there by Definition 2.6.1 (preprint 2.47); restricted to the germ $G \subseteq U'$ it is an sc-smooth germ. And $t(0) = 0$, from $\varphi^{-1}(0) = 0$, $s(0) = 0$ and $\Phi(0, 0)_2 = 0$ (fibre linearity).
2. *Absorbing the $\mathbb R^N$-component.* As in Theorem 3.6.8 (preprint 3.54) (§III.23, step 3), $h' = h + (t_{\mathbb R^N}, 0)$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicClassComposition · ConjugationData.absorb⟧ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFT · ScBanach.qPartOp⟧ is a basic germ with the same contraction map: it is an sc-smooth germ (pairing and the sum operator), $h'(0) = h(0) + 0 = 0$, and its $W$-component is that of $h$. The $W$-component $t_W$ is an sc-smooth germ into $W^1$ vanishing at $0$, so the data $\mathcal S$ of Theorem 3.4.2 (preprint 3.38) exist for $s = t_W$ on the germ $G$ of $h$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicClass · StabilityData.exists_of_scPlus⟧, and $\mathcal S.\mathrm{fsum}\,h' = h + \iota t$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicClass · StabilityData.fsum⟧.
3. *$\Phi_*(g + s) = h + t$ near $0$.* For $u \in U$ at which the conjugation identity holds, $\Phi_*(g + \iota s)(\varphi(u)) = (\Phi(u, g(u) + \iota s(u)))_2$ because $\varphi^{-1}\varphi(u) = u$; by fibre linearity this is $(\Phi(u, g(u)))_2 + (\Phi(u, \iota s(u)))_2 = h(\varphi(u)) + \iota t(\varphi(u))$, where $\iota(\Phi[1](u, s(u)))_2 = (\Phi(u, \iota s(u)))_2$ ⟦Chapter3/SectionCompactness · IsStrongBundleMap.toBase_shiftFiber_snd⟧. So $\Phi_*(g + s) = \mathcal S.\mathrm{fsum}\,h'$ at the points $\varphi(u)$, $u \in U$ near $0$ — not everywhere, as the text's "clearly" suggests.
4. *The composite.* $\Gamma = \Lambda \circ \Phi^1$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicClassComposition · ConjugationData.compIso⟧, with inverse $\Phi'^1 \circ \Lambda^{-1}$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicClassComposition · ConjugationData.compIsoInv⟧, is a strong bundle isomorphism $U^1 \triangleleft F^1 \to \psi(U'^1) \triangleleft ((\mathbb R^N \oplus Z) \oplus X^1)$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicClassComposition · ConjugationData.compDomain⟧: the index raise of $\Phi$ (relatively open $U'_1$ in the shifted quadrant) followed by $\Lambda$, composed by the composition lemma. Its base map is $u \mapsto \psi(\varphi_1(u))$, which vanishes at $0$; $\Gamma_* = \Lambda_* \circ \Phi^1_*$ (composition of push-forwards); and the germ of $\mathcal S.\mathrm{pushMap}\,h'$ lies in the target, because `glue` maps the level-$1$ part of $G$ into $U'_1$.
5. *The basic germ.* $\mathcal S.\mathrm{pushMap}\,h' = \mathrm{fiber} \circ (\mathcal S.\mathrm{fsum}\,h')^1 \circ \mathrm{glue}$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicClass · StabilityData.pushMap⟧ is a basic germ by Theorem 3.4.2 (preprint 3.38) (§III.18) ⟦Chapter3/BasicClass · theorem_3_4_2⟧.
6. *The conjugation identity on level $1$.* For $u \in U^1$ near $0$: $(\Gamma(u, (g + s)^1(u)))_2 = \mathcal S.\mathrm{pushMap}\,h'(\gamma(u))$. Since $\gamma(u) = \mathrm{split}(\varphi_1(u))$ and $\mathrm{glue} \circ \mathrm{split} = \mathrm{id}$, both sides are `fiber` applied to elements of $(\mathbb R^N \oplus W)_1$, which are compared after the injective inclusion into level $0$: $\iota(\Phi^1(u, (g + s)^1(u)))_2 = (\Phi(\iota u, (g + s)(\iota u)))_2$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicClassComposition · IsStrongBundleMap.toBase_strongShift_snd⟧, which is $\mathcal S.\mathrm{fsum}\,h'(\varphi(\iota u))$ by step 3, and this is $\iota$ of $(\mathcal S.\mathrm{fsum}\,h')^1(\varphi_1(u))$. "Near $0$" means: $\iota u$ lies in the neighbourhood where the conjugation identity holds (continuity of $\iota : E_1 \to E_0$), and $\varphi_1(u)$ lies in the level-$1$ part of the germ $G$, a relatively open condition at $0$ by the continuity of $\varphi$ on level $1$.

The conclusion of `corollary_3_4_3` collects these: $\mathcal S$ with $\mathcal S.G = G$ and $\mathcal S.s = t_W$, $t(0) = 0$, $h'$ basic, $\Phi_*(g + s) = \mathcal S.\mathrm{fsum}\,h'$ near $0$, $\Gamma$ a strong bundle isomorphism onto a relatively open subset of $(\widetilde C \oplus C) \oplus X^1$ with $\gamma(0) = 0$ and $\Gamma_* = \Lambda_* \circ \Phi^1_*$, the basic germ $\mathcal S.\mathrm{pushMap}\,h'$ inside the target, and the identity of step 6. In the form of Definition 3.1.11 (preprint 3.11): **$(g + s)^1 : U^1 \to F^1$ is conjugated to a basic germ** on the shifted structures $E^1$, $F^1$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicClassComposition · corollary_3_4_3_isConjugate⟧, with parameter space $\mathbb R^n \oplus C$, target $\mathbb R^N \oplus Z$ and fibre $X^1$ (the text's $\tau : Z \to C$ is not applied, as in §III.18). The text's form "$\Gamma = \Psi \circ \Phi$, then raise the index" is the same map: $\Lambda_0 \circ \Phi$ is a strong bundle isomorphism on level $0$ and $(\Lambda_0 \circ \Phi)^1 = \Lambda_0^1 \circ \Phi^1 = \Lambda \circ \Phi^1$ on $U_1 \oplus F_1$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicClassComposition · ConjugationData.compIso_eq_strongShift⟧. No hypothesis is added beyond the two readings ("sc-germ" as sc-smooth on $U$; "equivalent by a strong bundle isomorphism" as conjugation data). The first reading is the whole-chart one of §III.6, narrower than the text's germs; no theorem of the germ reading uses Corollary 3.4.3 (preprint 3.39) (the facts at the nearby zeros come from the chart at $x_0$ through the linear coordinate change of Theorem 3.4.2 (preprint 3.38), §III.43).

### III.29 Good complements with a projection into the quadrant; Theorems 3.5.6, 3.5.11 and 3.6.8 (3) (preprint 3.43, 3.47 and 3.54) with a closed projecting complement

Two steps of the text depend on the choice of a good complement without saying so. In the proof of Theorem 3.2.3 (preprint 3.27) the retraction $t(v + w) = v + \delta(v)$ is defined on $\Sigma = \{v + y \in C : v \in V,\ y \in Y\}$, which the text calls relatively open in $C$; §III.24 shows that this fails for the good complement $Y = \{x + y - z = 0\}$ of $N = \mathbb R(1, 1, 1)$ in $C = [0, \infty)^3$, and makes the remedy a hypothesis: condition (b) of the regularity conditions, $\pi(O) \subseteq C$ for the projection $\pi$ onto $N$ along $Y$ ⟦Chapter3/TangentGerm · MPolyfold.TangentGermChart.IsRegular⟧. In the proof of Theorem 3.5.6 (preprint 3.43) (Lemma 3.5.10 (preprint 3.46)) a good complement $Z$ of $\widetilde K = \ker DH(0)$ is produced by projecting a good complement of $K'$. This section shows that for solution sets the complement can be chosen once and for all, at the bottom of the chain Theorem 3.8.22 (preprint 3.78) → Theorem 3.5.6 (preprint 3.43) → Theorem 3.5.11 (preprint Corollary 3.47) → Theorem 3.6.8 (3) (preprint 3.54), so that its projection maps the partial quadrant into itself; condition (b) then holds for the chart of §III.34 without any hypothesis (Findings, Chapter 3, items 130–133). Along the way the closedness of the complement, which Lemma 3.6.9 (preprint 3.55) and Definition 3.2.1 (preprint 3.25) need (items 49, 81), is recorded in the statements (item 93).

**The projection property.** For subspaces $N$, $Y$ of a vector space and a set $C$, $N$ *projects $C$ into itself along $Y$* if $z \in C$, $k \in N$ and $z - k \in Y$ imply $k \in C$ ⟦Chapter3/GoodComplementProjection · ProjectsInto⟧. The definition avoids naming the projection; when $E = N \oplus Y$ it says exactly that the projection $\pi$ onto $N$ along $Y$ maps $C$ into $C$ ⟦Chapter3/GoodComplementProjection · ProjectsInto.projectionOnto_mem⟧ ⟦Chapter3/GoodComplementProjection · projectsInto_of_projectionOnto_mem⟧ ($z - \pi z \in Y$, and conversely $z - k \in Y$, $k \in N$ force $\pi z = k$). It is carried by a linear isomorphism $S$ with $x \in C \iff Sx \in C'$: given $z' \in C'$, $k \in N$ and $z' - Sk = Sy$ with $y \in Y$, the point $S^{-1}z'$ lies in $C$ and $S^{-1}z' - k = y$, so $k \in C$ and $Sk \in C'$ ⟦Chapter3/GoodComplementProjection · ProjectsInto.map⟧, in particular for continuous linear isomorphisms ⟦Chapter3/GoodComplementProjection · ProjectsInto.mapL⟧ — the analogue of Lemma 3.5.9 (preprint 3.45) (§III.14).

**The finite-dimensional lemma.** *If the finite-dimensional subspace $N$ is in good position to the partial quadrant $C$ of the sc-Banach space $E$, then $N$ has a closed good complement $Y$ such that the projection onto $N$ along $Y$ maps $C$ into $N \cap C$* ⟦Chapter3/GoodComplementProjection · exists_goodComplement_projectsInto⟧. The text has no such statement; Definition 3.1.24 (preprint 3.21) and Proposition 3.1.25 (preprint 3.22) do not need it. The proof works in the coordinates of §III.14: after a presentation, $C = \{\ell_i \ge 0\}$ for continuous functionals $\ell_1, \dots, \ell_n$ jointly onto $\mathbb R^n$, the cone $Q = N \cap C$ is $\{x \in N : \ell_i(x) \ge 0\}$, $\Sigma$ is the set of indices vanishing at some nonzero point of $Q$, and for a set $S$ of indices $Q_S = (\ell_i)_{i \in S} : N \to \mathbb R^S$ ⟦Chapter3/GoodComplementProjection · coordOn⟧ (for $S = \Sigma$ it is the map $Q_\Sigma$ of Proposition 3.1.25 (preprint 3.22) ⟦Chapter3/GoodComplementProjection · sigmaCoord_eq_coordOn⟧).

*Step 1: the choice of coordinates* ⟦Chapter3/GoodComplementProjection · exists_coordSet⟧. Wanted is a set $S$ of indices with (1) $Q_S$ onto, (2) $Q = \{x \in N : \ell_i(x) \ge 0 \text{ for } i \in S\}$, (3) every $\ell_i$, $i \notin S$, positive on $Q \setminus 0$. Property (3) holds for $S = \Sigma$ by the definition of $\Sigma$ ⟦Chapter3/GoodPosition · pos_of_not_mem_vanIdx⟧, and (1) for $S = \Sigma$ is the consequence of Lemma 3.8.8 (preprint 3.65) proved in §III.14 ⟦Chapter3/GoodPosition · sigmaCoord_surjective⟧. Three cases, those of the normal form. If $Q = N$, take $S = \Sigma$; (2) is trivial. If $\Sigma \ne \emptyset$, take $S = \Sigma$; (2) is the segment argument of Proposition 3.1.25 (preprint 3.22) ⟦Chapter3/GoodPosition · mem_quadCone_of_vanIdx⟧. In the remaining case, $\Sigma = \emptyset$ and $Q \ne N$, the choice $S = \Sigma$ fails, and here the sketch of Finding 83, which takes $S = \Sigma$ throughout, misses a case (item 131). With $S = \emptyset$, (2) would say $Q = N$; and the formula of Step 4 below would reduce to an arbitrary projection onto $N$, which need not map $C$ into $C$: for $C = [0, \infty)^2$ and $N = \mathbb R(1, 1)$ every nonzero point of $Q$ has both coordinates positive, so $\Sigma = \emptyset$; the line $\mathbb R(1, 2)$ is a good complement of $N$ (for $|s| \le \varepsilon|t|$ the coordinates of $t(1, 1) + s(1, 2)$ have the sign of $t$), and the projection along it maps $(0, 1) = -(1, 1) + (1, 2)$ to $-(1, 1) \notin C$. The formal proof keeps one coordinate. Some $x_1 \in N$ lies outside $C$, so there is at least one coordinate, $j_0$ say, and $S = \{j_0\}$. An interior point $y$ of $Q$ is nonzero — if $0$ were interior, $Q$ would contain a ball of $N$ around $0$ and, being closed under positive scaling, all of $N$ ⟦Chapter3/GoodComplementProjection · mem_quadCone_of_zero_mem_interior⟧ ⟦Chapter3/GoodPosition · smul_mem_quadCone⟧ — so $\ell_{j_0}(y) > 0$ by (3), and $Q_{\{j_0\}}$ is onto: $k \mapsto (k/\ell_{j_0}(y))\,y$ is a preimage. Property (2) for $S = \{j_0\}$ is the next step.

*Step 2: the segment argument for any $S$* ⟦Chapter3/GoodComplementProjection · mem_quadCone_of_coordSet⟧. Suppose (3) holds for $S$, some $i_0 \in S$ is positive at a point $y \in Q$, and $x \in N$ has $\ell_i(x) \ge 0$ for $i \in S$. The set $T = \{s \ge 0 : x + sy \in Q\}$ is nonempty (for $i \in S$, $\ell_i(x) + s\ell_i(y) \ge 0$ for all $s \ge 0$; for $i \notin S$, $\ell_i(y) > 0$, so large $s$ work), closed and bounded below; let $s^* = \min T$. If $s^* > 0$, the point $z = x + s^*y$ is nonzero, since $\ell_{i_0}(z) \ge s^*\ell_{i_0}(y) > 0$; so by (3) all $\ell_i$, $i \notin S$, are positive at $z$ and, by continuity, at $x + sy$ for $s$ near $s^*$; the coordinates in $S$ stay $\ge 0$ for all $s \ge 0$; so a point $s_1 = \max(0, s^* - \delta/2) < s^*$ lies in $T$, contradicting minimality. Hence $s^* = 0$ and $x \in Q$. This is the argument of Proposition 3.1.25 (preprint 3.22), Case C (§III.14), with the positivity outside $S$ as the only input; with $S = \{j_0\}$ and $y$ interior it gives (2) in the third case of Step 1.

*Step 3: a lower bound* ⟦Chapter3/GoodComplementProjection · exists_coord_ge⟧. A coordinate positive on $Q \setminus 0$ is bounded below by $c|x|$ on $Q$, $c > 0$: its minimum on the compact set $Q \cap \{|x| = 1\}$ is positive, and $Q$ is a cone.

*Step 4: the projection and the estimate* ⟦Chapter3/GoodComplementProjection · exists_proj_quadCone⟧. Take $S$ from Step 1, a continuous projection $p_N : E_0 \to N$ (Hahn–Banach, $N$ finite-dimensional), a linear right inverse $g$ of $Q_S$, and
$$\pi(z) = p_N(z) + g\big((\ell_i(z - p_N z))_{i \in S}\big).$$
Then $\pi$ is continuous, $\pi = \mathrm{id}$ on $N$ (the bracket vanishes), and $\ell_i(\pi z) = \ell_i(p_Nz) + \ell_i(z - p_Nz) = \ell_i(z)$ for $i \in S$, so for $z \in C$ the coordinates of $\pi z$ in $S$ are $\ge 0$ and $\pi z \in Q$ by (2): $\pi(C) \subseteq N \cap C$. The kernel of $\pi$ is a complement of $N$, and it is a good complement. For $p \in \ker\pi$ the coordinates in $S$ vanish ($\ell_i(p) = \ell_i(\pi p) = 0$). Let $c_i$ be the constants of Step 3 for $i \notin S$ and $\varepsilon' = \min_i c_i/(\|\ell_i\| + 1)$ ⟦Chapter3/GoodComplementProjection · exists_pos_le_of_finite⟧. Let $x \in N$, $p \in \ker\pi$, $|p| \le \varepsilon'|x|$. If $x \in C$: for $i \in S$, $\ell_i(x + p) = \ell_i(x) \ge 0$; for $i \notin S$, $\ell_i(x + p) \ge c_i|x| - \|\ell_i\|\,|p| \ge c_i|x| - \|\ell_i\|\varepsilon'|x| \ge 0$. If $x + p \in C$: the coordinates of $x$ in $S$ are those of $x + p$, hence $\ge 0$, and $x \in Q$ by (2). The reverse implication needs no estimate.

*Step 5: partial quadrants.* For a partial quadrant presented by an sc-isomorphism $T$ the functionals are the coordinate functions of $T$, continuous ⟦Chapter3/GoodComplementProjection · continuous_coordFun⟧ and jointly onto ⟦Chapter3/GoodPosition · coordFun_surj⟧; good position supplies a good complement $P$ with its constant and the interior point. Step 4 then gives a continuous projection $\pi : E_0 \to N$ with $\pi(C) \subseteq C$ whose kernel is a good complement ⟦Chapter3/GoodComplementProjection · exists_proj_goodComplement⟧ ($\ker\pi$ is a topological complement of $N$, as the kernel of a continuous projection). With $Y = \ker\pi$, closed as the kernel of a continuous map, the projection property is immediate: $z \in C$, $k \in N$, $z - k \in Y$ give $k = \pi k = \pi z \in C$ ⟦Chapter3/GoodComplementProjection · exists_goodComplement_projectsInto⟧.

**Theorem 3.5.6 (preprint 3.43) with a prescribed good complement.** In the text's proof the complement $Z$ of $\widetilde K = \ker DH(0)$ in $\mathbb R^{n'}$ comes from a good complement of $K' = \{(\alpha, L\alpha) : \alpha \in \widetilde K\}$ through Lemma 3.5.10 (preprint 3.46) (§III.20). Nothing later uses that origin. *For every good complement $Z$ of $\widetilde K$ (constant $\varepsilon$), $Z \oplus W$ is a good complement of $K'$ with respect to $\widetilde C \oplus W$, with constant $\varepsilon/(\|L\| + 1)$* ⟦Chapter3/GoodComplementProjection · IsGoodComplement.prod_top_graph⟧. The splitting: $K' \cap (Z \oplus W) = 0$ because $\widetilde K \cap Z = 0$, and $(a, w) = (\alpha, L\alpha) + (z, w - L\alpha)$ for $a = \alpha + z$. The estimate: with the maximum norm, $|(\alpha, L\alpha)| \le (\|L\| + 1)|\alpha|$, so $|(z, w)| \le \varepsilon/(\|L\| + 1)\cdot|(\alpha, L\alpha)|$ gives $|z| \le \varepsilon|\alpha|$, and $(\alpha, L\alpha) + (z, w) \in \widetilde C \oplus W$ iff $\alpha + z \in \widetilde C$ iff $\alpha \in \widetilde C$ iff $(\alpha, L\alpha) \in \widetilde C \oplus W$ ⟦Chapter3/LocalSolvability · graphMap_mem_prod_iff⟧. This is the second half of the proof of Lemma 3.5.10 (preprint 3.46) for an arbitrary $Z$; Lemma 3.5.10 (preprint 3.46) itself is needed only for the good position of $\widetilde K$. The projection property passes the same way: if $z \in \widetilde C \oplus W$ and $z - (\alpha, L\alpha) \in Z \oplus W$, then $z_1 - \alpha \in Z$, so $\alpha \in \widetilde C$ when $(\widetilde K, Z)$ has the property, and $(\alpha, L\alpha) \in \widetilde C \oplus W$ ⟦Chapter3/GoodComplementProjection · ProjectsInto.prod_top_graph⟧; the projection along $Z \oplus W$ is $(a, w) \mapsto (\pi_Z a, L\pi_Z a)$, and $\widetilde C \oplus W$ constrains only the first component.

So the data of the proof of Theorem 3.5.6 (preprint 3.43) (§III.20: the derivative data, the graph description $K' = \operatorname{graph}(L|_{\widetilde K})$ of Lemma 3.5.8 (preprint 3.44), a complement $Z$, and the solution $\sigma$ of $H(k + \sigma(k)) = 0$ from Theorem 3.8.22 (preprint 3.78) with its uniqueness constants ⟦Chapter3/LocalSolvability · ShiftedBasicData.Sol⟧) exist with any prescribed good complement $Z$ of $\widetilde K$ ⟦Chapter3/GoodComplementProjection · ShiftedBasicData.exists_sol_of_goodComplement⟧: $K'$ is the graph ⟦Chapter3/LocalSolvability · ShiftedBasicData.Deriv.ker_Dg_eq⟧, so $\widetilde K$ is in good position (Lemma 3.5.10 (preprint 3.46)) ⟦Chapter3/LocalSolvability · lemma_3_5_10_isInGoodPosition⟧, $Z \oplus W$ is a good complement of $K'$ by the lemma above, and Theorem 3.8.22 (preprint 3.78) is applied to $H$ with $Z$ ⟦Chapter3/QuadrantIFT · theorem_3_8_22⟧. Choosing $Z$ by the finite-dimensional lemma in $\mathbb R^{n'}$ (constant sc-structure, partial quadrant $\widetilde C$) gives data whose $Z$ projects $\widetilde C$ into $\widetilde K \cap \widetilde C$ ⟦Chapter3/GoodComplementProjection · ShiftedBasicData.exists_sol_proj⟧.

**Theorem 3.5.6 (preprint 3.43), closed and projecting.** With the hypotheses of Theorem 3.5.6 (preprint 3.43) (§III.20: $\widetilde C$ a partial quadrant, $Dg(0)$ onto, $K' = \ker Dg(0)$ in good position, the regularity of Lemma 3.5.5 (preprint 3.42) as a hypothesis), the conclusions of the level-wise form ⟦Chapter3/LocalSolvability · theorem_3_5_6_sc_smooth⟧ hold with a good complement $Y = Z \oplus W$ that is **closed** ⟦Chapter3/LocalSolvabilityClosed · theorem_3_5_6_closed⟧ — $Z$ is finite-dimensional, hence closed, and $Z \times W_0$ is closed — and, with the data of the last paragraph, that has the **projection property** for $\widetilde C \oplus W$ ⟦Chapter3/GoodComplementProjection · theorem_3_5_6_proj⟧ (rewrite $K'$ as the graph and apply the passage of the property to $Z \oplus W$). The other conclusions ($V$, $\tau$ with values in $Y$ and $W$-components on level $1$, $C^1$, $\tau(0) = 0$, $D\tau(0) = 0$, the graph of zeros, the $C^k$ lifts $\tau_k$, the neighbourhood $U''$ in which every zero lies on the graph) are those of §III.20, read off the same data ⟦Chapter3/LocalSolvability · ShiftedBasicData.Sol.exists_contDiffOn_level⟧ ⟦Chapter3/LocalSolvability · ShiftedBasicData.Sol.exists_eq_add_τ⟧.

**Theorem 3.5.11 (preprint Corollary 3.47), closed and projecting** ⟦Chapter3/LocalSolvabilityClosed · theorem_3_5_11_proj⟧. For a basic germ $f$, the data $\mathcal S$ of Theorem 3.4.2 (preprint 3.38) with $D(f + \iota s)(0)$ onto and kernel $K$ in good position to $\widetilde C \oplus W$: the conclusions of the level-wise Theorem 3.5.11 (preprint Corollary 3.47) ⟦Chapter3/LocalSolvability · theorem_3_5_11_sc_smooth⟧ hold with $Y$ closed and $K$ projecting $\widetilde C \oplus W$ into itself along $Y$. The proof is that of §III.20 with the two new properties carried along. Let $S$ be the linear coordinate change $\psi(a, w) = ((a, (1 - P_1)w), P_1w)$ of Theorem 3.4.2 (preprint 3.38) on level $0$, a continuous linear isomorphism with inverse the gluing map. The push-forward $g = \Phi_*(f + s)$, packaged as data of Theorem 3.5.6 (preprint 3.43) ⟦Chapter3/LocalSolvability · StabilityData.toShiftedBasicData⟧, has $Dg(0) = \Psi \circ D(f + \iota s)(0) \circ S^{-1}$ ⟦Chapter3/LocalSolvability · StabilityData.scDerivGerm_pushMap₀⟧, so $\ker Dg(0) = S(K)$, $Dg(0)$ is onto, and $K$ is finite-dimensional (Lemma 3.5.8 (preprint 3.44) for $g$ and the injectivity of $S^{-1}$). $S$ carries $\widetilde C \oplus W$ onto $(\widetilde C \oplus C_s) \oplus X$, where $C_s$ is the finite-dimensional space of the splitting of Theorem 3.4.2 (preprint 3.38) (§III.18) and $\widetilde C \oplus C_s$ is a partial quadrant of $\mathbb R^n \oplus C_s$ ⟦Chapter3/LocalSolvability · IsPartialQuadrant.const_prod_univ⟧; so $S(K)$ is in good position (Lemma 3.5.9 (preprint 3.45), §III.14). Theorem 3.5.6 (preprint 3.43), closed and projecting, for $g$, with Lemma 3.5.5 (preprint 3.42) for its regularity hypothesis ⟦Chapter3/LocalSolvability · StabilityData.lemma_3_5_5⟧, gives $Y'$, $V'$, $\tau$. Transported back: $Y = S^{-1}(Y')$ is a good complement of $K = S^{-1}(S(K))$ (Lemma 3.5.9 (preprint 3.45)) ⟦Chapter3/GoodPosition · IsGoodComplement.map⟧, closed as the image of a closed set under a linear homeomorphism, and projecting, by the transport of the projection property ⟦Chapter3/GoodComplementProjection · ProjectsInto.mapL⟧; $V = (S|_K)^{-1}(V')$ and $\sigma = S^{-1} \circ \tau \circ S|_K$. The $W$-component of $\sigma(y)$ is $c + w$ with $c \in C_s \subseteq W_\infty$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicClass · StabilityData.Cs_le_smooth⟧ and $w$ the level-$1$ $X$-component of $\tau$, so it lies on level $1$; $D\sigma(0) = S^{-1} \circ D\tau(0) \circ S|_K = 0$, by the chain rule within $V$, which has unique differentiability at $0$ as a relatively open subset of the convex cone $K \cap C$ with interior; the graph points are zeros because the linear fibre map of Theorem 3.4.2 (preprint 3.38) is injective; the lifts are $\psi^{-1}$ on level $k$ applied to $\tau_k$; and $U'' := S^{-1}(U'')$. The form with only the closedness is a corollary ⟦Chapter3/LocalSolvabilityClosed · theorem_3_5_11_closed⟧.

**Theorem 3.6.8 (3) (preprint 3.54), closed and projecting** ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTG · theorem_3_6_8_graph_proj⟧. The graph description of §III.23 — for a section $f$ of a strong bundle whose charts have tame base retractions, $f(x_0) = 0$ and $(f, x_0)$ in good position: the chart $\varphi$ centred at $x_0$, the conjugating sc-diffeomorphism $\psi$, the germ $h + t$ with surjective linearization and kernel $K$ in good position, $Y$, $V'$, $\sigma$, and the open $V_x \ni x_0$ with $\psi\varphi(\{y \in V_x : f(y) = 0\}) = \{v + \sigma(v) : v \in V'\}$ — is proved with Theorem 3.5.11 (preprint Corollary 3.47) closed and projecting in place of the plain Theorem 3.5.11 (preprint Corollary 3.47), and the statement now records five more facts. (i) The inverse $\psi'$ of $\psi$, sc-smooth on $U'$, with $\psi'\psi = \mathrm{id}$ on $U$ and $\psi\psi' = \mathrm{id}$ on $U'$ (the base maps of the conjugating strong bundle isomorphism and its inverse); §III.34 needs it to make $\psi \circ \varphi$ a chart. (ii) $Y$ is closed, and (iii) $K$ projects $C' = \widetilde C \oplus W$ into itself along $Y$, both from Theorem 3.5.11 (preprint Corollary 3.47) — the shrinking of $V'$ in step 6 of §III.23 ($V' = V_0 \cap O_1$ with $O_1$ open, chosen so that $v + \sigma(v)$ stays in $\Omega = U'' \cap W'$) does not touch $Y$. (iv) $K$ is a smooth subspace ⟦Chapter3/SolutionGermRegularity · StabilityData.ker_Dfs_le_smooth⟧ (proved in §III.34). (v) Every $v + \sigma(v)$, $v \in V'$, is a zero of $h + t$ in the germ, and for every $k \ge 1$ the $C^k$ lift $\sigma_k$ of Theorem 3.5.11 (1) (preprint Corollary 3.47) survives the shrinking: on $V_k \cap O_1$, relatively open in $K \cap C'$, containing $0$ and contained in $V'$, it is still $C^k$ and still lifts $\sigma$. The theorem of §III.23, with the same statement except the projection property, is a corollary ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTG · theorem_3_6_8_graph⟧.

*Where the formal route departs from the text.* The text never chooses the good complement; Definition 3.2.1 (preprint 3.25) lets it be any good complement, and for an arbitrary subset with the tangent germ property the repair of the claim "$\Sigma$ is relatively open" would need a re-graphing over a better complement, which is not done ($\pi(O) \subseteq C$ remains a hypothesis of Theorem 3.2.3 (preprint 3.27), §III.24). For solution sets the complement is not given but produced, at Theorem 3.8.22 (preprint 3.78), and every link of the chain accepts a prescribed complement; so the repair is a choice made at the bottom of the chain, not a change in the proof of Theorem 3.2.3 (preprint 3.27) (item 130). The closedness of $Y$ is part of the text's sc-complement and is recorded because Lemma 3.6.9 (preprint 3.55) and Definition 3.2.1 (preprint 3.25) use it at the nearby zeros (items 49, 81, 93).

### III.30 Proposition 3.6.5 (preprint 3.52) with its index

**Proposition 3.6.5 (preprint 3.52)** (text): the linearization $Tf(x) : T_xX \to Y_x$ of an sc-Fredholm section at a zero is an sc-Fredholm operator; in the proof (and in Theorem 3.6.8 (preprint 3.54)) its index is the index $n - N$ of the basic germ $h : O(([0, \infty)^k \oplus \mathbb R^{n-k}) \oplus W, 0) \to (\mathbb R^N \oplus W, 0)$ to which $g - s$ is conjugated. The statements of §III.21 state the index as "$n - N$ for some natural numbers $n$, $N$", which every integer is. The reason is that the conjugation of Definition 3.1.11 (preprint 3.11) is an existential proposition ⟦Chapter3/StrongBundleSections · IsConjugateToBasicGerm⟧, so the dimensions of the basic germ cannot be named outside it. Here the witnesses become data.

*Conjugation data* ⟦Chapter3/FredholmGermIndex · ConjugationData⟧: finite-dimensional normed spaces $P_n$ (the text's $\mathbb R^n$) and $Q$ (the text's $\mathbb R^N$), an sc-Banach space $W$, a partial quadrant $\widetilde C \subseteq P_n$, a relatively open $U' \subseteq \widetilde C \oplus W$, a strong bundle isomorphism $\Psi : U \triangleleft F \to U' \triangleleft (Q \oplus W)$ with inverse $\Psi'$ and $\psi(0) = 0$ for its base map, a germ $G$ with $G_0 \subseteq U'$, a basic germ $h$ on $G$ with contraction map $B$, and $(\Psi(u, k(u)))_2 = h(\psi(u))$ for $u \in U$ near $0$. A map is conjugated to a basic germ iff such data exist ⟦Chapter3/FredholmGermIndex · isConjugateToBasicGerm_iff_nonempty⟧.

**The derivative of a conjugated map** ⟦Chapter3/FredholmGermIndexG · ConjugationData.scDeriv_isFredholm_index⟧. Let $U$ be relatively open in the partial quadrant $C$, $0 \in U$, $k$ an sc¹-germ with derivative family $Dk$ on a germ $G_k$ with $(G_k)_0 \subseteq U$, $k(0) = 0$, and $d$ conjugation data of $k$. Then $Dk(0) : E_0 \to F_0$ is Fredholm of index $\dim P_n - \dim Q$; for $k$ sc-smooth on $U$ this is the whole-chart form ⟦Chapter3/FredholmGermIndex · ConjugationData.scDeriv_isFredholm_index_wholeChart⟧. The proof is that of §III.21, step (a), with the dimensions kept:

*(a)* $Dh(0)$ is Fredholm of index $\dim P_n - \dim Q$ (Proposition 3.1.10 (preprint 3.10) for the basic germ on level $0$) ⟦Chapter3/FredholmGermLinearization · proposition_3_1_10_basic_level_zero⟧.

*(b)* $\Gamma(0)Dk(0) = Dh(0)D\psi(0)$, with $\Gamma(0) = (\Psi(0, \cdot))_2$ the fibre operator of $\Psi$ at $0$ ⟦Chapter3/FredholmGermLinearization · IsStrongBundleMap.fiberCLM⟧. The left side is the derivative at $0$ of $u \mapsto (\Psi(u, k(u)))_2$, because $k(0) = 0$ ⟦Chapter3/FredholmGermLinearization · IsStrongBundleMap.scDeriv_fiber_comp⟧. For the right side, let $N_1$ be an open neighbourhood of $0$ on which the conjugation identity holds; the germ $\{U \cap N_1\}$ has a subgerm $G_1$ mapped by $\psi$ into $G$ level by level ⟦Chapter3/GermFilling · ScNbhdGerm.preimage⟧; on $G_1$ the chain rule for germs gives the derivative family $Dh(\psi_1(x))D\psi(x)$ of $h \circ \psi$ ⟦Chapter3/Germ · IsSc1GermWith.comp⟧; and $u \mapsto (\Psi(u, k(u)))_2$, an sc¹-germ on $G_k$ by the chain rule with the whole-chart outer map (§III.48), agrees with $h \circ \psi$ on $G_1 \cap G_k$, so the two derivative families agree at $0$ (uniqueness of the derivative of a germ at points of $U_1$) ⟦Chapter3/Germ · IsSc1Germ.scDerivGerm_eqOn⟧, with $\psi_1(0) = 0$.

*(c)* $D\psi(0)$ and $D\psi'(0)$ are inverse to each other (chain rule for $\psi' \circ \psi = \mathrm{id}$ on $U$ and $\psi \circ \psi' = \mathrm{id}$ on $U'$) ⟦Chapter2/DegeneracyIndex · scDeriv_comp_eq_self⟧, and so are $\Gamma(0)$ and the fibre operator $\Gamma'(0)$ of $\Psi'$ at $0$: $\Psi'(\Psi(0, y)) = (0, y)$ with $\Psi(0, y) = (0, \Gamma(0)y)$ because $\psi(0) = 0$, and symmetrically.

*(d)* Hence $Dk(0) = \Gamma(0)^{-1} \circ Dh(0) \circ D\psi(0)$, a Fredholm operator composed with isomorphisms on both sides, of the same index ⟦Chapter3/FredholmGermLinearization · isFredholm_equiv_comp_equiv⟧.

Consequently **the index $n - N$ does not depend on the conjugation**: two conjugation data $d$, $d'$ of the same $k$ give $\dim d.P_n - \dim d.Q = \mathrm{ind}\,Dk(0) = \dim d'.P_n - \dim d'.Q$ ⟦Chapter3/FredholmGermIndexG · ConjugationData.index_eq⟧. This is the text's remark after Theorem 3.4.2 (preprint 3.38) that "the Fredholm index is invariant under strong bundle isomorphisms", for the conjugations of one germ.

**Proposition 3.6.5 (preprint 3.52) in the chart, with the index** ⟦Chapter3/FredholmGermIndexG · proposition_3_6_5_local_index⟧. Hypotheses: $U$ relatively open in $C$, $R$ a strong bundle retraction on $U \triangleleft F$ with base retraction $r$, $0 \in U$ and $0 \in O = r(U)$; $g$ a filling germ of the principal part $\hat f$ (§III.3) and $Dg$ a derivative family of $g$ on a germ $G$ with $G_0 \subseteq U$; $\hat f(0) = 0$ and $(x, \hat f(x)) \in K$ for $x \in O$; $s$ an sc⁺-germ with derivative family $Ds$ and $\iota s(0) = g(0)$; and conjugation data $d$ of $k = g - \iota s$. Conclusion: $Dg(0) : E_0 \to F_0$ and $Tf(0) = Dg(0)|_{T_0O} : T_0O \to K_0$ ⟦Chapter3/FillerLinearAlgebra · fillerRestrict⟧ are Fredholm with index $\dim d.P_n - \dim d.Q$. Proof: $g(0) = \hat f(0) = 0$ (filler condition (1) at $0$), so $k$ is an sc¹-germ on $G \cap G_s$ with the family $Dg - \iota Ds$ and $k(0) = 0$ ⟦Chapter3/FredholmGermLinearizationG · IsSc1GermWith.sub_toBase_shift⟧, and $Dk(0)$ is Fredholm of index $\dim d.P_n - \dim d.Q$ by the preceding theorem. Since $0$ is a smooth point, $Ds(0) : E \to F^1$ is an sc-operator (the remark after Proposition 1.2.1 (preprint 1.16), for germs) ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermLinearization · IsSc1GermWith.isScOperator_deriv_zero⟧, so $\iota Ds(0)$ is the level-$0$ map of an sc⁺-operator and compact. $Dg(0) = Dk(0) + \iota Ds(0)$ is then the value at $0$ of the identity $Dg = (Dg - \iota Ds) + \iota Ds$ ⟦Chapter3/FredholmGermLinearizationG · IsSc1GermWith.deriv_zero_eq_add_scPlus⟧; on $U$ it was the uniqueness of derivatives within $U_1$ and the density of $E_1$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermLinearization · ContinuousLinearMap.ext_of_comp_toBase_one⟧. A compact perturbation of a Fredholm operator is Fredholm of the same index ⟦Chapter1/FredholmPerturbation · isFredholm_add_isCompactOperator⟧ ⟦Chapter1/FredholmIndex · fredholmIndex_add_isCompactOperator⟧. Proposition 3.1.5 (preprint 3.5) (§III.4) transfers the Fredholm property and the index to $Tf(0)$ ⟦Chapter3/GermFillerLinearization · IsFillingGerm.proposition_3_1_5⟧.

**Proposition 3.6.5 (preprint 3.52), with the index** ⟦Chapter3/FredholmGermIndexG · proposition_3_6_5_index⟧. Let $f$ be a section of the strong bundle $P : Y \to X$ with $(f, x_0)$ an sc-Fredholm germ and $f(x_0) = 0$. In the strong bundle chart of Definition 3.1.11 (preprint 3.11) centred at $x_0$, with the filling germ $g$ of $\hat f$, its germ $G$ and a derivative family $Dg$, and the local sc⁺-germ $s$, conjugation data of $g - s$ exist, $Dg(0)$ and $Tf(x_0)$ are Fredholm, and **for every** conjugation data $d$ of $g - s$ their index is $\dim d.P_n - \dim d.Q$. Proof: unpack Definition 3.1.11 (preprint 3.11); $0 \in O$ because the chart is centred at $x_0$; $\hat f(0) = 0$ from $f(x_0) = 0$; $(x, \hat f(x)) \in K$ because the principal part of a section lies in the retract $K$ of the chart; data $d_0$ exist by the equivalence above and give the Fredholm property; and the local theorem applied to an arbitrary $d$ gives the index. No hypothesis is added. As in §III.21, only the level-$0$ Fredholm property is proved ($T_0O$ and $K_0$ are not set up as sc-Banach spaces). The statements of §III.21 stand next to these, with the same proofs and without the dimensions; the whole-chart forms are kept as well ⟦Chapter3/FredholmGermIndex · proposition_3_6_5_index_wholeChart⟧.

### III.31 The linearization at a zero in charts, its chart independence, and Definition 3.6.6 (preprint 3.53) from a non-centred chart

Definition 3.6.6 (preprint 3.53) is stated in $T_xX$, with the linearization $f'(x) : T_xX \to Y_x$ and the partial cone $C_xX$ of Definition 2.5.8 (preprint 2.38). The text defines $f'(x)$ only at the *centre* of a chart, before Definition 3.1.4 (preprint 3.4), as the restriction of $D(\mathbf f \circ r)(0)$ to $T_0O$, identifies it with $Dg(0)|_{T_0O}$ for a filler $g$, and appeals to Proposition 2.4.10 (preprint 2.30) for the independence of $T^R_xX$ and $C_xX$ of the chart. That $f'(x)$ itself, and the two conditions of Definition 3.6.6 (preprint 3.53), do not depend on the chart is used — the proof of Theorem 3.6.8 (preprint 3.54) repeats "as above" the argument at $x_0$ in a chart centred at a nearby zero $y$ — but not proved (Finding 110). The formalization (§III.21) states Definition 3.6.6 (preprint 3.53) in the chart of Definition 3.1.11 (preprint 3.11) centred at the zero; this section proves the local form of the missing independence at a zero, in charts, and the assembly step that Theorem 3.6.8 (1) (preprint 3.54) needs: Definition 3.6.6 (preprint 3.53) at a zero $y$ from data in a chart which is *not* centred at $y$ (the chart at $x_0$). Throughout, $z$ is a point on level $1$ (the text has smooth points), and tameness is not used.

**Linear algebra of conjugate operators.** Let $L : T \to K$ and $L' : T' \to K'$ be bounded operators between normed spaces and $A : T \cong T'$, $\Gamma : K \cong K'$ topological linear isomorphisms with $L'A = \Gamma L$. Then $L' = \Gamma L A^{-1}$ ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · eq_comp_of_conj⟧, so $L$ is onto iff $L'$ is ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · surjective_iff_of_conj⟧; $\ker L' = A(\ker L)$ ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · ker_eq_map_of_conj⟧; for complete spaces $L$ is Fredholm iff $L'$ is, with the same index, because Fredholm operators and their index are stable under composition with isomorphisms on both sides ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · isFredholm_iff_of_conj⟧ ⟦Chapter3/FredholmGermLinearization · isFredholm_equiv_comp_equiv⟧; and if $D \subseteq K$ is dense and contained in the range of $L$, then $\Gamma(D)$ is dense in $K'$ and contained in the range of $L'$ ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · dense_image_of_conj⟧. The isomorphisms that occur are restrictions of bounded operators $a : E_0 \to E'_0$, $a' : E'_0 \to E_0$ which map a subspace $T$ into $T'$ and $T'$ into $T$ and are inverse to each other there ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · restrictEquiv⟧. A subspace $N \subseteq E_0$ is transported by $A : T \cong T'$ to $A(N) = \{Ax : x \in T \cap N\} \subseteq T'$ ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · transportSub⟧, with $y \in A(N) \iff A^{-1}y \in N$ for $y \in T'$ ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · mem_transportSub_iff⟧ and $A^{-1}(A(N)) = N$ for $N \subseteq T$ ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · transportSub_symm⟧.

**Good position inside a subspace is transported** ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · IsInGoodPositionIn.transport⟧. Good position inside $T$ (§III.21, ⟦Chapter3/GoodPositionGerm · IsInGoodPositionIn⟧) asks: $N \subseteq T$, the interior of $N \cap C$ in $N$ nonempty, and a complement $P \subseteq T$ of $N$ *in* $T$ with the $\varepsilon$-condition of Definition 3.1.24 (preprint 3.21). If $A : T \cong T'$ respects the cones ($x \in C \iff Ax \in C'$ for $x \in T$) and $N$ is in good position to $C$ inside $T$, then $A(N)$ is in good position to $C'$ inside $T'$. *The interior*: $A$ restricts to a homeomorphism $N \cong A(N)$ which carries $N \cap C$ onto $A(N) \cap C'$, so it carries interior points to interior points. *The complement*: $A(P)$; $A(N) \cap A(P) = 0$ and $A(N) + A(P) = T'$ by pulling back with $A^{-1}$. *The constant*: with $a = \|A\|$, $b = \|A^{-1}\|$, take $\varepsilon' = \varepsilon/(ab + 1)$; for $y = Ax$, $q = Ap$ with $|q| \le \varepsilon'|y|$, $|p| \le b|q| \le b\varepsilon' a|x| \le \varepsilon|x|$, so $x \in C \iff x + p \in C$, i.e. $y \in C' \iff y + q \in C'$ — the constant of Lemma 3.5.9 (preprint 3.45), no finite dimensionality needed (Finding 115). Applied to $A$ and $A^{-1}$ this gives an equivalence ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · isInGoodPositionIn_transport_iff⟧.

**Two derivative tools at a level-$1$ point.** (i) *Uniqueness*: if $f_1 = f_2$ on a relatively open $V \subseteq U_1 \cap U_2$ containing $z$, then $Df_1(z) = Df_2(z)$: both are derivatives within $V_1$ (level $1$), $V$ is an sc-domain with unique differentiability, and two operators $E_0 \to F_0$ that agree after composition with the dense inclusion $E_1 \to E_0$ are equal ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · scDeriv_eq_of_eqOn_relOpen⟧. (ii) *The fibre derivative at a point of the zero section* ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · scDeriv_fiber_of_vanishing⟧: let $\Phi$ be sc¹ on a set $D \subseteq E \oplus F$ containing $\{(u, 0) : u \in S\}$ ($S$ relatively open, $z \in S_1$) and the whole fibre $\{z\} \times F$, with $\Phi(u, 0) = 0$ on $S$ and $\Phi(z, k) = \Gamma k$ for a bounded $\Gamma$. Then $D\Phi(z, 0)(h, k) = \Gamma k$. Proof: along $k_1 \mapsto (z, k_1)$, $k_1 \in F_1$, the composite is $\Gamma \circ \iota$, so $D\Phi(z, 0)$ restricted to the fibre direction is $\Gamma$ after composition with the inclusion, hence equal to $\Gamma$ by density; along $u_1 \mapsto (u_1, 0)$, $u_1 \in S_1$, the composite vanishes, and unique differentiability of $S_1$ at $z$ gives zero in the base direction. This is the generalization from $0$ to $z$ of the identities of §III.4 and §III.21; it is the reason why only the fibre part survives at a zero (Finding 112).

**The linearization at a point of the retract, without a filler.** For the principal part $\mathbf f$ of a section in a strong bundle chart with retraction $R(u, h) = (r(u), \rho(u)h)$ and a level-$1$ point $z$ of $O = r(U)$, put
$$\mathrm{Lin}(z) = D(\mathbf f \circ r)(z)|_{T_zO}, \qquad T_zO = Dr(z)E_0$$
⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · linAt⟧; $Dr(z)$ is idempotent ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · scDeriv_r_idem_at⟧. Three facts make it the right object.

*Its values lie in $K_z = \rho(z)F$ at a zero* ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · rho_scDeriv_comp_r⟧ (the text's "$\rho(0)Tf(0) = Tf(0)$", now at any zero). Since $(r(u), \mathbf f(r(u))) \in K$, $\rho(r(u))\mathbf f(r(u)) = \mathbf f(r(u))$ on $U$ ⟦Chapter3/Filler · ρ_eq_self_of_mem_K⟧; so $\mathbf f \circ r$ equals $\Psi_2(u) = (R(r(u), \mathbf f(r(u))))_2$, and their derivatives at $z$ agree by (i). The chain rule for $\Psi_2 = (\text{fibre part of } R) \circ (r, \mathbf f \circ r)$ at $z$, where $(r, \mathbf f \circ r)$ passes through $(z, 0)$ (because $r(z) = z$ and $\mathbf f(z) = 0$), and (ii) for the fibre map $(u, k) \mapsto \rho(u)k$, which vanishes on the zero section and is $\rho(z)$ on the fibre over $z$, give $D(\mathbf f \circ r)(z)v = \rho(z)D(\mathbf f \circ r)(z)v$. The fibre $K_z$ is the fibre of $K$ over $z$ ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · mem_rangeS_ρCLM_iff⟧.

*The bridge to fillers* ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · scDeriv_comp_r_eq_of_eventuallyEq⟧ (the text's "$Tg(0) = Tf(0)$ on $T_0O$", now at any point): if $g$ is sc¹ and agrees with $\mathbf f$ on $O$ near $z$, say on $O \cap W$ with $W$ open, then $\mathbf f \circ r = g \circ r$ on the relatively open $U \cap r^{-1}(W)$, so by (i) and the chain rule ($r$ maps $z$ to itself on level $1$) $D(\mathbf f \circ r)(z) = Dg(z)Dr(z)$. Hence $\mathrm{Lin}(z) = Dg(z)$ on $T_zO$ ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · linAt_eq_scDeriv⟧, by idempotence of $Dr(z)$. At a centre, for a filled version, $\mathrm{Lin}(0)$ is the $Tf(0) = Dg(0)|_{T_0O}$ of Proposition 3.1.5 (preprint 3.5) ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · IsFilledVersion.fillerRestrict_eq_linAt⟧ ⟦Chapter3/FillerLinearAlgebra · fillerRestrict⟧. At $z \ne 0$ the full $Dg(z) : E \to F$ is not related to $\mathrm{Lin}(z)$ (condition (3) of the filler holds at $0$ only), so the relevant kernel is $\ker Dg(z) \cap T_zO = \ker \mathrm{Lin}(z)$ (Finding 111). *For a filling germ* $g$ on $G$ the bridge needs two things the whole-chart one did not: $Dg(z)$ exists only for $z$ on level $1$ of $G$, and the chain rule for $g \circ r$ is the inner one on the germ $r^{-1}(G)$, which is defined when $r(0) = 0$, i.e. $0 \in O$ ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationChartsG · scDeriv_comp_r_eq_germ⟧. Under these two conditions $\mathrm{Lin}(z) = Dg(z)$ on $T_zO$ ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationChartsG · linAt_eq_germDeriv⟧ ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationChartsG · StrongBundle.linAt_eq_germDeriv_chart⟧, and at the centre, which lies in every germ, $\mathrm{Lin}(0) = Tf(0)$ ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationChartsG · IsFillingGerm.fillerRestrict_eq_linAt⟧. Both hold wherever the bridge is used: the chart is centred at a zero, and the points $z \ne 0$ are the level-$1$ lifts of nearby zeros, which lie in the germ of $g$ by the shrinking of the basic germ (§§III.32, III.48).

**Fibres of strong bundle charts and of transitions.** For a strong bundle chart $\Phi : P^{-1}(V) \to K$ covering $\varphi$: points of $K$ lie over $O$ ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · StrongBundleChart.fst_mem_O⟧; $\Phi^{-1}(x, \cdot)$ is additive and homogeneous on the fibre of $K$ over $x$, since $\Phi$ is a fibrewise linear bijection of $P^{-1}(\varphi^{-1}(x))$ onto that fibre ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · StrongBundleChart.symm_add⟧ ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · StrongBundleChart.symm_smul⟧; the fibre of $K$ is closed under scalars and contains $(u, \rho(u)k)$ ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · StrongBundleChart.mem_K_ρ⟧. For a second chart $\Phi'$ the transition $\Theta = \Phi' \circ \Phi^{-1}$ covers the base transition $\tau = \varphi' \circ \varphi^{-1}$ ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · StrongBundleChart.transition_fst⟧, is linear on the fibres ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · StrongBundleChart.transition_snd_add⟧ ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · StrongBundleChart.transition_snd_smul⟧, maps the zero section to the zero section ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · StrongBundleChart.transition_snd_zero⟧ and $K$ into $K'$ ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · StrongBundleChart.transition_mem_K⟧, and is inverted by the reverse transition ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · StrongBundleChart.transition_transition⟧. Over a point $x$ of the base overlap, $\Gamma(x)k = (\Theta(x, \rho(x)k))_2$ is a bounded operator $F \to F'$ — linear by the fibre linearity, continuous as a composition of $\rho(x)$, $\Phi^{-1}$ and $\Phi'$ ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · StrongBundleChart.transitionFiberCLM⟧ — which on $K_x$ is the transition itself ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · StrongBundleChart.transitionFiberCLM_apply_of_mem⟧. The text's "bounded linear on the fibres" (Definition 2.6.4 (preprint 2.50)) is about $\Phi$ only; the linearity of $\Phi^{-1}$ on the fibres of $K$ is this lemma (Finding 113). Finally, if $\varphi(V \cap V') = O \cap W$ with $W$ open, the overlap of $K$ is $K \cap (W \times F)$ ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · StrongBundleChart.overlap_eq_inter_prod⟧.

**The chart change at a zero** ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · linAt_chart_change⟧. *Statement.* Let $c$, $c'$ be strong bundle charts whose transition $\Theta$ is sc-smooth on $[0]$ (as a map between the overlap retracts of $K$ and $K'$), $f$ a section whose representatives $z \mapsto (z, \mathbf f(z))$ are sc-smooth $O \to K$ and $O' \to K'$, $y$ a zero of $f$ in both chart domains, and $z$, $z'$ level-$1$ points over $\varphi(y)$, $\varphi'(y)$. Then there are topological isomorphisms $A : T_zO \cong T_{z'}O'$ and $\Gamma : K_z \cong K'_{z'}$ with $\Gamma \circ \mathrm{Lin}(z) = \mathrm{Lin}'(z') \circ A$, and $x \in C_z \iff Ax \in C'_{z'}$ for $x \in T_zO$, where $C_z$ is the partial quadrant at $\varphi(y)$ of a presentation of $C$ ⟦Chapter2/Tame · partialQuadrantAtWith⟧. The proof, in the order of the Lean:

1. *The base transition as a germ of sc-diffeomorphisms.* The base charts are compatible (both are charts of $X$, ⟦Chapter2/MPolyfold · IsChart.compatible⟧). Choose open $W$, $W'$ with $\varphi(V \cap V') = O \cap W$ and $\varphi'(V \cap V') = O' \cap W'$ ⟦Chapter2/MPolyfold · exists_isOpen_image_eq⟧ and restrict the base retractions to $U \cap r^{-1}(W)$, $U' \cap r'^{-1}(W')$ (Proposition 2.3.1 (1) (preprint 2.10)); their retracts are the overlaps. Then $\tau$, $\tau' = \varphi \circ \varphi'^{-1}$ form a germ of sc-diffeomorphisms between them ⟦Chapter2/Boundary · ScRetraction.IsDiffeoGerm⟧, and $\tau \circ r_W$ maps $z$ to $z'$ on level $1$. The derivatives of the restricted retraction and of $\mathbf f \circ r$ at $z$ are those on the full domains, by (i) (Finding 114).
2. *The transition near the zero section.* Restrict the strong bundle retraction to $W \times F$; its retract is the overlap of $K$, so $\Theta \circ R_W$ is sc-smooth and its fibre part $\Phi$ is sc¹. $\Phi(z, k) = \Gamma(z)k$ because $R(z, k) = (z, \rho(z)k)$; $\Phi(u, 0) = 0$ because $R(u, 0) = (r(u), 0)$ and $\Theta$ preserves the zero section.
3. *The section in the two charts.* For $u$ in the restricted domain, $r(u) = \varphi(y_1)$ for some $y_1 \in V \cap V'$, and both $\mathbf f' \circ r' \circ \tau \circ r_W$ and $\Phi \circ (r, \mathbf f \circ r)$ take at $u$ the value $(\Phi'(f(y_1)))_2$ (the principal parts are the fibre parts of $\Phi \circ f \circ \varphi^{-1}$ and $\Phi' \circ f \circ \varphi'^{-1}$, ⟦Chapter3/StrongBundleSections · StrongBundleChart.apply_section'⟧). By (i) their derivatives at $z$ agree.
4. *The key identity.* The chain rule on both sides, with $(r, \mathbf f \circ r)$ passing through $(z, 0)$ since $\mathbf f(z) = 0$, and (ii) for $\Phi$ give
$$D(\mathbf f' \circ r')(z')\,D(\tau \circ r_W)(z)v = \Gamma(z)\,D(\mathbf f \circ r)(z)v .$$
The derivative of $x \mapsto \Gamma(x)\mathbf f(z)$ in the base direction has dropped out *because $\mathbf f(z) = 0$*; away from zeros the linearization is not chart independent (Finding 112).
5. *The isomorphism $A$.* $A_0 = D(\tau \circ r_W)(z)$ maps $T_zO$ into $T_{z'}O'$ and $A_0' = D(\tau' \circ r'_{W'})(z')$ maps back ⟦Chapter2/Boundary · ScRetraction.IsDiffeoGerm.deriv_mem_range⟧; on these subspaces they are inverse ⟦Chapter2/Boundary · ScRetraction.IsDiffeoGerm.deriv_inv_deriv⟧ (with the idempotence of $Dr(z)$); $A$ is the induced isomorphism (`restrictEquiv`).
6. *The isomorphism $\Gamma$.* $\Gamma(z)$ maps $K_z$ into $K'_{z'}$ (the transition maps $K$ into $K'$ and covers $\tau$), the fibre operator $\Gamma'(z')$ of the reverse transition maps back, and they are inverse on these fibres (the reverse transition inverts the transition).
7. *Conclusions.* For $x \in T_zO$, $\mathrm{Lin}(z)x = D(\mathbf f \circ r)(z)x$ and $\mathrm{Lin}'(z')(Ax) = D(\mathbf f' \circ r')(z')A_0x$, so step 4 is $\Gamma\,\mathrm{Lin}(z) = \mathrm{Lin}'(z')A$. The cones: Proposition 2.4.10 (preprint 2.30) for the partial cone, applied to the germ of step 1 ⟦Chapter2/Boundary · proposition_2_4_10_cone⟧, gives $A_0(T_zO \cap C_z) = T_{z'}O' \cap C'_{z'}$ for the formalized partial cone ⟦Chapter2/Tame · partialConeWith⟧, which is the cone condition on $T_zO$.

*Hypotheses.* Of the compatibility of Definition 2.6.4 (preprint 2.50) only the sc-smoothness of $\Theta$ on $[0]$ is used — not on $[1]$, not the reverse transition (Finding 116). For two strong bundle charts of $P$ it holds because both are charts of the M-polyfold $Y[0]$, hence compatible ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · StrongBundle.IsChart.transition_isScSmoothRetractMap⟧; and for an sc-smooth section the representatives are sc-smooth in every strong bundle chart of $P$, so no hypothesis is added.

**Chart independence** ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · StrongBundle.linAt_chart_independence⟧. For two strong bundle charts of $P$ around a zero $y$ of an sc-smooth section, with level-$1$ points $z$, $z'$ over $\varphi(y)$, $\varphi'(y)$: the linearizations $L = \mathrm{Lin}(z) : T_zO \to K_z$ and $L' = \mathrm{Lin}'(z') : T_{z'}O' \to K'_{z'}$ (corestricted to the fibres by the first fact above) are simultaneously surjective, simultaneously Fredholm with the same index, and $\ker L$ is in good position to $C_z$ inside $T_zO$ iff $\ker L'$ is in good position to $C'_{z'}$ inside $T_{z'}O'$. The four spaces are complete, being ranges of the idempotents $Dr(z)$, $\rho(z)$ and their primed versions ⟦Chapter3/FillerLinearAlgebra · isClosed_range_of_idem⟧; the rest is the conjugation algebra, with $\ker L' = A(\ker L)$ and the transport of good position.

**Definition 3.6.6 (preprint 3.53) at a zero, from a non-centred chart** ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · StrongBundle.isGoodPositionGerm_of_chart_dense⟧. *Statement.* Let $f$ be an sc-smooth section, $y$ a zero with $(f, y)$ an sc-Fredholm germ (Definition 3.1.11 (preprint 3.11)), $c$ a strong bundle chart of $P$ around $y$ (not necessarily centred at $y$), $z$ on level $1$ over $\varphi(y)$, and $g$ sc¹ on $U$ agreeing with $\mathbf f$ on $O$ near $\varphi(y)$ — the case in view was the chart at $x_0$ with a whole-chart filled version. Assume (a) the range of $Dg(z)|_{T_zO}$ contains $\rho(z)\hat k$ for every smooth $\hat k \in F_\infty$, and (b) $\ker Dg(z) \cap T_zO$ is in good position to $C_{\varphi(y)}$ inside $T_zO$. Then $(f, y)$ is in good position in the whole-chart sense ⟦Chapter3/GoodPositionGerm · StrongBundle.IsGoodPositionGermWholeChart⟧, in the chart $c'$ centred at $y$ given by Definition 3.1.11 (preprint 3.11), with its filled version $g'$. *Proof.* The chart change between $c$ at $z$ and $c'$ at $0$ gives $A$, $\Gamma$ with $\Gamma\,\mathrm{Lin}(z) = \mathrm{Lin}'(0)A$; the bridges give $\mathrm{Lin}(z) = Dg(z)$ on $T_zO$ and $\mathrm{Lin}'(0) = Dg'(0)$ on $T_0O'$ (filler condition (1)). *Surjectivity of $Tf(y) = Dg'(0)|_{T_0O'}$ onto $K'_0$.* By Proposition 3.6.5 (preprint 3.52) in the centred chart $c'$ ⟦Chapter3/FredholmGermLinearizationG · proposition_3_6_5_local⟧ it is Fredholm, so its range $S$ is closed in the closed subspace $K'_0$, hence closed in $F'$. The map $k \mapsto \Gamma(\rho(z)k)$ is continuous $F \to F'$; by (a) and the conjugation it maps $F_\infty$ into $S$; it maps $F$ onto $K'_0$ (preimages by $\Gamma^{-1}$, which lands in $K_z$ where $\rho(z)$ is the identity); since $F_\infty$ is dense in $F$, every point of $K'_0$ is a limit of points of $S$, hence in $S$. So only a dense subset of $K_z$ in the range is needed (Finding 117 (b)). *The kernel.* (b) is transported by $A$ to good position inside $T_0O'$ with respect to $C'_0 = C'$ (the partial quadrant at the corner is $C'$ itself ⟦Chapter3/GoodPositionGerm · partialQuadrantAtWith_zero⟧); and the transported subspace is $\ker Dg'(0)$: $\ker Dg'(0) \subseteq T_0O'$ by condition (3) of the filler $g'$ *at its centre* ⟦Chapter3/GoodPositionGerm · IsFillerOf.ker_deriv_le_range⟧, and for $w \in T_0O'$, $Dg'(0)w = 0 \iff \Gamma(Dg(z)A^{-1}w) = 0 \iff A^{-1}w \in \ker Dg(z)$. This is where the hypothesis is on $\ker Dg(z) \cap T_zO$ and not on $\ker Dg(z)$: at $z \ne 0$ condition (3) is not available and $\ker Dg(z)$ need not lie in $T_zO$ (Finding 117 (a)). Variants: with all of $K_z$ in the range ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationChartsG · StrongBundle.isGoodPositionGerm_of_chart⟧, and with the hypotheses on $\mathrm{Lin}(z)$ itself, taking $g = \mathbf f \circ r$, which agrees with $\mathbf f$ on $O$ because $r$ is the identity there ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · StrongBundle.isGoodPositionGerm_of_linAt⟧ ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationChartsG · StrongBundle.isGoodPositionGerm_of_linAt_surj⟧.

*In the germ reading* the assembly is the same, with $g$ a germ with derivative family $Dg$, $z$ on level $1$ of its germ, and the bridge for germs at $z$ and at $0$; the conclusion is good position at $Dg'(0)$ for the filling germ $g'$ at $y$. The dense form needs the closed range of $Dg'(0)|_{T_0O'}$, which is now a hypothesis at chart level ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationChartsG · StrongBundle.isGoodPositionAtGerm_of_chart_dense⟧, supplied at section level by Proposition 3.6.5 (preprint 3.52) for germs in a chart centred at $y$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbySurjG · StrongBundle.isGoodPositionGermTame_of_chart_dense⟧. The forms with all of $K_z$ in the range need no Proposition 3.6.5 (preprint 3.52) at all: $\mathrm{Lin}'(0) = \Gamma\,\mathrm{Lin}(z)A^{-1}$ is then onto $K'_0$ directly ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationChartsG · StrongBundle.isGoodPositionAtGerm_of_chart⟧ (the whole-chart variant had gone through the dense one).

What remains is to supply (a) and (b) in the chart at $x_0$, at the zeros $z_0 = \varphi(y)$ near $0$: (b) with the text's hypotheses (§III.32), (a) first under an added hypothesis (§III.33) and then with the text's hypotheses (§III.43).

### III.32 Theorem 3.6.8 (1) (preprint 3.54) at the nearby zeros: Corollary 3.5.3 (preprint 3.41) and Lemma 3.6.9 (preprint 3.55) on the graph, and the kernel

The text proves property (4) of Theorem 3.6.8 (preprint 3.54) — at every zero $y \in V$, with $\psi\varphi(y) = v + \sigma(v)$, "$\ker f'(y)$ is in good position to $C_yX$ and $f'(y)$ is surjective" — in one sentence: it follows from Corollary 3.5.3 (preprint 3.41) and Lemma 3.6.9 (preprint 3.55). Unpacked, three steps are needed beyond the graph description of §III.23. First, Corollary 3.5.3 (preprint 3.41) and Lemma 3.6.9 (preprint 3.55) have to be applied at the graph points, Lemma 3.6.9 (preprint 3.55) on the relatively open $V'$ of Theorem 3.5.11 (preprint Corollary 3.47) and for a general presentation of $C'$ (Finding 92). Second, Lemma 3.6.9 (preprint 3.55) is about the tangent space $N_v = \{n + D\sigma(v)n\}$ of the graph, not about a kernel; the identification $N_v = \ker D(h + t)(v + \sigma(v))$ is not commented on (Finding 91 (a), 101). Third, the statements live in the push-forward chart $\psi \circ \varphi$, about $D(h + t)$ on $E'$, while Definition 3.6.6 (preprint 3.53) at $y$ (via §III.31) asks for statements about $Dg(z_0)$ on $T_{z_0}O$ in the chart at $x_0$ (Findings 102–104). This section proves all of it for the kernel; the surjectivity of $f'(y)$ is §III.33. No hypothesis beyond those of the graph description is used.

**Lemma 3.6.9 (preprint 3.55) for a relatively open domain and a general presentation** ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTFull · lemma_3_6_9_relOpen⟧. *Statement.* Let $C = \{x : \ell_i(x) \ge 0\}$ be a partial quadrant presented by an sc-isomorphism $T$, with the coordinate functionals $\ell_i(x) = (Tx)_i$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTFull · coordCLMWith⟧, $N$ finite-dimensional in good position to $C$ with a good complement $Y$, **$Y$ closed**, $V$ relatively open in $N \cap C$ with $0 \in V$, and $\sigma : N \to E_0$ with values in $Y$, $C^1$ on $V$, $\sigma(0) = 0$, $D\sigma(0) = 0$ (within $V$). Then there is $\rho > 0$ such that for $v \in V$ with $|v| < \rho$ and $z = v + \sigma(v)$: $z \in C$; $Y$ is a good complement of $N_v = \{n + D\sigma(v)n\}$ ⟦Chapter3/GoodPositionOpen · graphSpace⟧ with respect to the partial quadrant $C_z$; and $N_v$ is in good position to $C_z$. *Proof.* $D = N \cap C$ is convex with nonempty interior in $N$ (good position), so $D$ and $V = D \cap O$ have unique differentiability. *The extension.* The text's Lemma 3.6.9 (preprint 3.55) (§III.22, ⟦Chapter3/GoodPositionOpen · lemma_3_6_9_core⟧) wants a map on the whole cone. Take a ball $B(0, r) \subseteq O$ and a smooth bump $b$ equal to $1$ on $\bar B(0, r/2)$ and $0$ outside $B(0, r)$, and $\Phi(v) = b(v)v$. $\Phi$ maps $D$ into $V$: $D$ is closed under nonnegative scaling, and $|b(v)v| < r$. So $\tau = \sigma \circ \Phi$ is $C^1$ on the cone, agrees with $\sigma$ on $B(0, r/2)$, and there its derivatives within the cone are those of $\sigma$ within $V$ (a neighbourhood of the point inside $O$ does not change derivatives within a set); in particular $\tau(0) = 0$, $D\tau(0) = 0$. *Lemma 3.6.9 (preprint 3.55) in coordinates* for $\tau$, with the good-complement estimate of $(N, Y)$ rewritten in the $\ell_i$ and the joint surjectivity of the coordinates ($T^{-1}(k, 0)$ has coordinates $k$), gives $\rho_1$, the membership $z \in C$, and the good complement $Y$ of $N_v$ with respect to $C_z$. *The interior*, which Lemma 3.6.9 (preprint 3.55) does not state but good position requires: by the continuity of $D\sigma$ at $0$ (a $C^1$ map on a set of unique differentiability) there is $\rho_2$ with $\|D\sigma(v)\| < \varepsilon$, the good-complement constant of $(N, Y)$. $L = D\sigma(v)$ takes values in $Y$ — this is where $Y$ closed is used ⟦Chapter3/GoodPositionOpen · fderivWithin_mem_of_mapsTo⟧ — so $n \mapsto n + Ln$ is injective ($N \cap Y = 0$), a linear homeomorphism $N \cong N_v$ in finite dimensions. It carries the interior of $D$ to an open subset of $N_v$ contained in $C_z$: for $w$ in the interior of $D$, $w \in C$ and $|Lw| \le \varepsilon|w|$, so $w + Lw \in C$ by the good-complement property, and $C \subseteq C_z$. Take $\rho = \min(\rho_1, r/2, \rho_2)$.

**Corollary 3.5.3 (preprint 3.41) at the graph points** ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTFull · StabilityData.exists_surjective_Dfs'_graph⟧. For the data of Theorem 3.4.2 (preprint 3.38) with $D(h + s)(0)$ surjective and a continuous $\sigma$ on $V \ni 0$, $\sigma(0) = 0$, whose graph points $v + \sigma(v)$ are zeros in the germ: there is $\rho > 0$ such that for $v \in V$, $|v| < \rho$, the zero $v + \sigma(v)$ lies on level $1$, at a point $x_1$ of the level-$1$ germ, and $D(h + s)(x_1) : E_0 \to F_0$ is a surjective Fredholm operator of index $\dim P - \dim Q$. Corollary 3.5.3 (preprint 3.41) ⟦Chapter3/SolutionLinearization · StabilityData.corollary_3_5_3⟧ gives a radius $r$ for the zeros, and continuity of $v \mapsto v + \sigma(v)$ at $0$ gives $\rho$.

**Property (4) in the push-forward chart** ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTFullG · theorem_3_6_8_nearby⟧. *Statement.* For a section $f$ of a strong bundle whose charts have tame base retractions, $f(x_0) = 0$ and $(f, x_0)$ in good position: the data of the graph description (§III.23, ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTG · theorem_3_6_8_graph⟧: chart, $\psi$, $h + t$, $K$, closed $Y$, $V'$, $\sigma$, $V_x$), a presentation $T$ of $C' = \widetilde C \oplus W$, and an open $V_{x,1} \subseteq V_x$ containing $x_0$ such that for every zero $y \in V_{x,1}$, with $\psi\varphi(y) = v + \sigma(v)$, $v \in V'$: the point lies on level $1$ and $D(h + t)$ there is surjective Fredholm of index $n - N$; $v + \sigma(v) \in C'$; $Y$ is a good complement of $N_v$ with respect to $C'_{v + \sigma(v)}$, and $N_v$ is in good position to it. Only $f$ a section is assumed, not an sc-Fredholm section. *Proof.* Take the radii $\rho_1$ (Corollary 3.5.3 (preprint 3.41) at the graph points) and $\rho_2$ (Lemma 3.6.9 (preprint 3.55), relatively open form), $\rho = \min(\rho_1, \rho_2)$, and the projection $\pi$ onto $K$ along $Y$, continuous because $Y$ is closed and $K$ finite-dimensional. Put $V_{x,1} = V_x \cap (\psi\varphi)^{-1}B(0, \rho/(\|\pi\| + 1))$, open since $\psi \circ \varphi$ is continuous on $V_x$. For a zero $y \in V_{x,1}$, $\psi\varphi(y) = v + \sigma(v)$ by the graph description, $v = \pi(\psi\varphi(y))$ because $\sigma(v) \in Y$, so $|v| \le \|\pi\|\,|\psi\varphi(y)| < \rho$, and both lemmas apply at $v$.

**Derivative identities at a nearby zero.** Four lemmas move these statements from $E'$ and $N_v$ to $E$ and $\ker Dg(z_0)$.

*Uniqueness at a point of $C_1$* ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyGraph · scDeriv_eq_of_eqOn_nhds_at⟧: two maps with sc¹-derivatives $D_1$, $D_2$ at $x \in C_1$ that agree on $W_1 \cap C_1$ for an open $W_1 \ni x$ of $E_1$ have $D_1 = D_2$ — uniqueness of the derivative within the quadrant on level $1$, then density of $E_1$. The text writes $\Psi_*(g) = h + t$ as an identity; formally they agree only on $U' \cap W'$, and this lemma identifies their derivatives at every nearby point (Finding 104).

*The fibre derivative of a strong bundle map at $(u_0, 0)$* ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyGraph · IsStrongBundleMap.scDeriv_fiber_at⟧: for a strong bundle map $\Psi$ with fibre operators $\Gamma(u) = (\Psi(u, \cdot))_2$ ⟦Chapter3/FredholmGermLinearization · IsStrongBundleMap.fiberCLM⟧ and $u_0 \in U_1$, $D(\mathrm{pr}_{F'} \circ \Psi)(u_0, 0)(h, k) = \Gamma(u_0)k$; hence the derivative of $u \mapsto \Gamma(u)k(u)$ at a zero $u_0$ of $k$ is $\Gamma(u_0)Dk(u_0)$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyGraph · IsStrongBundleMap.scDeriv_fiber_comp_at⟧ — §III.21's identity at $0$, moved to $u_0$.

*The chain rule for the push-forward at a zero* ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyGraph · IsStrongBundleIso.scDeriv_pushforward_at⟧: for a strong bundle isomorphism $\Psi$ covering $\psi$ with inverse $\Psi'$ covering $\psi'$, and a zero $u_0 \in U_1$ of the sc-smooth $g$, $\Psi_*(g) = \kappa \circ \psi'$ with $\kappa(u) = (\Psi(u, g(u)))_2$, so
$$D(\Psi_*g)(\psi(u_0)) = \Gamma(u_0) \circ Dg(u_0) \circ D\psi'(\psi(u_0)).$$
$D\psi'(\psi(u_0))$ and $D\psi(u_0)$ are inverse on level $0$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyGraph · IsStrongBundleIso.scDeriv_base_inv_base⟧, and $\Gamma(u_0)$ is injective because $\Psi'$ inverts $\Psi$ on the fibres ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyGraph · IsStrongBundleIso.fiberCLM_injective⟧; hence $\ker Dg(u_0) = D\psi'(\psi(u_0))(\ker D(\Psi_*g)(\psi(u_0)))$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyGraph · IsStrongBundleIso.ker_scDeriv_eq_map⟧. *For a germ* $g$ with derivative family $Dg$ the formula is an identity of the explicit derivative family of $\Psi_*(g)$ (§III.23), valid at every level-$1$ zero $u_0$ and for every family ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyGraphG · IsStrongBundleIso.pushforwardDeriv_at⟧, and the kernel formula follows as before ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyGraphG · IsStrongBundleIso.ker_deriv_eq_map_germ⟧; that $g$ is sc¹ at $u_0$ then means that $u_0$ lies on level $1$ of the germ of $g$.

*Corresponding partial quadrants* ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyGraph · scDeriv_mem_partialQuadrantAtWith_iff⟧: for an sc-diffeomorphism $\psi : U \to U'$ between relatively open subsets of partial quadrants with inverse $\psi'$ and $z \in U'_1$, $x \in C'_z \iff D\psi'(z)x \in C_{\psi'(z)}$: the one-sided sign argument ⟦Chapter2/Boundary · IsSc1With.mapsTo_partialQuadrant⟧ for $\psi'$ at $z$ gives one direction, for $\psi$ at $\psi'(z)$ the other, and $D\psi(\psi'(z))D\psi'(z) = 1$. At the centre this was "$C' = T\psi(0)C$" (§III.23).

*Tangent vectors of a family of points of a retract* ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyGraph · scDeriv_apply_toBase_deriv_eq_of_fixed⟧: let $\rho$ be sc¹ on $U$ and $\Theta_1 : N \to E_1$ differentiable at $v$ within $V$ (unique differentiability at $v$), with values in $U_1$ and $\rho(\iota\Theta_1(w)) = \iota\Theta_1(w)$ for $w \in V$. Then $D\rho(\Theta_1(v))$ fixes $\iota(D\Theta_1(v)n)$ for every $n$: differentiate $\rho \circ \iota \circ \Theta_1 = \iota \circ \Theta_1$ at $v$. For $\rho$ a retraction onto $O$: the tangent vectors of a $C^1$ family of points of $O$ lie in $T_{z_0}O$.

**$N_v = \ker D(h + s)(v + \sigma(v))$** (Finding 101). *The inclusion* ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyGraph · StabilityData.graphSpace_le_ker_Dfs'⟧. For the solution graph $\sigma$ of Theorem 3.5.11 (preprint Corollary 3.47) on $V$, a $C^1$-lift $\sigma_1 : V_1 \to E_1$ of $\sigma$ on a relatively open $V_1 \subseteq V$ (Theorem 3.5.11 (1) (preprint Corollary 3.47), §III.20), $v \in V_1$ and the level-$1$ point $x_1$ over $v + \sigma(v)$ in the germ: $N_v \subseteq \ker D(h + s)(x_1)$. *Proof.* $h + s$ is differentiable on level $1$ only, so the graph is lifted: the kernel $K$ is a smooth subspace ⟦Chapter3/SolutionGermRegularity · StabilityData.ker_Dfs_le_smooth⟧, hence lifts linearly to $E_1$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicClass · finLift⟧, and $\Gamma_1 = \mathrm{lift} + \sigma_1$ is a $C^1$ map $V_1 \to E_1$ with $\iota\Gamma_1(w) = w + \sigma(w)$ and $\Gamma_1(v) = x_1$. $V_1 = V \cap O_1$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyGraph · relOpen_cone_inter⟧ has unique differentiability at its points ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyGraph · uniqueDiffWithinAt_relOpen_cone⟧, and $D\sigma(v) = \iota \circ D\sigma_1(v)$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyGraph · fderivWithin_eq_toBase_comp⟧. The composite $(h + s) \circ \iota \circ \Gamma_1$ vanishes on $V_1$, and near $v$ (on $V_1 \cap \Gamma_1^{-1}$ of the open set defining the level-$1$ germ) $\Gamma_1$ maps into the level-$1$ germ, where $h + s$ has the derivative $D(h + s)(x_1)$ composed with $\iota$ ⟦Chapter3/SolutionLinearization · StabilityData.isSc1GermWith_Dfs'⟧. The chain rule and uniqueness give $D(h + s)(x_1)(n + D\sigma(v)n) = 0$ for $n \in K$. *Equality* ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyGraph · StabilityData.graphSpace_eq_ker_Dfs'⟧ under the conclusions of Corollary 3.5.3 (preprint 3.41) at $x_1$ (Fredholm, index $\dim P - \dim Q$, surjective) and with the closed good complement $Y$: $D\sigma(v)$ takes values in $Y$, so $\dim N_v = \dim K$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyGraph · finrank_graphSpace_of_compl⟧; for a surjective Fredholm operator $\dim\ker = \mathrm{ind}$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFT · finrank_ker_eq_fredholmIndex_of_surjective⟧, so $\dim K = \mathrm{ind}\,D(h + s)(0) = \dim P - \dim Q = \mathrm{ind}\,D(h + s)(x_1) = \dim\ker D(h + s)(x_1)$, and an inclusion of finite-dimensional spaces of the same dimension is an equality.

**Good position restricts to a subspace containing the kernel** ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyGraph · IsInGoodPosition.isInGoodPositionIn_of_le⟧: if $N$ is in good position to $C$ in $E_0$ and $N \subseteq T$, a good complement $P$ restricts to the good complement $P \cap T$ of $N$ in $T$, with the same constant ($n + p \in T$ and $n \in T$ give $p \in T$).

**The graph description with the filler data exposed** ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyGraphG · theorem_3_6_8_chart_graph⟧. This is the body of the graph description (§III.23) stated in the chart, with the filling germ $g$ of $\mathbf f$ on $G_g$ and a derivative family $Dg$, the sc⁺-germ $t_0$ on $G_s$, the conjugation $\Psi$ (base $\psi$, inverse $\psi'$), the basic germ on a germ $G$ that $\psi'$ maps into $G_g \cap G_s$ level by level ($G \subseteq G''$, §III.48), and the tameness consequence $(1 - Dr(0))E \subseteq E_0$ as hypotheses, so that the later steps can use $g$ at the points $z_0$. Its conclusions: the data $\mathcal S$ of Theorem 3.4.2 (preprint 3.38) on $G$ itself and the basic germ $h' = h + (t_{\mathbb R^N}, 0)$ with $\mathcal S.\mathrm{fsum}\,h' = \Psi_*(g)$ on $U' \cap W'$; $D(h + t)(0)$ surjective with kernel $K$ in good position; $Y$ (closed), $V'$, $\sigma$ with its $C^k$-lifts; an open $N_1 \ni 0$ on which the filler identities (1), (2) and the conjugation identity hold, with $\psi'(U' \cap W') \subseteq N_1$; and an open $N_2 \subseteq N_1$ with $\psi(U \cap N_2)$ inside the germ and $U' \cap W'$, every zero of $g$ in $U \cap N_2$ in $O$, $g(u) = 0 \iff (h + t)(\psi(u)) = 0$ on $O \cap N_2$, and $\psi(\{u \in O \cap N_2 : g(u) = 0\}) = \{v + \sigma(v) : v \in V'\}$. The proof is that of §III.23 (items 1–7), in the chart; $D(h + t)(0) = D(\Psi_*g)(0)$ for the explicit family is part of the conclusion.

**The kernel at the nearby zeros, in the chart at $x_0$** ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyKernelG · theorem_3_6_8_chart_kernel⟧. *Statement.* In the data of the preceding theorem there is an open $N_3 \subseteq N_2$ containing $0$ such that for every zero $z_0 \in O \cap N_3$ of $g$ there are $v \in V'$ with $\psi(z_0) = v + \sigma(v)$, the level-$1$ point $z_{0,1}$ over $z_0$, **on level $1$ of the germ $G_g$ of the filling**, and $x_1 = \psi_1(z_{0,1})$ in the level-$1$ germ over $v + \sigma(v)$, with ($Dg(z_{0,1})$ the value of the derivative family): (i) $D(h + t)(x_1)$ surjective Fredholm of index $n - N$; (a) $N_v = \ker D(h + t)(x_1)$; (b) $D(\Psi_*g)(x_1) = D(h + t)(x_1)$ and $\ker Dg(z_{0,1}) = D\psi'(x_1)(\ker D(h + t)(x_1))$, and $Dg(z_{0,1})$ is surjective; (c) $\ker Dg(z_{0,1}) \subseteq T_{z_0}O = Dr(z_{0,1})E$; (d) $\ker Dg(z_{0,1})$ is in good position to $C_{z_0}$ inside $T_{z_0}O$. *Proof.*

1. *The neighbourhood.* Take the $C^1$-lift $\sigma_1$ on $V_1 = V' \cap O_1$ (Theorem 3.5.11 (1) (preprint Corollary 3.47) with $k = 1$), the radii $\rho_1$ (Corollary 3.5.3 (preprint 3.41) at the graph points), $\rho_2$ (Lemma 3.6.9 (preprint 3.55) relatively open, for the presentation of $C' = \widetilde C \oplus W$), $\rho_3$ with $B(0, \rho_3) \subseteq O_1$, and $\rho = \min(\rho_1, \rho_2, \rho_3)$. With the projection $\pi$ onto $K$ along $Y$, let $N_3 = N_2 \cap O_3$ where $O_3 \cap U = \psi^{-1}(B(0, \rho/(\|\pi\| + 1))) \cap U$ (continuity of $\psi$ on $U$). For a zero $z_0 \in O \cap N_3$ of $g$, $\psi(z_0) = v + \sigma(v)$ with $v \in V'$, and as in `theorem_3_6_8_nearby` $|v| < \rho$, so $v \in V_1$ and both lemmas apply.
2. *(i), and the level-$1$ points.* Corollary 3.5.3 (preprint 3.41) at $v$ gives $x_1$ on level $1$ of $\mathcal S.G = G \subseteq G''$ (§III.48, ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermShrinkIFT · ConjugationData.corollary_3_5_3_shrink⟧); $x_1$ lies in $U'_1$, so $z_{0,1} = \psi'_1(x_1)$ is a level-$1$ point over $\psi'(\psi(z_0)) = z_0$, and $\psi_1(z_{0,1}) = x_1$ ⟦Chapter2/DegeneracyIndex · levelMap_comp_eq_self⟧. Since $\psi'_1$ maps $G''_1$ into $(G_g)_1$, $z_{0,1}$ lies in $(G_g)_1$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermShrink · ConjugationData.mem_of_levelMap_base_mem⟧: this is the step that needs the shrinking of the basic germ, since $g$ is differentiable only there. (With $g$ sc-smooth on $U$, any level-$1$ point would do.)
3. *(a)* is $N_v = \ker D(h + t)(x_1)$ above, fed with the lift and (i).
4. *$D(\Psi_*g)(x_1) = D(h + t)(x_1)$*: the two maps agree on $U' \cap W'$, so the uniqueness lemma at $x_1$ applies on the open set of $E'_1$ cut out by $W' \cap U_o$ (with $U' = U_o \cap C'$) and the open set defining the level-$1$ germ $G_1$; $\Psi_*(g)$ is an sc¹-germ on the preimage of $G_g$, which contains $G$ ⟦Chapter3/GermPushforward · IsStrongBundleIso.pushforward_isSc1GermWith⟧ (whole-chart: sc-smooth on $U'$ ⟦Chapter3/GoodPositionGermG · IsStrongBundleIso.pushforward_linearization⟧).
5. *(b)*: the kernel formula is the chain rule for the push-forward at the zero $z_{0,1}$ of $g$, in the germ form above. *Surjectivity of $Dg(z_{0,1})$*: given $k$, solve $D(h + t)(x_1)w = \Gamma(z_0)k$ by (i); then $\Gamma(z_0)Dg(z_{0,1})D\psi'(x_1)w = \Gamma(z_0)k$, and $\Gamma(z_0)$ is injective. This is the surjectivity of the *filled* $Dg(z_0) : E \to F$, not of $Tf(z_0) = Dg(z_0)|_{T_{z_0}O} : T_{z_0}O \to K_{z_0}$ (§III.33).
6. *(c), the tangency to $O$* (Finding 102). Condition (3) of Definition 3.1.4 (preprint 3.4), which gave $\ker Dg(0) \subseteq T_0O$ at the centre, is not available at $z_0$. Instead: the transported retraction $r' = \psi \circ r \circ \psi'$ on $U'$ ⟦Chapter2/TameInvariance · IsScDiffeoQuadrant.transport⟧ fixes every graph point, because each $w + \sigma(w)$, $w \in V'$, is $\psi(u)$ for a zero $u \in O$ of $g$ (the graph description), and $r(u) = u$. The lifted graph map $\Gamma_1 = \mathrm{lift} + \sigma_1$ maps $V_1$ into $U'_1$, so the tangent-vector lemma for $r'$ gives $Dr'(x_1)$ fixing $n + D\sigma(v)n$ for every $n \in K$, i.e. $Dr'(x_1)$ is the identity on $N_v$. The derivative of the transported retraction is $Dr'(x_1) = D\psi(z_{0,1})Dr(z_{0,1})D\psi'(x_1)$ ⟦Chapter2/TameInvariance · IsScDiffeoQuadrant.scDeriv_transport⟧ (§III.27); applying $D\psi'(x_1)$ and using that it inverts $D\psi(z_{0,1})$, $Dr(z_{0,1})$ fixes $D\psi'(x_1)m$ for every $m \in N_v$. By (a) and (b), these are exactly the elements of $\ker Dg(z_{0,1})$, so $\ker Dg(z_{0,1}) \subseteq T_{z_0}O$.
7. *(d)* (Finding 103). Lemma 3.6.9 (preprint 3.55) gives $N_v$ in good position to $C'_{v + \sigma(v)}$ in $E'$.
