(c) "$\ker f'(y) = T_yS(f, U)$": in the chart at $x_0$, $\ker D(h + t)(z) = N_v$, the tangent space of the graph (§III.32); the chart form of the statement is §III.45, and the identification with $T_yS$ as a subspace of $T_yX$ needs the linear structure of $T_yX$ (gap G.13 of Chapter 2) and is not formalized.

**Theorem 3.6.11 (preprint 3.57), with the text's hypotheses** ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTTheoremsG · theorem_3_6_11⟧. *Let $f$ be an sc-Fredholm section of a strong bundle whose charts have tame base retractions, with $(f, x)$ in good position at every zero $x$, and let $S \ne \emptyset$. Then $S$ is a sub-M-polyfold of $X$, its induced structure is tame and equivalent to that of a manifold with boundary with corners of locally constant dimension ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTTheorems · MPolyfold.IsManifoldWithCornersLoc⟧ (an equivalent atlas of charts on relatively open subsets of partial quadrants of finite-dimensional spaces), and at every $x \in S$ there are regular data of Definition 3.2.1 (preprint 3.25) of a dimension $d(x)$ such that every datum at a point of $S$ in their chart source has dimension $d(x)$.* *Proof.* At every $x \in S$, Theorem 3.6.8 (2) (preprint 3.54) with $V_{x_0} = X$ — the hypothesis "every zero near $x$ in good position" is part of the global hypothesis — gives the regular data and the local constancy. Theorem 3.2.3 (preprint 3.27) with locally constant dimension (§III.36) ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTTheorems · theorem_3_2_3_loc_of_isRegular⟧ then gives the sub-M-polyfold, the tameness and the corner atlas. **No hypothesis is added**: good position at every zero is the text's hypothesis, and it supplies at every point the nearby good position that the re-centring needs, so the gap of Theorem 3.6.8 (1) (preprint 3.54) does not enter (item 156). $S \ne \emptyset$ is needed for the formal charts, whose inverses need a point of $S$ as a value off the chart (for $S = \emptyset$ the statement says nothing). "Tame strong bundle" is read as above. The text states no dimension; the theorem says that the dimension is locally constant, which the text's tangent germ property of a fixed dimension $d$ near each point implies but does not state (item 152).

**Corollary 3.1.27 (preprint 3.24)** ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTTheoremsG · corollary_3_1_27⟧ is Theorem 3.6.11 (preprint 3.57) without the last clause: if at every zero $f'(x)$ is onto with kernel in good position, the solution set is a sub-M-polyfold with tame induced structure equivalent to a manifold with boundary with corners of locally constant dimension.

**Theorem 3.6.10 (preprint 3.56), $\partial X = \emptyset$, with the text's hypotheses** ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTTheoremsG · theorem_3_6_10⟧. *Under the hypotheses of Theorem 3.6.11 (preprint 3.57) and $d_X \equiv 0$, $S$ is a sub-M-polyfold whose induced structure is equivalent to a manifold with corners of locally constant dimension and has degeneracy index $0$ everywhere*: Theorem 3.6.11 (preprint 3.57) and Lemma 2.4.6 (preprint 2.26), as for Theorem 3.1.22 (preprint 3.19). (The statement keeps only these two conclusions; the tameness of the induced structure and the regular data are those of Theorem 3.6.11 (preprint 3.57).) The text's hypothesis "$f'(x)$ surjective at every zero" is read as good position, which is the text's Definition 3.6.6 (preprint 3.53) for $d_X(x) = 0$; that condition (2) is void in a tame chart at $d_X = 0$, and Theorem 3.6.10 (preprint 3.56) with "$f'(x)$ surjective" and the tameness of the induced structure, are §III.45.

**The parts of fixed dimension** ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTTheoremsG · theorem_3_6_11_dim⟧. Under the hypotheses of Theorem 3.6.11 (preprint 3.57) (without $S \ne \emptyset$), let $S_n$ be the set of points of $S$ at which $S$ has data of Definition 3.2.1 (preprint 3.25) of dimension $n$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTTheorems · solutionSetDim⟧, and $U_n$ the union of the sources of the regular data of dimension $n$ along which the dimension is constantly $n$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTTheorems · solutionSetDimOpen⟧, an open set ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTTheorems · isOpen_solutionSetDimOpen⟧. *Then $S$ is the union of the $S_n$, the $S_n$ are pairwise disjoint, $S_n = S \cap U_n$ and $S_n = S \cap F_n$ for a closed $F_n$ (so $S_n$ is open and closed in $S$), and each nonempty $S_n$ is a sub-M-polyfold whose induced structure is tame and equivalent to an $n$-dimensional manifold with boundary with corners.* *Proof.* At every $x \in S$, Theorem 3.6.8 (2) (preprint 3.54) with $V_{x_0} = X$ gives regular data $D_x$ of a dimension $d(x)$ with the local constancy. *The dimension at a point is well defined*: two data at $x$ of dimensions $n$, $m$ both have the dimension $d(x)$ by the local constancy along $D_x$, since $x$ lies in its source; so the $S_n$ cover $S$ (by $D_x$) and are pairwise disjoint. *$S_n = S \cap U_n$*: for $x \in S_n$, $d(x) = n$ and $D_x$ is one of the data whose sources make up $U_n$; conversely, if $x \in S$ lies in the source of regular data of dimension $n$ along which the dimension is $n$, then $d(x) = n$ and $D_x$ witnesses $x \in S_n$. *$S_n$ is closed in $S$*: $S_n = S \setminus \bigcup_{m \ne n} U_m$, since a point of $S$ lies in exactly one $S_m$; take $F_n$ the complement of that open union. *Each nonempty $S_n$*: at $x \in S_n$, restrict $D_x$ (of dimension $n$) to a datum for $S_n$ — $S_n \cap \text{source} = S \cap \text{source}$ because every point of $S$ in the source has dimension $n$ — still regular ⟦Chapter3/SolutionGermRecentre · MPolyfold.TangentGermChart.shrink_isRegular⟧, and apply Theorem 3.2.3 (preprint 3.27) with fixed dimension $n$ ⟦Chapter3/TangentGerm · theorem_3_2_3_of_isRegular⟧.

*Where the formal route departs from the text.* Two steps of the text's proof are replaced (items 150, 157): the regularity of the solution germ away from the centre, which the proof of Theorem 3.2.3 (preprint 3.27) asserts for any set and which fails for general sets (§III.24), is proved for solution sets by re-centring with the $C^1$-property of $\sigma$ on $V'$ and the level-wise lifts at the nearby zero (§§III.35–III.36); and the relative openness of $\Sigma$, false for an arbitrary good complement, is obtained by choosing the complement (§III.29). With these, the global theorems 3.56, 3.57 and Corollary 3.1.27 (preprint 3.24) are proved with the text's hypotheses, and the local Theorems 3.6.8, 3.1.26 and 3.1.22 (preprint 3.54, 3.23 and 3.19) under the hypothesis that the zeros near the point are in good position, which is Theorem 3.6.8 (1) (preprint 3.54); this section proves that one under the filler condition at the nearby zeros, which Definition 3.1.4 (preprint 3.4) does not impose, and §III.43 with the text's hypotheses. Not stated here: the uniqueness of the manifold structure separately from the equivalence of atlases; the dimension $d$ of these statements is existentially quantified (only as the common dimension of all data of Definition 3.2.1 (preprint 3.25) for $S$ near the point) — its identification with $\dim\ker f'(x) = \operatorname{ind}f'(x)$ is §III.44, and $\ker f'(y) = T_yS$ in the chart is §III.45.

### III.38 The basic germ criterion: Theorem 3.7.1 (preprint 3.58)

**Theorem 3.7.1 (preprint 3.58)** (text): let $U$ be a relatively open neighbourhood of $0$ in a partial quadrant $C \subseteq E$ and $f : U \to F$ sc-smooth, with (1) $Df(x)$ sc-Fredholm at the smooth points, of constant index; (2) an sc-splitting $E = B \oplus X$, $B$ finite-dimensional containing $\ker Df(0)$, with a compactness property: for $b_j \to b$ in $B \cap U$ small and $(\eta_j)$ bounded in $X_m$ with $Df(b_j)\eta_j = y_j + z_j$, $y_j \to 0$ in $F_m$, $(z_j)$ bounded in $F_{m+1}$, $(\eta_j)$ has a convergent subsequence in $X_m$; (3) for every $m$ and $\varepsilon > 0$, $|[D_2f(b, 0) - D_2f(b, x)]h|_m \le \varepsilon|h|_m$ for $b$ small and $x \in X_{m+1}$ small on level $m$. Then $f - f(0)$ is conjugated near $0$ to a basic germ. The proof: $Y = Df(0)X$, $F = A \oplus Y$, $P$ the projection onto $Y$; Lemmas 3.7.2, 3.7.3 (preprint 3.59, 3.60) and an index argument make $L(b) = P \circ Df(b)|_X$ sc-isomorphisms with uniform lower bounds; Lemma 3.7.4 (preprint 3.61) makes $\Phi((b, x), (c, y)) = ((b, x), (c, L(b)^{-1}y))$ a strong bundle isomorphism; and $H(b, x) = x - L(b)^{-1}Ph(b, x)$, $h = f - f(0)$, is a contraction by (3).

**The coordinates.** The formalization works in the coordinates of the text's proof: $E = \mathbb R^n \oplus X$ with $\mathbb R^n$ a finite-dimensional normed space $P$ (constant sc-structure), $C = \widetilde C \oplus X$ with $\widetilde C$ a partial quadrant of $P$, $F = \mathbb R^N \oplus Y$ with $\mathbb R^N = Q$ finite-dimensional, and "$P \circ$" the $Y$-component. Condition (3) becomes ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionProof · DerivCondition⟧: for every $m$ and $\varepsilon > 0$ there is $r > 0$ with $|Df(b, 0)(0, h) - Df(b, x)(0, h)|_m \le \varepsilon|h|_m$ for $h \in X_m$, $b \in \widetilde C$, $|b| < r$, $x \in X_{m+1}$, $|x|_m < r$, $(b, 0)$ and $(b, x)$ in $U_{m+1}$ (the level operators of Proposition 1.2.1 (preprint 1.16)). Condition (2) becomes ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionIndex · CompactnessCondition⟧: there is $r > 0$ such that for every $m$, every $b_j \to b_0$ in $\widetilde C$ with $(b_j, 0) \in U$ and $|b_0| < r$, every bounded $(\eta_j) \subseteq X_m$ with $Df(b_j, 0)(0, \eta_j) = y_j + \iota z_j$, $y_j \to 0$ in $F_m$ and $(z_j)$ bounded in $F_{m+1}$, $(\eta_j)$ has a subsequence converging in $X_m$. (The smallness of $b$ is read as uniform in $m$, as the order of the text's quantifiers suggests; Lemma 3.7.3 (preprint 3.60) needs the property on every level on one neighbourhood.)

**Theorem 3.7.1 (preprint 3.58) as formalized** ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionIndex · theorem_3_7_1⟧. Let $U$ be relatively open in $\widetilde C \oplus X$ with $0 \in U$ and $f : U \to Q \oplus Y$ sc-smooth such that (1) for every $b \in \widetilde C$ with $(b, 0) \in U$ the linearization $Df(b, 0)$, an sc-operator ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionIndex · derivParamOp⟧, is sc-Fredholm with the sc-index of $Df(0)$; (2) $h \mapsto (Df(0)(0, h))_2$ is bijective $X_0 \to Y_0$, and the compactness condition holds; (3) the derivative condition holds. Then there are an open $V_o \ni 0$ in $P$ and sc-isomorphisms $L(b) : X \to Y$ with $L(b)h = (Df(b, 0)(0, h))_2$ for $b \in V_o \cap \widetilde C$, such that the push-forward $k(b, x) = ((f - f(0))_1(b, x), L(b)^{-1}(f - f(0))_2(b, x))$ is a basic germ on $U \cap (V_o \oplus X)$ with contraction map $H(b, x) = x - L(b)^{-1}(f(b, x) - f(0))_2$, $\Phi$ is a strong bundle isomorphism covering the identity, and $f - f(0)$ is conjugated to a basic germ (Definition 3.1.11 (preprint 3.11)). The hypotheses differ from the text's in three places: (1) is asked only at the points $(b, 0)$, $b \in \widetilde C$, which is all the proof uses; "constant index" is equality with the index at $0$; and the text's "$B \supseteq \ker Df(0)$, $Y = Df(0)X$" is replaced by its consequence, the bijectivity of $P \circ Df(0)|_X$ on level $0$. The reduction of a general sc-splitting $E = B \oplus X$, $F = A \oplus Y$ to these coordinates is not formalized.

The proof has two halves, as in the text.

**First half: Theorem 3.7.1 (preprint 3.58) from the family of sc-isomorphisms.**

**Theorem 3.7.1 (preprint 3.58), given the family** ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionProof · theorem_3_7_1_of_family⟧. In addition to the coordinates, $f$ sc-smooth on $U$ and (3), suppose: $V_o$ open with $0 \in V_o$ and $(b, 0) \in U$ for $b \in V_o \cap \widetilde C$; sc-isomorphisms $L(b) : X \to Y$ with $L(b)h = (Df(b, 0)(0, h))_2$ on level $0$ for $b \in V_o \cap \widetilde C$; and for every $m$ a constant $c_m > 0$ with $c_m|h|_m \le |L(b)h|_m$ for $b \in V_o \cap \widetilde C$, $h \in X_m$. Then, with $U' = U \cap (V_o \oplus X)$, the conclusions above hold.

*Step 1: $(b, h) \mapsto L(b)h$ is sc-smooth on $(V_o \cap \widetilde C) \oplus X$* ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionProof · famMap_isScSmooth_of_deriv⟧ (the text: "since $B$ is a finite-dimensional sc-smooth space, the map $(b, x) \mapsto P \circ Df(b)x$ is sc-smooth"). It is $\mathrm{pr}_Y \circ Tf \circ J$ with the sc-operator $J(b, h) = ((b, 0), (0, h))$ into the tangent space ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionProof · tangentInjOp⟧; $J$ maps into the tangent set because $(b, 0) \in U$ lies on every level; and the tangent map of the sc-smooth $f$ is sc-smooth.

*Step 2: $(b, y) \mapsto L(b)^{-1}y$ is sc-smooth on $(V_o \cap \widetilde C) \oplus Y$* ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionProof · famInv_isScSmooth_of⟧. This is Lemma 3.7.4 (preprint 3.61) (§III.25) ⟦Chapter3/InverseFamily · lemma_3_7_4⟧ for the germ $\{V_o \cap \widetilde C\}$ with the uniform lower bounds, translated between maps on sets and germs on the special germ of a set: an sc$^k$-germ on $\{U\}$ is an sc$^k$-map on $U$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionProof · IsSckGerm.isSck_ofSet⟧ ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionProof · IsScSmoothGerm.isScSmooth_ofSet⟧, by induction on $k$: the sc⁰ case is a matter of definitions ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionProof · IsSc0Germ.isSc0_ofSet⟧; for the step, the tangent germ of $\{U\}$ is the germ of the tangent set, and the derivative family of the germ agrees with the derivative of the map on the tangent set; the converse is `IsSck.isSckGerm` (§III.1).

*Step 3: the strong bundle isomorphism $\Phi$.* For a family $M(b) : Y \to X$ of sc-isomorphisms put $\Phi_M((b, z), (c, y)) = ((b, z), (c, M(b)y))$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionProof · fiberIsoMap⟧. If $(b, y) \mapsto M(b)y$ is sc-smooth on $(V_o \cap \widetilde C) \oplus Y$ and the parameters of $U'$ lie in $V_o \cap \widetilde C$, $\Phi_M$ is a strong bundle map $U' \triangleleft (Q \oplus Y) \to U' \triangleleft (Q \oplus X)$ covering the identity ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionProof · fiberIsoMap_isStrongBundleMap⟧: it is linear on the fibres; it preserves the filtration because $M(b)$ maps $Y_k$ to $X_k$; on $[0]$ it is sc-smooth as the pairing of the projection with $(c, M(b)y)$, the latter the composite of the sc-operator $((b, z), (c, y)) \mapsto (b, y)$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionProof · paramFiberOp⟧ with $(b, y) \mapsto M(b)y$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionProof · fiberIsoMap_isScSmooth⟧; on $[1]$, $\Phi_M[1]$ is $\Phi_{M^1}$ for the shifted family $M^1(b) = M(b) : Y^1 \to X^1$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionProof · shiftFam⟧ ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionProof · shiftFiber_fiberIsoMap⟧, and $(b, y) \mapsto M^1(b)y$ is sc-smooth by Proposition 1.2.2 (preprint 1.17) ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionProof · famMap_shiftFam_isScSmooth⟧ — the text's "if we raise the index by one, the map is also sc-smooth by Proposition 1.17". With $M = L^{-1}$ (Step 2) and $M = L$ (Step 1) the two maps are inverse to each other, so $\Phi = \Phi_{L^{-1}}$ is a strong bundle isomorphism ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionProof · fiberIsoMap_isStrongBundleIso⟧.

*Step 4: the push-forward and the contraction map.* With $h = f - f(0)$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionProof · shiftedGerm⟧, $\Phi_*h = k$, $k(b, x) = (h_1(b, x), L(b)^{-1}h_2(b, x))$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionProof · pushGerm358⟧, and the $X$-component of $k$ is $x - H(b, x)$ with $H(b, x) = x - L(b)^{-1}h_2(b, x)$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionProof · contrMap358⟧ ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionProof · pushGerm358_snd⟧. Both are sc-smooth on $U'$: sums, pairings and the composite of $(b, y) \mapsto L(b)^{-1}y$ with $(b, x) \mapsto (b, h_2(b, x))$. On level $m$, $H_m(b, w) = w - L(b)_m^{-1}((f_m(b, w))_2 - (f_m(0))_2)$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionProof · levelMap_contrMap358⟧, and $|L(b)_m^{-1}| \le c_m^{-1}$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionProof · norm_symm_le⟧.

*Step 5: the contraction estimate* ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionProof · contrMap358_contraction⟧. For every $m$ and $\varepsilon > 0$ there is $r > 0$ such that $(a, w) \in U'_m$ and $|H(a, w) - H(a, w')|_m \le \varepsilon|w - w'|_m$ for $a \in \widetilde C$, $|a| < r$, $w, w' \in X_m$ small on level $m$. Choose $r$ below the radius $r_3$ of (3) for $\varepsilon c_m$, below the radius of a ball around $0$ in $V_o$, and so small that $(a, z) \in U_m$ whenever $|a| < r$ and $|z|_m < r$ ($U = V \cap C$ with $V$ open; the level-$m$ norm of $z$ controls its level-$0$ norm through the norm of the inclusion) ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionProof · mem_lvlSet_of_small⟧. First let $w_1, w'_1 \in X_{m+1}$ with $|w_1|_m, |w'_1|_m < r$. On the convex set $S = \{z \in X_{m+1} : |z|_m < r\}$ the map $g(z) = f_m(a, \iota z)$ has the derivative $Df(a, z)(0, \iota\,\cdot)$ (Proposition 1.2.1 (preprint 1.16) on level $m$) ⟦Chapter1/Levels · IsSc1With.hasFDerivWithinAt_level⟧, and by (3) $|Df(a, z)(0, v) - Df(a, 0)(0, v)|_m \le \varepsilon c_m|v|_m$. **The mean value inequality with the derivative bound in the weaker norm** ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionProof · norm_sub_sub_le_of_hasFDerivWithinAt_segment⟧ — the one-dimensional mean value inequality for $t \mapsto g(w'_1 + t(w_1 - w'_1)) - T\iota(w'_1 + t(w_1 - w'_1))$ on $[0, 1]$, which measures the increment in the norm of $X_m$ rather than of $X_{m+1}$, as condition (3) requires — gives
$$|g(w_1) - g(w'_1) - T\iota(w_1 - w'_1)|_m \le \varepsilon c_m|\iota(w_1 - w'_1)|_m, \qquad T = Df(a, 0)(0, \cdot).$$
The $Y$-component of $T$ is $L(a)_m$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionProof · snd_levelDeriv_eq_L⟧ (the level-$m$ operator of Proposition 1.2.1 (preprint 1.16) restricts the level-$0$ one). Then $H(a, w_1) - H(a, w'_1) = L(a)^{-1}[L(a)(w_1 - w'_1) - (f(a, w_1) - f(a, w'_1))_2]$, whose norm is at most $c_m^{-1}\varepsilon c_m|w_1 - w'_1|_m$. This is the text's integral $\int_0^1 D_2f(b, tx + (1 - t)x')(x - x')\,dt$, made precise. For general $w, w' \in X_m$: approximate them by sequences from $X_{m+1}$ (dense in $X_m$), small on level $m$ for large indices, and pass to the limit with the continuity of $H_m$ on $U'_m$ — the text's "by the density of $X_{m+1}$ in $X_m$".

*Step 6: the basic germ and the conjugation.* $k$ is a basic germ on the germ $\{U'\}$ with contraction map $H$ (Definition 3.1.7 (preprint 3.7) as formalized ⟦Chapter3/LocalCompactness · IsBasicGerm⟧): it is an sc-smooth germ (Step 4), $k(0) = 0$, its $X$-component is $x - H(b, x)$, and $H$ is an sc⁰-contraction germ — sc⁰, $H(0) = 0$ since $L(0)^{-1}0 = 0$, and the contraction estimate on every level with every $\varepsilon$ (Step 5). The conjugation identity $(\Phi(u, h(u)))_2 = k(u)$ holds on all of $U'$, because $\Phi$ covers the identity; with $\Phi$ from Step 3 these are the data of Definition 3.1.11 (preprint 3.11) for $h = f - f(0)$.

**Second half: the family from conditions (1) and (2).**

*The family.* At the smooth point $(b, 0)$ the linearization $Df(b, 0)$ is an sc-operator ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionIndex · derivParamOp⟧ ⟦Chapter1/Properties · IsSc1With.scOperator_deriv⟧ whose level maps are the level operators of Proposition 1.2.1 (preprint 1.16) ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionIndex · derivParamOp_lvl⟧; $L(b) = \mathrm{pr}_Y \circ Df(b, 0) \circ \mathrm{inr}_X$ is an sc-operator $X \to Y$ (set to $0$ where $(b, 0) \notin U$) ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionIndex · derivFamOp⟧; and $(b, h) \mapsto L(b)h$ is jointly continuous on $O \times X_m$ for parameters with $(b, 0) \in U$, by the continuity of the level operators (Proposition 1.2.1 (2) (preprint 1.16)) ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionIndex · continuousOn_levelDeriv_param⟧ ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionIndex · continuousOn_derivFamOp⟧.

*The compactness property of the family* ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionIndex · familyCompactness_derivFamOp⟧. Let $O$ be a set of parameters in $\widetilde C$ with $(b, 0) \in U$ and $|b| < r$ ($r$ from (2)), $b_j \to b_0$ in $O$, $(h_j)$ bounded in $X_m$, $L(b_j)h_j \to 0$. Write $Df(b_j, 0)(0, h_j) = (0, L(b_j)h_j) + ((Df(b_j, 0)(0, h_j))_Q, 0)$. The first summand tends to $0$ in $F_m$. The second lies in $Q \oplus 0$, where all levels coincide, and is bounded: a jointly continuous family of operators is uniformly bounded near a parameter ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionIndex · exists_bound_of_continuousOn_clm⟧ (continuity at $(b_0, 0)$, then scaling), so $|Df(b_j, 0)(0, \cdot)|$ is bounded for $j$ large. Condition (2) applied to the tails of the sequences gives a convergent subsequence: this is the compactness property of the abstract Lemmas 3.7.2, 3.7.3 (preprint 3.59, 3.60) (§III.25) ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterion · FamilyCompactness⟧. The text's "$z_j = Df(b_j)h_j \in A$ is bounded on every level since $A$ is a smooth subspace" omits the uniform operator bound.

*Lemma 3.7.2 (preprint 3.59).* Let $V' = \{b : (b, 0) \in V\}$ ($U = V \cap C$) and $O = V' \cap B(0, r) \cap \widetilde C$, which contains $0$. $L(0)$ is injective on $X_0$ by (2), so Lemma 3.7.2 (preprint 3.59) ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterion · lemma_3_7_2⟧ gives $r_1 > 0$ with $L(b)$ injective on $X_0$ for $b \in O$, $|b| < r_1$, hence on every level (the inclusions are injective and commute with $L(b)$) ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionIndex · ScOperator.injective_lvl⟧.

*Lemma 3.7.3 (preprint 3.60).* Take a closed ball $\bar B(0, r'_1)$ inside $V' \cap B(0, r) \cap B(0, r_1)$ and $K = \bar B(0, r'_1) \cap \widetilde C$, compact (since $\widetilde C$ is closed) and inside $O$. On $K$ every $L(b)_m$ is injective, the family is jointly continuous and has the compactness property (restricted from $O$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionIndex · FamilyCompactness.mono⟧), so Lemma 3.7.3 (preprint 3.60) ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterion · lemma_3_7_3⟧ gives $c_m > 0$ with $c_m|h|_m \le |L(b)h|_m$ for $b \in K$.

*The index argument.* The text: "$P \circ Df(b)|_X$ is an injective sc-Fredholm operator of index $0$, hence an sc-isomorphism". The formal proof uses a block computation instead. **The index of a block operator** ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionIndex · fredholmIndex_block⟧: for a Fredholm $T : P \oplus X \to Q \oplus Y$ with $P$, $Q$ finite-dimensional and the block $L = \mathrm{pr}_Y \circ T \circ \mathrm{inr}_X$ of finite index, $\mathrm{ind}\,T = \dim P + \mathrm{ind}\,L - \dim Q$. Indeed $T - \mathrm{inr}_Y \circ L \circ \mathrm{pr}_X$ is $(p, x) \mapsto T(p, 0) + ((T(0, x))_Q, 0)$, of finite rank, hence compact, so $\mathrm{inr}_Y \circ L \circ \mathrm{pr}_X$ is Fredholm with the index of $T$ ⟦Chapter1/FredholmPerturbation · isFredholm_add_isCompactOperator⟧ ⟦Chapter1/FredholmIndex · fredholmIndex_add_isCompactOperator⟧; and the indices of $\mathrm{pr}_X$ and $\mathrm{inr}_Y$ are $\dim P$ and $-\dim Q$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionIndex · hasFiniteIndex_snd_and_linIndex⟧ ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionIndex · hasFiniteIndex_inr_and_linIndex⟧, which add under composition ⟦Chapter1/FredholmIndex · linIndex_comp⟧. At $b = 0$, $L(0)$ is bijective on level $0$, of index $0$, so $\mathrm{ind}\,Df(0) = \dim P - \dim Q$ on level $0$, which is the sc-index (an sc-Fredholm operator has the same index on every level) ⟦Chapter1/FredholmIndex · IsScFredholm.fredholmIndex_lvl⟧. **Surjectivity from the index** ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionIndex · surjective_of_fredholmIndex_block⟧: if $\mathrm{ind}\,T = \dim P - \dim Q$ and the block $L$ is injective, then $L$ has finite index — its cokernel is a quotient of the cokernel of the Fredholm operator $\mathrm{inr}_Y \circ L \circ \mathrm{pr}_X$ — and index $0$ by the block formula, so it is surjective. For $b \in K$ and every $m$, $Df(b, 0)_m$ is Fredholm of index $\mathrm{ind}\,Df(0) = \dim P - \dim Q$ by (1) and the level-wise index, and $L(b)_m$ is injective; so $L(b)_m$ is bijective, a topological isomorphism by the open mapping theorem, and the $L(b)$, $b \in K$, are sc-isomorphisms. The sc-Fredholm property of the projection $P$ and the composition rule for sc-Fredholm operators are not needed.

*Conclusion.* Extend $b \mapsto L(b)$ outside $K$ by $L(0)$, put $V_o = B(0, r'_1)$ (so $V_o \cap \widetilde C \subseteq K$), and apply the first half with the lower bounds of Lemma 3.7.3 (preprint 3.60). The neighbourhood replacement $U' = U \cap (V_o \oplus X)$ is the text's "replacing, if necessary, $U$ by a smaller neighbourhood".

### III.39 What the addendum taught us

1. **The global implicit function theorems hold with the text's hypotheses.** Theorem 3.6.11 (preprint 3.57), Corollary 3.1.27 (preprint 3.24) and Theorem 3.6.10 (preprint 3.56) are proved for an sc-Fredholm section of a strong bundle whose charts have tame base retractions, with good position at every zero ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTTheoremsG · theorem_3_6_11⟧ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTTheoremsG · corollary_3_1_27⟧ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTTheoremsG · theorem_3_6_10⟧ (§III.37). Theorem 3.6.10 (preprint 3.56) reads "$f'(x)$ surjective" as good position, which the text's Definition 3.6.6 (preprint 3.53) makes the same thing at points with $d_X(x) = 0$; that the kernel condition is void in the chart form is not formalized.
2. **Two steps of the text's proof had to be replaced, and both repairs are specific to solution sets.** The regularity of the solution germ away from the centre, which the proof of Theorem 3.2.3 (preprint 3.27) asserts for any set and which fails in general (§III.24), follows for two graph descriptions of one set from the $C^1$-property of the first and the level-wise lifts of the second, by the re-centring lemma ⟦Chapter3/SolutionGermRecentre · recentre_contDiffOn_levels⟧ (§III.35). The relative openness of $\Sigma$, false for an arbitrary good complement, holds for a complement whose projection maps the partial quadrant into itself, and the proof of Theorem 3.6.8 (preprint 3.54) can produce such a complement from the start ⟦Chapter3/GoodComplementProjection · exists_goodComplement_projectsInto⟧ (§III.29). Read off the statement of the regularity theorem ⟦Chapter3/SolutionGermRecentre · MPolyfold.TangentGermChart.exists_isRegular_of_locally⟧, the first repair works for every set with the tangent germ property whose data satisfy the second regularity condition (§III.36).
3. **The proof of the local Theorem 3.6.8 (1) (preprint 3.54) has a gap, and it is the same gap as in the book's arXiv version.** At a nearby zero, Corollary 3.5.3 (preprint 3.41) gives the surjectivity of the filled linearization; the surjectivity of $f'(y)$ needs condition (3) of Definition 3.1.4 (preprint 3.4) at that zero, which the definition imposes at the centre only ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbySurj · exists_range_scDeriv_eq_ρ_of_normal_injective⟧. The local theorems were therefore first proved under that condition at the nearby zeros ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbySurjG · StrongBundle.IsGoodPositionGermFilled⟧ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbySurjG · theorem_3_6_8_one⟧ (§III.33); the kernel half needs nothing (§III.32). The global theorems do not need it, because their hypothesis already gives good position at every zero. The second addendum proves the surjectivity with Definition 3.1.4 (preprint 3.4) as stated (§§III.41–III.43).
4. **Definition 3.6.6 (preprint 3.53) presupposes a chart independence the text does not prove.** It holds at zeros, because only the fibre part of a chart transition survives there ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · linAt_chart_change⟧ (§III.31), and it is what lets the good position found in the chart at $x_0$ be transported to the chart centred at a nearby zero.
5. **Three silent steps are theorems of their own**: tameness is carried by sc-diffeomorphisms and by open subsets ⟦Chapter2/TameInvariance · IsScDiffeoQuadrant.isTameRetract_image⟧ (§III.27); the kernel of the linearization of a basic germ plus an sc⁺-germ consists of smooth points ⟦Chapter3/SolutionGermRegularity · StabilityData.ker_Dfs_le_smooth⟧ (§III.34); and the index raise of a strong bundle isomorphism is one ⟦Chapter3/BasicClassComposition · IsStrongBundleIso.shift⟧ (§III.28).
6. **The dimension of the solution set is locally constant, not constant**, and the dimension count that proves the re-centring is also what shows it (Sard's lemma in fixed dimension ⟦Chapter3/SolutionGermRecentre · finrank_le_of_interior_image_nonempty⟧, §III.35). Theorem 3.2.3 (preprint 3.27) is therefore also proved with a dimension that may vary ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTTheorems · theorem_3_2_3_loc_of_isRegular⟧ (§III.36), and the solution set of Theorem 3.6.11 (preprint 3.57) splits into open and closed parts of fixed dimension ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTTheoremsG · theorem_3_6_11_dim⟧.
7. **Proposition 3.6.5's (preprint 3.52) index needs the conjugation as data.** With the witnesses of the conjugation named, the index of $Tf(x_0)$ is the $n - N$ of every conjugating basic germ, and that number does not depend on the conjugation ⟦Chapter3/FredholmGermIndexG · proposition_3_6_5_index⟧ ⟦Chapter3/FredholmGermIndexG · ConjugationData.index_eq⟧ (§III.30).
8. **The quadrant inverse function theorem is not the right tool away from the centre**: it needs the degeneracy index to be preserved, which for the solution set is unknown; Lemma 3.8.21's (preprint 3.77) bootstrapping with the hypotheses its proof uses suffices ⟦Chapter3/SolutionGermRecentre · InvDataW.contDiffOn_inv⟧ (§III.35).
9. **The basic germ criterion is proved in the coordinates of its proof**, with condition (1) needed only along the finite-dimensional directions and the text's index argument replaced by a block index formula ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionIndex · fredholmIndex_block⟧ ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermCriterionIndex · theorem_3_7_1⟧ (§III.38).
10. **What was left of Chapter 3** after this addendum: the surjectivity in Theorem 3.6.8 (1) (preprint 3.54) with Definition 3.1.4 (preprint 3.4) as stated; in Theorem 3.1.22 (preprint 3.19), the dimension as the Fredholm index and $\ker f'(y) = T_yS$; Proposition 3.6.5 (preprint 3.52) as an sc-Fredholm operator on $T_xX$; Theorem 3.7.1 (preprint 3.58) for a general sc-splitting; Theorem 3.1.21 (preprint 3.18); Lemma 3.8.13 (preprint 3.70) for a general presentation of the partial quadrant; and Theorem 3.2.3 (preprint 3.27) for sets whose data violate the second regularity condition. The second addendum (§§III.40–III.46) settles the first three items, the third in charts, and Theorem 3.1.21 (preprint 3.18).

### III.40 Theorem 3.1.21 (preprint 3.18): parametrized perturbations

The text states Theorem 3.1.21 (preprint 3.18) after a five-line argument: the section $\tilde f(r, x) = ((r, x), f(x))$ of the pull-back bundle $\pi^*P \to \mathbb R^n \times X$, $\pi(r, x) = x$, "is a sc-Fredholm section as is readily verified", $\tilde s(r, x) = ((r, x), \sum_i r_is_i(x))$ is an sc⁺-section, and Theorem 3.1.18 (preprint 3.15) adds them. The formalization follows this route. What it has to build first is everything the five lines take for granted: $\mathbb R^n$ as an M-polyfold with enough charts, the pull-back bundle as a strong bundle, the sc-calculus for a scalar factor, the product chart with its filler, and the extension of a basic germ by $n$ inert parameters (Findings 160–167). Tameness is not used anywhere, as for Theorem 3.1.18 (preprint 3.15).

**$\mathbb R^n$ and $\mathbb R^n \times X$.** $\mathbb R^n$ carries the constant sc-structure ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · rnSc⟧ and the local model $(\mathbb R^n, \mathbb R^n, \mathbb R^n)$ ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · rnModel⟧. For every $a$ the translation $r \mapsto r - a$ is a chart ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · rnChart⟧; two translation charts are compatible, because their transition maps are translations and a $C^\infty$ map between finite-dimensional spaces is sc-smooth for the constant structures ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · isScSmoothRetractMap_const_of_contDiff⟧ ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · rnChart_compatible⟧. The M-polyfold $\mathbb R^n$ has the identity chart as its atlas ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · rnPolyfold⟧, and every translation chart is a chart of it ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · isChart_rnChart⟧. These charts are needed: Definition 3.1.11 (preprint 3.11) asks for a chart *centred* at the smooth point $(r_0, x)$, and the product of the identity of $\mathbb R^n$ with a chart of $X$ is centred only at $r_0 = 0$. $\mathbb R^n$ is tame ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · rnPolyfold_isTame⟧ and every point lies on every level ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · rnPolyfold_levelSet⟧; so $(r, x)$ is on level $m$ of the product iff $x$ is ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · prod_rn_mem_levelSet_iff⟧, smooth iff $x$ is ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · prod_rn_mem_smoothSet_iff⟧, and $\mathbb R^n \times X$ is tame when $X$ is ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · prod_rn_isTame⟧ — which the text needs, since Definition 3.1.16 (preprint 3.14) is stated for tame bundles.

**Two rules of the sc-calculus.** Scalar multiplication $\mathbb R \oplus F \to F$ is continuous and bilinear on every level, hence sc-smooth by Proposition 1.2.4 (preprint 1.19) ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · isScSmooth_smul⟧; composed with a pairing, $x \mapsto a(x)v(x)$ is sc-smooth for sc-smooth $a : U \to \mathbb R$ and $v : U \to F$ ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · IsScSmooth.smul⟧; finite sums of sc-smooth maps are sc-smooth ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · IsScSmooth.finsetSum⟧. The coordinate $r \mapsto r_i$ is an sc-operator ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · rnCoordOp⟧. Chapter 1 states neither rule.

**The pull-back bundle.** Proposition 2.6.8 (preprint 2.54) is formalized as an existence statement ⟦Chapter2/StrongBundlePullback · proposition_2_6_8⟧; here it is made a structure, the strong bundle $\pi^*P$ with the atlas of all pulled-back charts ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · StrongBundle.pullback⟧, whose total space is paracompact by Theorem 2.3.10 (preprint 2.16) ⟦Chapter2/StrongBundlePullback · paracompactSpace_pullbackSpace⟧. A pulled-back chart is made from a chart $\psi$ of the base $Z$ around $z$ and a strong bundle chart $c$ of $P$ around $\pi(z)$, with $\pi$ mapping the domain of $\psi$ into that of $c$ ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · exists_pullbackChartData⟧; such a chart is a chart of $\pi^*P$ as soon as $c$ is merely compatible with the atlas of $P$ ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · PullbackChartData.isChart_toChart⟧. A map $\tau : Z \to Y$ over $\pi$ is a section $z \mapsto (z, \tau(z))$ of $\pi^*Y$ ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · pullbackLift⟧ ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · isSection_pullbackLift⟧, whose value in a pulled-back chart is $(\psi(z), \mathrm{pr}_F\Phi(\tau(z)))$ ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · PullbackChartData.toChart_pullbackLift⟧. For $\tau = \sigma \circ \pi$ the representative is $w \mapsto (w, \hat\sigma(g(w)))$ with $g = \varphi \circ \pi \circ \psi^{-1}$, which maps the retract of $\psi$ into that of $\varphi$ ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · PullbackChartData.g_mem_O⟧ ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · PullbackChartData.toChart_pullbackLift_comp⟧; so the pull-back of an sc-smooth section along an sc-smooth map is sc-smooth ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · StrongBundle.IsScSmoothSection.pullback⟧ (Finding 167).

**The three sections.** $F(r, x) = f(x) + \sum_ir_is_i(x)$ is computed in the fibre $P^{-1}(x)$, from the fibre combination $\sum_ir_is_i(x)$ ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · fiberComb⟧, and read in the fibre of $\pi^*Y$ over $(r, x)$ ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · paramSection⟧; $\tilde f$ ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · paramLift⟧ and $\tilde s$ ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · paramComb⟧ are the text's sections, and $F = \tilde f + \tilde s$ in the fibres of $\pi^*Y$ ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · paramSection_eq_sectionAdd⟧.

**$\tilde s$ is an sc⁺-section** ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · StrongBundle.isScPlusSection_paramComb⟧. The atlas of $\pi^*P$ contains the charts pulled back through *every* chart $\psi$ of $\mathbb R^n \times X$, not only product charts, so the check must work for an arbitrary $\psi$. In a pulled-back chart, by the linearity of $\Phi$ on the fibre over $x = \pi(z)$, $\tilde s(z)$ has the value $(\psi(z), \sum_ir_i\hat s_i(x))$ ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · PullbackChartData.toChart_paramComb⟧, so the principal part is $w \mapsto \sum_i\lambda_i(w)\hat s_i(g(w))$ with $\lambda(w) = (\psi^{-1}(w))_1$. The map $\lambda$ is sc-smooth on the retract of $\psi$: it is the projection $\mathbb R^n \times X \to \mathbb R^n$ (an sc-smooth map of M-polyfolds) read in $\psi$ and in the identity chart of $\mathbb R^n$ ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · isScSmooth_chart_coord⟧. Each $\hat s_i \circ g$ is sc-smooth into $F^1$, $s_i$ being sc⁺. The product rule and the sum rule conclude.

**The product chart and its filler.** For a strong bundle chart $c$ of $P$ and $r_0 \in \mathbb R^n$, the product chart ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · PullbackChartData.ofProd⟧ is $\psi = (r \mapsto r - r_0) \times \varphi$ on $\mathbb R^n \times V$, with the retraction $1 \times r$ of $\mathbb R^n \times U$ onto $\mathbb R^n \times O$; there $g = \varphi \circ \pi \circ \psi^{-1}$ is the projection $(a, u) \mapsto u$. *Filler conditions.* If $g$ fills $\hat f$ in $c$, then $(a, u) \mapsto g(u)$ fills $(a, u) \mapsto \hat f(u)$ in the product chart ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · PullbackChartData.ofProd_isFillerOf⟧. Conditions (1) and (2) are those of $g$, because the fibre projection of the product retraction at $(a, u)$ is $\rho(r(u))$ — which uses $\varphi(\varphi^{-1}(r(u))) = r(u)$ on $O$. Condition (3): $D(1 \times r)(0) = 1 \times Dr(0)$ ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · PullbackChartData.ofProd_scDeriv_r⟧, so its kernel is $0 \times \ker Dr(0)$; the fibre projection at $0$ is $\rho(0)$ (from $r(0) = 0$); and $[1 - \rho(r(u))]g(u)$ does not depend on $a$, so its derivative at $0$ on $0 \times \ker Dr(0)$ is that of $g$. *For a filling germ* $g$ on $G$ the same holds on the product germ $\mathbb R^n \times G$, with levels $\mathbb R^n \times G_m$, unbounded in the parameter direction, which Definition 3.1.1 (preprint 3.1) allows ⟦Chapter3/GermCalculusParam · ScNbhdGerm.univProd⟧: $(a, u) \mapsto g(u)$ is a germ on it with the derivative family $Dg(u) \circ \mathrm{pr}_2$ ⟦Chapter3/GermCalculusParam · IsSc1GermWith.univProd⟧, and the fill defect in the product chart is the pull-back along $\mathrm{pr}_2$ of that of $g$, so condition (3) is the kernel identification just made, without a uniqueness argument ⟦Chapter3/GermCalculusParam · PullbackChartData.ofProd_isFillingGerm⟧. *Principal parts* ($a = r - r_0$ the chart coordinate): $\tilde s$ has $(a, u) \mapsto \sum_i(a + r_0)_i\hat s_i(u)$ ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · PullbackChartData.ofProd_principal_paramComb⟧ and $F$ has $(a, u) \mapsto \hat f(u) + \sum_i(a + r_0)_i\hat s_i(u)$ ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · PullbackChartData.ofProd_principal_paramSection⟧, the text's $(r, u) \mapsto \hat f(u) + \sum_ir_i\hat s_i(u)$.

**Inert parameters.** Let $\Lambda$ be finite-dimensional. The product $1_\Lambda \times \Phi$, $((a, u), k) \mapsto ((a, \varphi(u)), \Gamma(u, k))$, of a map $\Phi$ of $U \triangleleft F$ with the identity of $\Lambda$ ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · paramMap⟧ is a strong bundle map when $\Phi$ is: on $[0]$ and $[1]$ it is $1 \times \Phi$ up to the reassociations of the factors, which are sc-operators ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · ScBanach.assocOp⟧ ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · ScBanach.assocInvOp⟧ ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · IsStrongBundleMap.paramMap⟧; and a strong bundle isomorphism, with inverse $1_\Lambda \times \Phi'$, when $\Phi$ is ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · IsStrongBundleIso.paramMap⟧. The $\Lambda$-extension of a germ of neighbourhoods is $U''_m = \{((a, \lambda), w) \mid (a, w) \in U_m\}$ ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · ScNbhdGerm.paramExtend⟧, unbounded in $\lambda$, which Definition 3.1.1 (preprint 3.1) allows. *The $\Lambda$-extension of a basic germ is basic* ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · IsBasicGerm.paramExtend⟧: if $h(a, w) = (h_1(a, w), w - B(a, w))$ is basic on $\widetilde C \oplus W$, then $h'((a, \lambda), w) = h(a, w)$ is basic on $(\widetilde C \times \Lambda) \oplus W$, with $B'((a, \lambda), w) = B(a, w)$: it is the composite of $h$ with the projection forgetting $\lambda$ ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · paramForgetOp⟧, and the contraction estimate of Definition 3.3.1 (preprint 3.28) holds with the same radius since $|a| \le |(a, \lambda)|$. *The $\Lambda$-extension of a conjugation* ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · IsConjugateToBasicGerm.paramExtend⟧: if $k$ is conjugated to $h$ by $\Psi$, then $(\lambda, u) \mapsto k(u)$ is conjugated to $h'$ by $\Psi''((\lambda, u), k) = ((((\psi(u))_1, \lambda), (\psi(u))_2), \Gamma(u, k))$, the composite ⟦Chapter3/BasicClassComposition · IsStrongBundleIso.comp⟧ of $1_\Lambda \times \Psi$ with the linear reassociation $(\lambda, (a, w)) \mapsto ((a, \lambda), w)$ ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · paramSwapOp⟧ ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · paramSwapInvOp⟧, a strong bundle isomorphism as a pair of sc-isomorphisms ⟦Chapter3/BasicClassComposition · ScOperator.isStrongBundleIso_prodMap⟧. The parameter quadrant is $\widetilde C \times \Lambda$ in $P \times \Lambda$, put second so as to reuse the product of a partial quadrant with a whole space; the order is immaterial (Finding 163).

**$\tilde f$ is an sc-Fredholm section** ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbationG · StrongBundle.IsScFredholmSection.param⟧. *Sc-smooth*: it is the pull-back of $f$ along the projection. *Regularizing* ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbationG · StrongBundle.regularizing_paramLift⟧: if $(r, x)$ is on level $m$ and $\tilde f(r, x)$ on level $(m, m + 1)$ of $\pi^*Y$, then $x \in X_m$ and $f(x) \in Y_{m,m+1}$ — tested in the pulled-back charts, whose fibre parts are those of $P$ — so $x \in X_{m+1}$ because $f$ is regularizing, and $(r, x)$ is on level $m + 1$. *The germ at a smooth point $(r_0, x)$* ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbationG · StrongBundle.IsScFredholmGerm.param⟧: $x$ is smooth, so Definition 3.1.11 (preprint 3.11) at $x$ gives a centred chart $c$, a filling germ $g$ on $G_g$, an sc⁺-germ $t_0$ on $G_{t_0}$ with $t_0(0) = g(0)$ and a conjugation of $g - t_0$ to a basic germ. In the product chart at $(r_0, x)$, which is centred there, the principal part of $\tilde f$ is $(a, u) \mapsto \hat f(u)$, its filling germ $(a, u) \mapsto g(u)$ on $\mathbb R^n \times G_g$ (the filler conditions above), the sc⁺-germ to subtract $(a, u) \mapsto t_0(u)$ on $\mathbb R^n \times G_{t_0}$ ⟦Chapter3/GermCalculusParam · IsScSmoothGerm.univProd⟧, and $(a, u) \mapsto (g - t_0)(u)$ is conjugated to the $\mathbb R^n$-extension of the basic germ; the conjugation concerns $g - t_0$ near $0$ in $U$, and $1 \times \Psi$ and the extended basic germ are whole-chart objects, so no germ calculus enters it. This is the "readily verified".

**Theorem 3.1.21 (preprint 3.18)** ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbationG · theorem_3_1_21_preprint⟧. *Statement.* For a strong bundle $P : Y \to X$, an sc-Fredholm section $f$ and sc⁺-sections $s_1, \dots, s_n$, the section $F$ is an sc-Fredholm section of $\pi^*P$. *Proof.* $F = \tilde f + \tilde s$; $\tilde f$ is sc-Fredholm and $\tilde s$ is sc⁺; Theorem 3.1.18 (preprint 3.15) ⟦Chapter3/FredholmSectionsG · theorem_3_1_18_preprint⟧. The paracompactness of $\mathbb R^n \times X$ and of $\pi^*Y$ (Definitions 2.3.4, 2.6.5 (preprint 2.13, 2.51)) enters as instance arguments; the version without them supplies both by Theorem 2.3.10 (preprint 2.16) ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbationG · theorem_3_1_21'⟧ ⟦Chapter2/FibredProduct · MPolyfold.paracompactSpace_prod⟧. The text's sketch of the germ of $F$ — filled version $(r, u) \mapsto g(u) + \sum_ir_i\hat s_i(r(u))$, sc⁺-part $t_0(u) + \sum_ir_i\hat s_i(r(u))$, conjugation $\Psi'(r, u, k) = (r - r_0, \Psi(u, k))$ — is exactly what Theorem 3.1.18 (preprint 3.15) produces from the germ data of $\tilde f$ and the principal part of $\tilde s$ ⟦Chapter3/FredholmSectionsG · StrongBundle.IsScFredholmGerm.sectionAdd⟧; the translation $r - r_0$ sits in the chart rather than in the conjugation (Finding 164).

**The linearization** (after Theorem 3.1.22 (preprint 3.19) in the text, used for the finite-dimensional reduction and for transversality). In the product chart at $(r_0, x)$, for a chart $c$ with $0 \in O$, the derivative of $\hat F \circ (1 \times r)$ at $0$ is
$$(\delta a, \delta u) \mapsto D(\hat G \circ r)(0)\delta u + \sum_i\delta a_i\hat s_i(0),\qquad\hat G = \hat f + \sum_ir_{0,i}\hat s_i$$
⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbationG · scDeriv_paramSection⟧: the product rule at $a = 0$, where the terms $\sum_ia_iD(\hat s_i \circ r)$ vanish. The tangent space of the product retract at $0$ lies in $\mathbb R^n \times T_0O$ ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · PullbackChartData.ofProd_mem_tangent⟧, and in the chart form of the linearization (§III.31) $\mathrm{Lin}_F(0)(\delta a, \delta u) = \mathrm{Lin}_{f + \sum r_{0,i}s_i}(0)\delta u + \sum_i\delta a_i\hat s_i(0)$ ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbationG · linAt_paramSection⟧. At $r_0 = 0$ this is the text's formula $\tilde f'(0, x)(h, u) = f'(x)u + \sum_ih_ie_i$ ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbationG · linAt_paramSection_zero⟧. The formula is the derivative at the centre of a chart and does not use $F(r_0, x) = 0$; at a general $(r_0, x)$ its first term is the linearization of $f + \sum r_{0,i}s_i$, not $f'(x)$ (Finding 165). The text's "sc¹-sections $s_i$" (p. 101 (preprint p. 75)) must be sc⁺ for Theorem 3.1.21 (preprint 3.18) to apply (Finding 166).

### III.41 Theorem 3.6.8 (1) (preprint 3.54) with the text's hypotheses: the plan and the elementary lemmas

§III.33 showed what the text's argument for property (4) of Theorem 3.6.8 (preprint 3.54) leaves open: at a zero $z_0 = \varphi(y)$ near $0$, in the chart at $x_0$, the filled operator $Dg(z_0)$ is onto (Corollary 3.5.3 (preprint 3.41)), but $f'(y) = Dg(z_0)|_{T_{z_0}O}$ is onto only if condition (3) of the filler holds at $z_0$, which Definition 3.1.4 (preprint 3.4) does not ask; and the perturbation argument of the text's Proposition 6.2.2 (preprint 6.2) gives $\rho(z_0)\Lambda_0 \subseteq \mathrm{range}\,f'(y)$ only on a neighbourhood depending on the finite-dimensional $\Lambda_0$. This section and the next two prove the surjectivity anyway, with the text's hypotheses, by a new argument found while formalizing (Findings 180–189). The text's statement is true; its proof, and that of the book's arXiv version, is incomplete.

**The plan.** By the assembly step of §III.31 it suffices to show, in the chart at $x_0$ and for every zero $z_0$ of $g$ in $O$ near $0$, that $\rho(z_0)k \in Dg(z_0)(T_{z_0}O)$ for every smooth $k \in F_\infty$; the closed range of $f'(y)$ (Proposition 3.6.5 (preprint 3.52) at $y$) does the rest. Fix $z_0$ and $k$. Suppose $u(\mu)$, $0 \le \mu < \mu_0$, is a curve through $u(0) = z_0$ of zeros of the *perturbed filler* $g + \mu\rho(r(\cdot))k$, right differentiable at $0$ into $E_1$ with velocity $\xi$. The curve lies in $O$ (the first lemma below), so $r(u(\mu)) = u(\mu)$ and $Dr(z_0)\xi = \xi$, i.e. $\xi \in T_{z_0}O$; differentiating $g(u(\mu)) = -\mu\rho(r(u(\mu)))k$ at $\mu = 0$ gives $Dg(z_0)\xi = -\rho(z_0)k$. So one curve for each pair $(z_0, k)$ suffices. It is produced by an implicit function theorem *at the nearby zero*, in the normal form of Theorem 3.4.2 (preprint 3.38): its infinite-dimensional part is Theorem 3.3.3 (preprint 3.30) for a contraction germ extended by the parameter $\mu$, applied at the centre, whose regions in the parameter $a$ do not depend on $k$ (§III.42); its finite-dimensional part is a one-sided implicit function theorem at the image of $z_0$, in the direction of an interior vector of the partial quadrant that good position provides (this section and §III.43). The set of zeros to which the argument applies is cut out by conditions on the data at $x_0$ alone. This section proves the elementary lemmas, with no sc-structure involved.

**Zeros of the perturbed filler lie in $O$** ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoLemmas · StrongRetr.mem_O_of_perturbed_zero⟧. *Statement.* Let $R$ be a strong bundle retraction on $U \triangleleft F$, $u \in U$, $\mu \in \mathbb R$, $k \in F$, and let condition (2) of Definition 3.1.4 (preprint 3.4) hold at $u$ ($g(u) = \rho(r(u))g(u)$ implies $u \in O$). If $g(u) + \mu\rho(r(u))k = 0$, then $u \in O$. *Proof.* $r(u) \in U$ ⟦Chapter3/Filler · StrongRetr.r_mem⟧ lies in $O$, $\rho(r(u))$ is linear ⟦Chapter3/Filler · StrongRetr.ρ_isLinear⟧ and idempotent on the fibre over a point of $O$ ⟦Chapter3/Filler · StrongRetr.ρ_ρ⟧; so $\rho(r(u))g(u) = -\mu\rho(r(u))\rho(r(u))k = -\mu\rho(r(u))k = g(u)$, and condition (2) applies. $\square$

**An inward direction from good position** ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoLemmas · exists_inward_of_isInGoodPosition⟧. *Statement.* Let $C = \{x : \ell_i(x) \ge 0\}$ be a partial quadrant presented by an sc-isomorphism $T$, with coordinate functionals $\ell_i = (T\cdot)_i$, $z \in C$, and $N \ne 0$ a subspace in good position to the partial quadrant $C_z = \{v : \ell_i(v) \ge 0$ for the $i$ with $\ell_i(z) = 0\}$. Then there are $v \in N$ and $\eta, t_0 > 0$ with $z + t(v + \zeta) \in C$ for all $t \in [0, t_0]$ and $|\zeta| < \eta$. *Proof.* Let $v$ be an interior point of $N \cap C_z$ in $N$ (good position). *For an active index $i$, $\ell_i(v) > 0$.* Otherwise $\ell_i(v) = 0$; for $u \in N$ and small $t$, $v \pm tu \in C_z$, so $\pm t\ell_i(u) \ge 0$ and $\ell_i$ vanishes on $N$. Let $P$ be a good complement with constant $\varepsilon$ and $p \in P$; choose $n_0 \ne 0$ in $N \cap C_z$ (it has interior and $N \ne 0$) and scale it so that $|p| \le \varepsilon|n_0|$. Good position gives $n_0 \pm p \in C_z$, so $\ell_i(\pm p) = \ell_i(n_0 \pm p) \ge 0$ and $\ell_i(p) = 0$. Then $\ell_i$ vanishes on $N \oplus P = E$, which contradicts the surjectivity of the coordinates of a presentation. *The estimates.* A finite family of positive numbers has a positive lower bound ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoLemmas · exists_pos_le_of_forall_pos_fin⟧: $\ell_i(v) \ge m$ for the active $i$ and $\ell_i(z) \ge m$ for the others. For the active $i$, $\ell_i(z + t(v + \zeta)) = t(\ell_i(v) + \ell_i(\zeta)) \ge t(m - \|\ell_i\|\,|\zeta|) \ge 0$ when $|\zeta|$ is small; for the others, $\ell_i(z + t(v + \zeta)) \ge m - t\|\ell_i\|(|v| + |\zeta|) \ge 0$ when $t$ is small. $\square$ The lemma needs $N \ne 0$: for the zero subspace good position is an empty condition, and the case $\dim K = 0$ of Theorem 3.6.8 (1) (preprint 3.54) is treated separately (§III.43). The note behind the formalization phrased it as "$N$ meets the interior of $C_z$"; the cone condition is what transports along the linear coordinate change (Finding 185).

**Inward directions absorb any vector after rescaling** ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoLemmas · inward_add_smul⟧. If $a_0 + t(\alpha + \beta) \in \widetilde C$ for $t \in [0, t_0]$ and $|\beta| < \eta$, then for every $a_1$ and $s \ge (|a_1| + 1)/\eta$ the vector $\alpha_0 = a_1 + s\alpha$ satisfies $a_0 + t(\alpha_0 + \beta) \in \widetilde C$ for $t \in [0, t_0/s]$ and $|\beta| < 1$: indeed $a_0 + t(\alpha_0 + \beta) = a_0 + ts(\alpha + (a_1 + \beta)/s)$ with $ts \le t_0$ and $|(a_1 + \beta)/s| < (|a_1| + 1)/s \le \eta$.

**A one-sided curve of zeros** ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoLemmas · exists_curve_of_inward⟧ (the finite-dimensional implicit function theorem at a boundary point, in an inward direction). *Statement.* Let $\widetilde C \subseteq P$ be convex, $\Omega \subseteq \mathbb R \times P$ open, $D = \Omega \cap (\mathbb R \times \widetilde C)$, $a_0 \in \widetilde C$ with $(0, a_0) \in \Omega$, $H : \mathbb R \times P \to Q$ of class $C^1$ on $D$, $H(0, a_0) = 0$, $D_aH(0, a_0)$ onto (the derivative within $D$, restricted to $0 \times P$), and $\alpha_0$ with $DH(0, a_0)(1, \alpha_0) = 0$ and $a_0 + t(\alpha_0 + \beta) \in \widetilde C$ for $t \in [0, t_0]$, $|\beta| < \eta$. Then there are $\mu_0 > 0$ and $a : \mathbb R \to P$ with $a(0) = a_0$, right derivative $\alpha_0$ at $0$, and $(\mu, a(\mu)) \in D$, $H(\mu, a(\mu)) = 0$ for $0 \le \mu < \mu_0$. *Proof.* *Unique derivatives.* The inward property puts a ball around $a_0 + t_0\alpha_0$ into $\widetilde C$, so $\mathbb R \times \widetilde C$ is convex with nonempty interior and $D$ has unique derivatives; derivatives within $D$ are well defined. *The contraction.* Let $S$ be a right inverse of $M_0 = D_aH(0, a_0)$ and $Z$ its range; $SM_0$ is the identity on $Z$. For $\mu \ge 0$ and $\zeta \in Z$ with $|\zeta| \le c\mu$, $c = \eta/2$, the point $(\mu, a_0 + \mu\alpha_0 + \zeta)$ lies in $D$ and close to $(0, a_0)$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoLemmas · dist_curve_le⟧, and $a_0 + \mu\alpha_0 + \zeta = a_0 + \mu(\alpha_0 + \zeta/\mu)$ is in $\widetilde C$ by the inward property ($|\zeta/\mu| \le c < \eta$). Put $T_\mu(\zeta) = \zeta - SH(\mu, a_0 + \mu\alpha_0 + \zeta)$. Its derivative in $\zeta$ is $1 - SD_aH(\mu, a_0 + \mu\alpha_0 + \zeta)|_Z$, which tends to $1 - SM_0|_Z = 0$ as $(\mu, \zeta) \to 0$ by the continuity of $DH$ at $(0, a_0)$; the mean value inequality on the convex ball makes $T_\mu$ a $\tfrac12$-contraction for small $\mu$. The differentiability of $H$ at $(0, a_0)$ in the direction $(1, \alpha_0)$, with $DH(0, a_0)(1, \alpha_0) = 0$, gives $|T_\mu(0)| = |SH(\mu, a_0 + \mu\alpha_0)| = o(\mu)$; so $|T_\mu(0)| \le c\mu/2$ for small $\mu$ and $T_\mu$ maps the ball of radius $c\mu$ into itself. *The curve.* The fixed point $\zeta(\mu)$ has $SH(\mu, a_0 + \mu\alpha_0 + \zeta(\mu)) = 0$, hence $H = 0$ there ($S$ is injective), and $|\zeta(\mu)| \le 2|T_\mu(0)| = o(\mu)$; so $a(\mu) = a_0 + \mu\alpha_0 + \zeta(\mu)$ has right derivative $\alpha_0$ at $0$. $\square$

**Two facts on right derivatives.** If $k$ is continuous at $0$ from the right, $\mu \mapsto \mu k(\mu)$ has the right derivative $k(0)$ at $0$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoLemmas · hasDerivWithinAt_smul_right_of_continuousWithinAt⟧; and right derivatives at $0$ on $[0, \infty)$ are unique ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoLemmas · eq_of_hasDerivWithinAt_Ici⟧.

### III.42 The λ-extended contraction germ and the perturbation in the normal form

Throughout, the normal form is the data of Theorem 3.4.2 (preprint 3.38) from §III.20 ⟦Chapter3/LocalSolvability · ShiftedBasicData⟧: a germ $g : O(\widetilde C \oplus W, 0) \to Q \oplus W$, sc-smooth, whose index raise $g^1 = (G_1, w - B(a, w))$ is a basic germ on the shifted structures; at the chart at $x_0$ it is obtained from the stability data of the graph description by Theorem 3.4.2 (preprint 3.38) ⟦Chapter3/LocalSolvability · StabilityData.toShiftedBasicData⟧. (In this section $g$ and $e$ denote the normal form and its perturbation; the filler of the chart at $x_0$ returns in §III.43.) The norms of $w$ below are those of $W_{m+1}$, the shifted level $m$.

**The perturbation on the shifted structure.** For an sc⁺-perturbation $e : O(\widetilde C \oplus W, 0) \to (Q \oplus W)^1$, its $W$-component on level $1$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoGerm · perturbEW⟧ is sc-smooth on the index-raised germ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoGerm · ShiftedBasicData.eW_isScSmoothGerm⟧, by Proposition 1.2.2 (preprint 1.17) for germs applied to the level-raised map ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoGerm · ShiftedBasicData.levelMap_e_isScSmoothGerm⟧ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoGerm · ShiftedBasicData.eW_eq_on⟧. Its level maps are those of $e$ read on the shifted levels ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoGerm · ShiftedBasicData.levelB_eW⟧.

**Contraction regions that do not depend on the perturbation** ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoGerm · ShiftedBasicData.Radii⟧. A system of regions consists of radii $\nu_m$ in $P$ and $\rho_m$ in $W_{m+1}$ such that for $a \in \widetilde C$, $|a| < \nu_m$ and $|w|, |w'| < \rho_m$: $(a, w)$ lies in the domain of $g^1$ on level $m$, $|B(a, w) - B(a, w')| \le \tfrac14|w - w'|$, and $|B(a, 0)| \le \rho_m/8$; $\nu$ decreasing ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoGerm · ShiftedBasicData.Radii.ν_anti_le⟧ and the $\rho_m$ compatible with the inclusions. *They exist below any prescribed radii* $\delta_m$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoGerm · ShiftedBasicData.exists_radii⟧: on each level, Definition 3.3.1 (preprint 3.28) with $\varepsilon = \tfrac14$ gives a radius on which $B(a, \cdot)$ is a $\tfrac14$-contraction, and since $B(0, 0) = 0$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoGerm · ShiftedBasicData.levelB_B_zero⟧ and $B$ is continuous at $0$ on each level, $|B(a, 0)| \le \rho_m/8$ for $|a|$ small; the radii are then shrunk level by level to be monotone and below $\delta_m$. Nothing here involves a perturbation.

**The λ-extended germ.** On $(\mathbb R \times \widetilde C) \oplus W^1$ take the germ of neighbourhoods $\{((\mu, a), w) \mid (a, w) \in U^1_m\}$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoGerm · ShiftedBasicData.perturbGerm⟧, with the projections forgetting $\mu$ and keeping $\mu$ as sc-operators ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoGerm · perturbProj⟧ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoGerm · perturbMuProj⟧ and $\mathbb R \times \widetilde C$ quadrant-like ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoGerm · isQuadrantLike_perturb⟧, and put
$$B_\mu(a, w) = B(a, w) - \mu\,Pe(a, w)$$
⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoGerm · ShiftedBasicData.perturbB⟧, $P$ the projection onto $W$, with $B_\mu(0, 0) = 0$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoGerm · ShiftedBasicData.perturbB_zero⟧. It is sc-smooth on the germ: $B$ composed with the projection, minus the scalar product of the $\mu$-coordinate with $Pe$ composed with the projection, by the product rule with a scalar factor of §III.40 ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoGerm · ShiftedBasicData.perturbB_isScSmoothGerm⟧ ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · IsScSmooth.smul⟧; its level maps are $B_m - \mu(Pe)_m$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoGerm · ShiftedBasicData.levelB_perturbB⟧. Given constants $c_m \ge 0$ with $|Pe(a, w)| \le c_m$ and $|Pe(a, w) - Pe(a, w')| \le c_m|w - w'|$ on the regions, define the $\mu$-radii $\mu_m = \min_{j \le m}\min(\tfrac14, \rho_j/8)/(c_j + 1)$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoGerm · ShiftedBasicData.perturbMu⟧, positive and decreasing ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoGerm · ShiftedBasicData.perturbMu_pos⟧ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoGerm · ShiftedBasicData.perturbMu_anti⟧, with $\mu_mc_m \le \tfrac14$ and $\mu_mc_m \le \rho_m/8$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoGerm · ShiftedBasicData.perturbMu_mul_le⟧.

**Lemma: $B_\mu$ is a contraction germ in the working form** ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoGerm · ShiftedBasicData.perturbContraction⟧, with the parameter regions $(-\mu_m, \mu_m) \times \{|a| < \nu_m\}$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoGerm · ShiftedBasicData.mem_perturbContraction_N_iff⟧, the open balls $\{|w| < \rho_m\}$ and the closed balls $\{|w| \le \rho_m/2\}$ (Definition 3.A, §III.7). *The contraction*: $|B_\mu(a, w) - B_\mu(a, w')| \le (\tfrac14 + |\mu|c_m)|w - w'| \le \tfrac12|w - w'|$. *The closed balls are invariant*: for $|w| \le \rho_m/2$, $|B_\mu(a, w)| \le |B(a, w) - B(a, 0)| + |B(a, 0)| + |\mu||Pe(a, w)| \le \tfrac14\cdot\tfrac{\rho_m}2 + \tfrac{\rho_m}8 + |\mu|c_m \le \tfrac{\rho_m}2$. The regions in $a$ and $w$ are those of $B$ and do not depend on $e$; only the $\mu$-intervals do. The general construction of a working form from Definition 3.3.1 (preprint 3.28) ⟦Chapter3/GermIFT · toContractionGerm⟧ is *not* used: its radii in $a$ would depend on $e$, and the finite-dimensional step needs the nearby zero inside the level-$3$ region for every perturbation (Finding 182).

**Theorem 3.3.3 (preprint 3.30) for $B_\mu$** ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoGerm · ShiftedBasicData.perturbContraction_isSc1GermWith⟧. The solution germ $\delta_\mu(\mu, a)$ is sc¹ on the germ re-indexed by one level, $((-\mu_{m+1}, \mu_{m+1}) \times \{|a| < \nu_{m+1}\}) \cap (\mathbb R \times \widetilde C)$, with the derivative family of the proof of Theorem 3.3.3 (preprint 3.30) ⟦Chapter3/ContractionGerm · theorem_3_3_3⟧. *At $\mu = 0$* ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoGerm · ShiftedBasicData.perturbContraction_sol_zero⟧: a fixed point $w \in W_1$ of $B(a, \cdot)$ with $|w| < \rho_0$, $|a| < \nu_0$, $a \in \widetilde C$, is $\delta_\mu(0, a)$, by the uniqueness of the fixed point in the open ball of level $0$.

**The perturbation in the normal form.** Now the chart at $x_0$ returns: the strong bundle retraction $R$ on $U \triangleleft F$, the conjugation $\Psi$ covering $\psi$ with inverse $\Psi'$, and the linear maps $\mathrm{glue}$, $\mathrm{split}$, $\mathrm{fiber}$ of the coordinate change of Theorem 3.4.2 (preprint 3.38) (§III.18). The perturbation $u \mapsto \rho(r(u))k$ of the filler, pushed to the normal form, is $\hat e_k(y) = \Xi(y, k)$ with the *joint* map
$$\Xi(y, \kappa) = \mathrm{fiber}\Big(\Psi[1]\big(\psi^{-1}(\mathrm{glue}\,y),\ (R[1](r(\psi^{-1}(\mathrm{glue}\,y)), \kappa))_F\big)\Big)_{F'}$$
⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoXi · xiMap⟧ on $\psi(U) \oplus F^1$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoXi · xiDom⟧ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoXi · mem_xiDom⟧, a relatively open set ($\mathrm{glue}^{-1}(U')$ is relatively open in the quadrant of the normal form) ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoXi · glue₀_preimage_relOpen⟧ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoXi · xiDom_relOpen⟧. *$\Xi$ is sc-smooth into the index-raised target* ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoXi · xiMap_isScSmooth⟧: it is the composite of the linear $\mathrm{glue}$, $\psi^{-1} \times 1$, the map $(u, \kappa) \mapsto (u, (R[1](r(u), \kappa))_F)$, the strong bundle map $\Psi[1]$ on the index-raised bundle (Definition 2.6.1 (preprint 2.47)), a projection and the linear $\mathrm{fiber}$. *$\Xi$ is linear in $\kappa$* ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoXi · xiMap_smul⟧ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoXi · xiMap_zero⟧, also on every level ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoXi · levelMap_xiMap_smul⟧, since $R$ and $\Psi$ are linear on the fibres. For a smooth $k$ with level-$1$ representative $k_1$, $\hat e_k = \Xi(\cdot, k_1)$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoXi · ehat⟧ is sc-smooth into the index-raised target ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoXi · ehat_isScSmooth⟧ (a constant map with smooth value is sc-smooth ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoXi · isScSmooth_const_of_smooth⟧), an sc⁺-perturbation on the germ of Theorem 3.4.2 (preprint 3.38) ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoXi · pushGerm₀_U_subset⟧ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoXi · ehat_isScSmoothGerm⟧, with level maps $(\hat e_k)_m(y) = \Xi_m(y, k_{m+1})$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoXi · levelMap_ehat⟧.

**Lemma: bounds uniform in $k$** ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoXi · exists_xi_bounds⟧. *For every level $m$ there are $\delta > 0$ and $M \ge 0$ such that for $|a| < \delta$, $|\kappa|_{m+2} < \delta$ and a convex set $S$ of $w \in W_{m+1}$ with $|w| < \delta$ on $S$ (inside the domain of $\Xi$ on level $m + 1$): $|\Xi_m((a, w), \kappa)| \le 1$ and $|\Xi_m((a, w), \kappa) - \Xi_m((a, w'), \kappa)| \le M|w - w'|$ on $S$.* *Proof.* By Proposition 1.2.3 (preprint 1.18) ⟦Chapter1/Levels · IsScSmooth.contDiffOn_levels⟧, $\Xi$ is $C^1$ from level $m + 1$ to level $m$ of its index-raised target, which is the shifted level $m$ of the normal form: on the shifted structure the perturbation is $C^1$ on every level, which is what contraction estimates on the shifted levels need. *This is where the index raise of Theorem 3.4.2 (preprint 3.38) is used* (Finding 183). $\Xi_m$ vanishes at $\kappa = 0$, so by continuity of $\Xi_m$ and of its derivative at $0$ both are small on a small ball, and the mean value inequality on the convex $S$ gives the Lipschitz bound. $\square$ *The constants for $\hat e_k$* ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoXi · exists_xi_radii_bounds⟧: there are radii $\delta_m > 0$, depending on $\Xi$ only, such that for every system of regions with $\nu_m, \rho_m \le \delta_m$ and every smooth $k$, the constants $c_m = (2|k|_{m+2}/\delta_m)(M_m + 1)$ bound $(P\hat e_k)_m$ and its Lipschitz constant in $w$ on the regions. The proof is the *scaling* $\Xi_m(y, \kappa) = (2|\kappa|/\delta_m)\,\Xi_m(y, (\delta_m/2|\kappa|)\kappa)$, which needs only the linearity of $\Xi$ in $\kappa$; the note behind the formalization used the linearity of $D_y\Xi$ in $\kappa$ instead.

### III.43 The curve of perturbed zeros, the surjectivity in the chart at $x_0$, and Theorem 3.6.8 (1) (preprint 3.54)

**The reduction at a nearby zero** (in the normal form, with a perturbation $e$, regions $R$ and constants $c$ as in §III.42). Let $z = (a_0, w_0)$ be a level-$1$ zero of $g$ in the domain of the germ with $a_0 \in \widetilde C$, $|a_0| < \nu_3$ and $|w_0|_1 < \rho_0$. Then $w_0$ is a fixed point of $B(a_0, \cdot)$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoCurve · ShiftedBasicData.B_eq_of_zero⟧ (because $g^1 = (G_1, w - B)$), so $w_0 = \delta_\mu(0, a_0)$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoCurve · ShiftedBasicData.sol_zero_eq⟧. On the domain $D_3 = (-\mu_3, \mu_3) \times (\{|a| < \nu_3\} \cap \widetilde C)$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoCurve · ShiftedBasicData.curveDom⟧, which has unique derivatives ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoCurve · ShiftedBasicData.uniqueDiffOn_curveDom⟧, put $(\mu, a) \mapsto (a, \delta_\mu(\mu, a)) \in E_1$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoCurve · ShiftedBasicData.curveLift⟧ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoCurve · ShiftedBasicData.curveLift_mem⟧ and
$$\widetilde G(\mu, a) = (g + \mu\,\iota e)\big(a, \delta_\mu(\mu, a)\big)$$
⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoCurve · ShiftedBasicData.curveMap⟧. *Its $W$-component vanishes* ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoCurve · ShiftedBasicData.curveMap_snd⟧: $\delta_\mu(\mu, a)$ is the fixed point of $B_\mu(a, \cdot)$, and the $W$-component of $g + \mu\iota e$ is $w - B_\mu(a, w)$ (read on level $1$, ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoCurve · ShiftedBasicData.snd_g_toBase⟧). So the whole equation is carried by $H = (\widetilde G)_Q$. *$\widetilde G$ is $C^1$* on $D_3$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoCurve · ShiftedBasicData.contDiffOn_curveMap⟧: $\delta_\mu$ is $C^1$ into $W_2$ by Theorem 3.3.3 (preprint 3.30) on the re-indexed germ (Proposition 1.2.1 (preprint 1.16) on level $1$), and $g$, $e$ are $C^1$ from level $1$ to level $0$. *Its derivative at $(0, a_0)$* ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoCurve · ShiftedBasicData.hasFDerivWithinAt_curveMap⟧ is
$$D\widetilde G(0, a_0)(\dot\mu, \alpha) = Dg(z)\big(\alpha,\ \iota D\delta_\mu(0, a_0)(\dot\mu, \alpha)\big) + \dot\mu\,\iota e(z)$$
⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoCurve · ShiftedBasicData.solD⟧ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoCurve · ShiftedBasicData.curveDeriv⟧: the chain rule, with the derivative of the solution germ on level $1$ ⟦Chapter3/GermLevels · IsSc1GermWith.hasFDerivWithinAt_level⟧, the derivative of $g$ at $z$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoCurve · ShiftedBasicData.hasFDerivWithinAt_Dg'⟧, and the product rule for $\mu\,\iota e$, whose derivative at $\mu = 0$ in the base directions vanishes. Its $W$-component vanishes too ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoCurve · ShiftedBasicData.curveDeriv_snd⟧.

**Lemma: $D_aH(0, a_0)$ is onto, by a dimension count** ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoCurve · ShiftedBasicData.surjective_DH_and_ker_eq⟧. *Statement.* Let moreover $\ker Dg(z)$ be finite-dimensional of dimension $\dim P - \dim Q$. Then $M_0 = D_aH(0, a_0) : P \to Q$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoCurve · ShiftedBasicData.DH⟧ is onto, and $\ker Dg(z)$ is the graph of $L = \iota D_a\delta_\mu(0, a_0)$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoCurve · ShiftedBasicData.solL⟧ over $\ker M_0$. *Proof.* At $\dot\mu = 0$ the derivative formula reads $Dg(z)(\alpha, L\alpha) = (M_0\alpha, 0)$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoCurve · ShiftedBasicData.curveDeriv_inr⟧ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoCurve · ShiftedBasicData.Dg'_graph⟧, so the graph of $L$ over $\ker M_0$ lies in $\ker Dg(z)$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoCurve · ShiftedBasicData.map_graph_le_ker⟧. The graph map is injective, so $\dim\ker M_0 \le \dim\ker Dg(z) = \dim P - \dim Q$; rank–nullity gives $\operatorname{rank}M_0 \ge \dim Q$, so $M_0$ is onto, and then $\dim\ker M_0 = \dim\ker Dg(z)$ and the inclusion is an equality. $\square$ The obvious route, eliminating the $W$-block with $(1 - D_2B(z))^{-1}$, needs $\|D_2B(z)\| \le \tfrac12$ on the level where $Dg(z)$ is known to be onto, i.e. the surjectivity on the shifted structure; the dimension count uses only the kernel dimension on level $0$ (Finding 184).

**Proposition: the curve of perturbed zeros** ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoCurve · ShiftedBasicData.exists_perturbed_curve⟧. *Statement.* Under the hypotheses of the lemma, let $v \in \ker Dg(z)$ with $a_0 + t(v_1 + \beta) \in \widetilde C$ for $t \in [0, t_0]$, $|\beta| < \eta$ ($v_1$ the $P$-component). Then there are $\mu_0 > 0$ and a curve $u'(\mu) = (a(\mu), \delta_\mu(\mu, a(\mu)))$, $0 \le \mu < \mu_0$, on level $2$, in the domain of the germ with $a(\mu) \in \widetilde C$, with $u'(0) = z$ (on level $1$), right differentiable at $0$ as a map into $E_2$, and $(g + \mu\iota e)(u'(\mu)) = 0$. *Proof.* (i) By the lemma, $M_0$ is onto and $v_1 \in \ker M_0$ (as $v = (v_1, Lv_1)$). (ii) Choose $a_1$ with $M_0a_1 = -D_\mu H(0, a_0)$, and $\alpha_0 = a_1 + sv_1$ with $s$ from the rescaling lemma of §III.41: $\alpha_0$ is inward at $a_0$ (with $|\beta| < 1$) and $DH(0, a_0)(1, \alpha_0) = D_\mu H(0, a_0) + M_0a_1 + sM_0v_1 = 0$. (iii) $H$ is $C^1$ on $D_3$ with $H(0, a_0) = 0$, and $\widetilde C$ is convex; the one-sided curve lemma (§III.41) on $\Omega = $ the open region of level $3$ gives $a(\mu)$ with right derivative $\alpha_0$ and $H(\mu, a(\mu)) = 0$. (iv) The level-$1$ map $(\mu, a) \mapsto \delta_\mu(\mu, a) \in W_2$ is differentiable at $(0, a_0)$ within $D_3$ (Theorem 3.3.3 (preprint 3.30), ⟦Chapter3/GermLevels · IsSc1GermWith.hasFDerivWithinAt_level⟧), so by the chain rule for $\mu \mapsto (\mu, a(\mu))$ the curve $u'$ is right differentiable at $0$ into $E_2$. (v) At $\mu = 0$, $\delta_\mu(0, a_0) = w_0$; and $(g + \mu\iota e)(u'(\mu)) = \widetilde G(\mu, a(\mu)) = (H(\mu, a(\mu)), 0) = 0$. $\square$

**The normal form at a nearby point.** For the chart at $x_0$: at every level-$1$ point $y$ of $\psi(U_1)$ in the germ, $Dg(y) = \mathrm{fiber} \circ D(h + t)(\mathrm{glue}\,y) \circ \mathrm{glue}$ for the normal form $g$ of $h + t$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOnto · StabilityData.scDerivGerm_pushMap₀_at⟧ (the coordinate change is linear), and the kernel of $\mathrm{fiber} \circ T \circ \mathrm{glue}$ is $\mathrm{split}(\ker T)$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOnto · StabilityData.ker_fiber₀_comp_glue₀⟧, $\mathrm{split}$ being injective ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOnto · StabilityData.split₀_injective⟧. Corollary 3.4.3 (preprint 3.39) is not used: the facts at the nearby zero come from the chart at $x_0$ through these linear maps (Finding 187).

**Theorem A: the surjectivity in the chart at $x_0$** ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoG · theorem_3_6_8_chart_onto⟧. *Statement.* Take the data of the graph description in the chart at $x_0$ (§III.29, ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyGraphG · theorem_3_6_8_chart_graph⟧): the strong bundle retraction $R = (r, \rho)$ on $U$, a filling germ $g$ of $\mathbf f$ on $G_g$ with a derivative family $Dg$, $\mathbf f(0) = 0$, the conjugation $\Psi$ covering $\psi$, $\psi(0) = 0$, the stability data on a germ that $\psi'$ maps into $G_g$ level by level (inside $G''$, §III.48) with the basic germ $h'$, $D(h' + \iota t)(0)$ onto with kernel $K$ in good position, a closed good complement $Y$ of $K$, the $C^1$ graph $\sigma : V' \to Y$ with $\sigma(0) = 0$, $D\sigma(0) = 0$ and its $C^k$-lifts, an open $W_o$ on which $h' + \iota t$ is the push-forward of $g$, an open $N_2 \ni 0$ on which $\psi$ maps into the germ and into $U' \cap W_o$, and on which $\psi$ maps the zeros of $g$ in $O$ onto the graph. Assume condition (2) of Definition 3.1.4 (preprint 3.4) on $U \cap N_2$ and $K \ne 0$. Then there is an open $N_4 \subseteq N_2$ containing $0$ such that for every zero $z_0 \in O \cap N_4$ of $g$, every level-$1$ point $z_{0,1}$ over $z_0$ and every smooth $k$, there is $x \in T_{z_0}O = Dr(z_{0,1})E_0$ with $Dg(z_{0,1})x = \rho(z_0)k$. *Proof*, in the order of the Lean:

1. *Data at $x_0$, independent of $k$.* Lemma 3.6.9 (preprint 3.55) on the graph with its radius $\rho_2$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTFull · lemma_3_6_9_relOpen⟧ (§III.32); the kernel at the nearby zeros on an open $N_3 \subseteq N_2$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyKernelG · theorem_3_6_8_chart_kernel⟧ (§III.32); the normal form $\mathcal D$ of Theorem 3.4.2 (preprint 3.38) ⟦Chapter3/LocalSolvability · StabilityData.toShiftedBasicData⟧; the radii $\delta_m$ of the bounds of $\Xi$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoXi · exists_xi_radii_bounds⟧ and contraction regions below them ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoGerm · ShiftedBasicData.exists_radii⟧; and the radius $r_{42}$ of Lemma 3.5.5 (preprint 3.42) for the bound $\rho_0$ ⟦Chapter3/LocalSolvability · StabilityData.lemma_3_5_5⟧: zeros of the normal form on level $0$ smaller than $r_{42}$ have their $W$-component on level $1$, smaller than $\rho_0$ there.
2. *The neighbourhood.* Let $\pi$ be the projection onto $K$ along $Y$ (a topological complement: $Y$ closed, $K$ finite-dimensional), $c_s = \|\mathrm{split}\| + 1$, and $r' = \min(\rho_2/(\|\pi\| + 1), r_{42}/c_s, \nu_3/c_s)$. By the continuity of $\psi$ on $U$, $\psi^{-1}(B(0, r')) \cap U = O_4 \cap U$ for an open $O_4 \ni 0$; put $N_4 = N_3 \cap O_4$. Nothing here depends on $k$.
3. *At a zero $z_0 \in O \cap N_4$.* The kernel theorem at $z_0$ gives $v \in V'$ with $\psi(z_0) = v + \sigma(v)$, the level-$1$ point $x_1$ over it, $D(h' + \iota t)(x_1)$ onto and Fredholm of index $\dim P - \dim Q$, its kernel $N_v$, and $\ker Dg(z_{0,1}) = D\psi^{-1}(x_1)N_v \subseteq T_{z_0}O$. Since $|v| = |\pi(\psi(z_0))| \le \|\pi\|r' < \rho_2$, Lemma 3.6.9 (preprint 3.55) at $v$ gives $x_1 \in C'$ and $N_v$ in good position to $C'_{x_1}$.
4. *The zero of the normal form.* $z = \mathrm{split}(x_1)$ lies in the germ on level $1$, with $a_0 = (x_1)_P \in \widetilde C$; $|\mathrm{split}\,\psi(z_0)| < c_sr'$, so $|a_0| < \nu_3$, and Lemma 3.5.5 (preprint 3.42) gives $|w_0|_1 < \rho_0$; $g$ vanishes at $z$ because $h' + \iota t$ vanishes at the graph point.
5. *Its kernel.* $Dg(z) = \mathrm{fiber} \circ D(h' + \iota t)(x_1) \circ \mathrm{glue}$, so $\ker Dg(z) = \mathrm{split}(N_v)$, finite-dimensional of dimension $\dim\ker D(h' + \iota t)(x_1) = \dim P - \dim Q$ (for a surjective Fredholm operator the kernel dimension is the index, ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFT · finrank_ker_eq_fredholmIndex_of_surjective⟧).
6. *The inward direction.* $N_v \ne 0$: its dimension is $\dim P - \dim Q$, which is also $\dim K$ ($D(h' + \iota t)(0)$ is onto and Fredholm of that index, ⟦Chapter3/SolutionLinearization · StabilityData.isFredholm_Dfs⟧), and $K \ne 0$. The inward lemma at $x_1$ (§III.41) gives $v_v \in N_v$ with $x_1 + t(v_v + \zeta) \in C'$; then $\mathrm{split}\,v_v \in \ker Dg(z)$, and its $P$-component is inward at $a_0$ in the quadrant of the normal form for $|\beta| < \eta/(\|\mathrm{glue}\| + 1)$, since $\mathrm{glue}(\beta, 0)$ has norm at most $\|\mathrm{glue}\|\,|\beta|$.
7. *The perturbation and the curve.* Take the level-$1$ representative $k_1$ of $k$; $\hat e_k$ is an sc⁺-perturbation (§III.42) with constants $c_m$ on the regions; the proposition above gives $\mu_0$ and the curve $u'$ of zeros of $g + \mu\iota\hat e_k$ through $z$.
8. *Back in the chart.* $u(\mu) = \psi^{-1}(\mathrm{glue}\,u'(\mu))$ on level $1$: $\psi^{-1}$ is $C^1$ from level $2$ to level $1$ (Proposition 1.2.3 (preprint 1.18), ⟦Chapter1/Levels · IsScSmooth.contDiffOn_levels⟧), so $u$ is right differentiable at $0$ with some velocity $\xi_1$, $u(0) = z_{0,1}$, and its base points $u_\mu = \psi^{-1}(\mathrm{glue}\,\iota u'(\mu))$ lie in $U$.
9. *The perturbed equation.* By continuity at $\mu = 0$, $\mathrm{glue}\,\iota u'(\mu) \in W_o$ and $u_\mu \in N_2$ for $0 \le \mu < \mu_2$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOnto · exists_Ico_of_eventually_nhdsWithin_Ici⟧. There the push-forward identity of $g$, the description of $\hat e_k$ as the $\Psi$-fibre image of $\rho(r(u_\mu))k$ transported by the linear $\mathrm{fiber}$, and the injectivity of the fibre maps of $\Psi$ turn $(g + \mu\iota\hat e_k)(u'(\mu)) = 0$ into $g(u_\mu) + \mu\rho(r(u_\mu))k = 0$.
10. *Tangency.* By the first lemma of §III.41 and condition (2) on $N_2$, $u_\mu \in O$.
11. *Differentiation.* $r$ is sc¹ on $U$, and $g$ is an sc¹-germ on $G_g$; the curve $u(\mu)$ stays on level $1$ of $G_g$, because $\mathrm{glue}\,\iota u'(\mu)$ lies on level $1$ of the germ of the stability data and $\psi'_1$ maps that into $(G_g)_1$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermShrink · ConjugationData.levelMap_inclK_mem_of_subset_shrinkGerm⟧. So both are differentiable from level $1$ to level $0$ at $z_{0,1}$ along the curve, $g$ by the chain rule within $(G_g)_1$ with derivative $Dg(z_{0,1})$ (with $g$ sc-smooth on $U$ no such condition was needed). Along the curve, $r(u_\mu) = u_\mu$, so the right derivatives at $0$ agree, $Dr(z_{0,1})\xi_0 = \xi_0$ ($\xi_0 = \iota\xi_1$; uniqueness of right derivatives, §III.41): $\xi_0 \in T_{z_0}O$. The map $\mu \mapsto \rho(r(u_\mu))k$ is continuous at $0$ ($R$ is continuous on level $0$), so $\mu \mapsto \mu\rho(r(u_\mu))k$ has the right derivative $\rho(r(z_0))k = \rho(z_0)k$; differentiating $g(u_\mu) = -\mu\rho(r(u_\mu))k$ gives $Dg(z_{0,1})\xi_0 = -\rho(z_0)k$. Take $x = -\xi_0$. $\square$

*Where the hypotheses enter.* Tameness and good position at $x_0$ through the graph description, Corollary 3.5.3 (preprint 3.41) and Lemma 3.6.9 (preprint 3.55) (steps 1, 3) and the inward direction at boundary zeros (step 6); condition (2) of the filler through the tangency (step 10); $K \ne 0$ for the inward direction. The sc-Fredholm property at $y$ is not used here; it enters only through the closed range in the assembly step. Condition (3) of the filler at the nearby zeros is not used. *Why there is no uniformity problem.* The only $k$-dependent quantities are the constants $c_m$ and the $\mu$-intervals; $N_4$ is fixed in step 2 before $k$ is chosen. The perturbation argument of §III.33 applied the implicit function theorem at $x_0$ to the $\Lambda_0$-extended problem, whose neighbourhood depends on $\Lambda_0$; here it is applied at the nearby zero (Finding 181).

**Theorem 3.6.8 (1) (preprint 3.54) with the text's hypotheses** ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoG · theorem_3_6_8_one'⟧. *Statement.* Let $f$ be an sc-Fredholm section of a strong bundle whose charts have tame base retractions, $f(x_0) = 0$, and $(f, x_0)$ in good position. Then there is an open $V_x \ni x_0$ such that $(f, y)$ is in good position at every zero $y \in V_x$. *Proof.* Destructure the good position at $x_0$: the centred chart $c$, the filling germ $g$ with its derivative family, the sc⁺-germ, the conjugation, shrunk to $G''$ (§III.48), and the good position in the chart. Tameness gives $(1 - Dr(0))E \subseteq E_0$ by Proposition 2.5.3 (preprint 2.35) ⟦Chapter2/TameRetracts · proposition_2_5_3⟧; the graph description in the chart ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyGraphG · theorem_3_6_8_chart_graph⟧ and the kernel at the nearby zeros ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyKernelG · theorem_3_6_8_chart_kernel⟧ give $N_1 \supseteq N_2 \supseteq N_3$. A zero $y$ of $f$ in the chart domain with $\varphi(y) \in N_2$ is a zero of $g$ ($\mathbf f = g$ on $O \cap N_1$). *Case $K = 0$.* Put $V_x = \varphi^{-1}(N_2)$ in the chart domain. A zero $y \in V_x$ maps by $\psi\varphi$ onto a graph point $v + \sigma(v)$ with $v \in K = 0$, so $\psi(\varphi(y)) = \sigma(0) = 0 = \psi(0)$, $\varphi(y) = 0$ (as $\psi^{-1}\psi = 1$ on $U$), and $y = x_0$ by the injectivity of the chart: the only zero is $x_0$, in good position by hypothesis. *Case $K \ne 0$.* Theorem A gives $N_4$ (condition (2) on $U \cap N_2$ is the filler's, §III.29); put $V_x = \varphi^{-1}(N_3 \cap N_4)$ in the chart domain. For a zero $y \in V_x$: $z_0 = \varphi(y) \in O$ is a zero of $g$; the kernel theorem gives the level-$1$ point $z_{0,1}$, $\ker Dg(z_{0,1}) \subseteq T_{z_0}O$ and its good position inside $T_{z_0}O$; $y$ is smooth ⟦Chapter3/TameGermsG · StrongBundle.IsScFredholmSection.mem_smoothSet_of_isZeroAt⟧, so $(f, y)$ is an sc-Fredholm germ; $\mathbf f = g$ on $O$ near $z_0$. The assembly step in its germ form ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbySurjG · StrongBundle.isGoodPositionGermTame_of_chart_dense⟧ (§III.31; whole-chart ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · StrongBundle.isGoodPositionGerm_of_chart_dense⟧) applies at $z_{0,1} \in (G_g)_1$ with (a) from Theorem A — $\rho(z_0)\hat k$ in the range of $Dg(z_{0,1})|_{T_{z_0}O}$ for every smooth $\hat k$ — and (b) from the kernel theorem, $\ker Dg(z_{0,1}) \cap T_{z_0}O = \ker Dg(z_{0,1})$. $\square$ **No hypothesis is added**: the statement has exactly the hypotheses of the kernel theorem of §III.32 plus the sc-Fredholm property (Finding 189).
