**Theorems 3.6.8, 3.1.26 and 3.1.22 (preprint 3.54, 3.23 and 3.19) with the text's hypotheses.** Theorem 3.6.8 (1) and (2) (preprint 3.54) ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoG · theorem_3_6_8'⟧: (1) as above on $V_x$, and (2), the regular data of Definition 3.2.1 (preprint 3.25) at $x_0$ of a dimension $d$ that every nearby datum shares, by §III.37 ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTTheoremsG · theorem_3_6_8_two⟧ fed with (1). Theorem 3.1.26 (preprint 3.23) ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoG · theorem_3_1_26'⟧: (1) supplies the open set of nearby good position that the boundary case from the nearby good position needs ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTTheoremsG · theorem_3_1_26_of_nearby⟧, and every zero of the resulting $U$ is in good position. Theorem 3.1.22 (preprint 3.19) ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoG · theorem_3_1_22'⟧: Theorem 3.1.26 (preprint 3.23) and Lemma 2.4.6 (preprint 2.26), $d_S \le d_X = 0$ ⟦Chapter2/SubMPolyfold · lemma_2_4_6⟧. The first forms under the filler condition at the nearby zeros (§§III.33, III.37) are kept; that condition is now a theorem (§III.45).

### III.44 The dimension of the solution set is the Fredholm index, and the index is locally constant

Theorem 3.1.22 (preprint 3.19) says that the solution set is a manifold "whose dimension agrees with the Fredholm index", and the proof of Theorem 3.6.8 (preprint 3.54) takes $d = \dim\ker f'(x)$. In the statements of §§III.34–III.37 the dimension was existential. This section identifies it, at every zero and in every chart, and deduces the local constancy of the index along the solution set (Findings 170–171).

**"$f'(y)$ is onto, Fredholm, of index $d$", chart-intrinsically** ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndex · StrongBundle.IsSurjLinOfIndex⟧: in every strong bundle chart around $y$ compatible with the atlas, at the level-$1$ point $z$ over $\varphi(y)$, every corestriction $L : T_zO \to K_z$ of the linearization $\mathrm{Lin}(z)$ (§III.31) is onto and Fredholm with $\mathrm{ind}\,L = \dim\ker L = d$. "$f'(y)$ is onto" alone ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndex · StrongBundle.IsLinSurjAt⟧: in *some* chart, $\mathrm{Lin}(z)$ maps onto the fibre $\{k \mid (\varphi(y), k) \in K\}$; by the chart independence it is then onto in every chart.

**Proposition 3.6.5 (preprint 3.52) in every chart** ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndexG · StrongBundle.linAt_index_of_conjugationData⟧. *Statement.* Let $f$ be an sc-smooth section, $x_0$ a zero, $c$ a strong bundle chart centred at $x_0$ with a filling germ $g$ of $\mathbf f$ and a derivative family $Dg$ on a germ inside $U$, an sc⁺-germ $s$ with $s(0) = g(0)$, and conjugation data $d_c$ of $g - s$ to a basic germ ⟦Chapter3/FredholmGermIndex · ConjugationData⟧ with finite-dimensional spaces $P_n$, $Q$. Then in every strong bundle chart $c'$ around $x_0$, at the level-$1$ point $z'$ over $\varphi'(x_0)$, every corestriction $L'$ of $\mathrm{Lin}'(z')$ is Fredholm of index $\dim P_n - \dim Q$, and onto if the germ is in good position at $Dg(0)$ in $c$. *Proof.* Proposition 3.6.5 (preprint 3.52) in $c$ (§III.30, ⟦Chapter3/FredholmGermIndexG · proposition_3_6_5_local_index⟧): $Tf(0)$ is Fredholm of index $\dim P_n - \dim Q$. $Tf(0) = Dg(0)|_{T_0O}$ is $\mathrm{Lin}(0)$ by the bridge for germs at the centre, which lies in every germ ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationChartsG · IsFillingGerm.fillerRestrict_eq_linAt⟧ (whole-chart: ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · IsFilledVersion.fillerRestrict_eq_linAt⟧), up to the identification of the fibres $\rho(0)F$ and $\rho(\iota0)F$, which are equal subspaces but different types in Lean ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndex · exists_rangeS_ρCLM_equiv⟧. The chart independence (§III.31, ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · StrongBundle.linAt_chart_independence⟧) carries the Fredholm property, the index and the surjectivity from $c$ at $0$ to $c'$ at $z'$; the four spaces are complete as ranges of idempotents ⟦Chapter3/FillerLinearAlgebra · isClosed_range_of_idem⟧. $\square$ At a zero in good position this gives $\mathrm{IsSurjLinOfIndex}(f, x_0, \dim P_n - \dim Q)$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndexG · StrongBundle.isSurjLinOfIndex_of_conjugationData⟧, because for a surjective Fredholm operator the kernel dimension is the index ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFT · finrank_ker_eq_fredholmIndex_of_surjective⟧; and the $d$ with $\mathrm{IsSurjLinOfIndex}(f, x_0, d)$ equals $\dim P_n - \dim Q$ for every conjugation in the chart of Definition 3.6.6 (preprint 3.53) ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndexG · StrongBundle.IsGoodPositionGerm.exists_isSurjLinOfIndex⟧ (a conjugation exists, ⟦Chapter3/FredholmGermIndex · isConjugateToBasicGerm_iff_nonempty⟧). The first clause of Theorem 3.1.22 (preprint 3.19) with the index ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndexG · theorem_3_1_22_linearization_index⟧: for an sc-Fredholm section and a zero in good position, in the chart of Definition 3.6.6 (preprint 3.53), $Dg(0)$ and $Tf(x_0)$ are Fredholm, $Tf(x_0)$ is onto, and $\mathrm{ind}\,Dg(0) = \mathrm{ind}\,Tf(x_0) = \dim\ker Tf(x_0) = \dim d.P_n - \dim d.Q$ for every conjugation $d$ — the old formulation's "$n - N$ for some natural numbers" made precise.

**The conjugation exposed.** The graph description and the tangent germ data at the centre are built from the chart of Definition 3.6.6 (preprint 3.53) by destructuring the existential good position, so their statements did not record which conjugation they used. Their proofs now prove versions that expose it ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTG · theorem_3_6_8_graph_proj_conj⟧ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTCenterG · theorem_3_6_8_tangentGermChart_proj_conj⟧: the filling germ with its germ and derivative family $Dg$, the sc⁺-germ, the good position at $Dg(0)$ in the chart and conjugation data $d_c$, with $\dim K = \dim d_c.P_n - \dim d_c.Q$ for $K = \ker D(h + t)(0)$, because $D(h + t)(0)$ is onto and Fredholm of index $n - N$ (§III.18, ⟦Chapter3/SolutionLinearization · StabilityData.isFredholm_Dfs⟧). The earlier statements are corollaries.

**Dimension = index.** *At the centre* ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndexG · theorem_3_6_8_tangentGermChart_index⟧: for an sc-smooth section of a strong bundle whose charts have tame base retractions and a zero $x_0$ in good position, the data of Definition 3.2.1 (preprint 3.25) at $x_0$ for $\{y \in V_x : f(y) = 0\}$ (with $\pi(O) \subseteq C$) have a dimension $d$ with $\mathrm{IsSurjLinOfIndex}(f, x_0, d)$: the exposed $d = \dim K = \dim d_c.P_n - \dim d_c.Q$ and the previous paragraph. The same for the global solution set ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndexG · theorem_3_6_8_tangentGermChart_solutionSet_index⟧ (restriction of the chart, §III.37) and for the regular data of Theorem 3.6.8 (2) (preprint 3.54) ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndexG · theorem_3_6_8_two_index⟧ (the re-centring keeps the dimension, ⟦Chapter3/SolutionGermRecentre · MPolyfold.TangentGermChart.exists_isRegular_of_locally⟧). *At a nearby zero* ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndexG · isSurjLinOfIndex_of_forall_dim⟧: if $y$ is a zero in good position at which every datum of Definition 3.2.1 (preprint 3.25) for the solution set has dimension $d$, then $\mathrm{IsSurjLinOfIndex}(f, y, d)$ — the data at $y$ from the centre theorem at $y$ have dimension $\mathrm{ind}\,f'(y)$, and that dimension is $d$.

**The index is locally constant along the solution set** ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndexG · theorem_3_6_8_index_locally_constant⟧. *Statement.* For an sc-Fredholm section of a strong bundle whose charts have tame base retractions, a zero $x_0$ in good position: there are an open $V_x \ni x_0$ and $d$ with $\mathrm{IsSurjLinOfIndex}(f, y, d)$ at every zero $y \in V_x$; in particular $\mathrm{ind}\,f'(y) = \mathrm{ind}\,f'(x_0)$. *Proof.* Theorem 3.6.8 (1) (preprint 3.54) (§III.43) gives an open $V_{x,1}$ of nearby good position; Theorem 3.6.8 (2) (preprint 3.54) with the index on it gives regular data $D$ of dimension $d$ with source in $V_{x,1}$ along which every datum has dimension $d$; on the source of $D$, every zero is in good position with all its data of dimension $d$, and the previous paragraph applies. $\square$ The proof does not repeat the kernel computation of the matrix argument (Finding 188): "the dimension is locally constant" (from the re-centring and Sard's lemma in fixed dimension, §III.35) and "the dimension is the index" give "the index is locally constant". Along the solution set this is the index stability of the text's Proposition 6.2.2 (preprint 6.2), whose proof needs exactly the surjectivity of §III.43. *Theorem 3.6.8 (preprint 3.54) with the index* ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndexG · theorem_3_6_8_index⟧: on an open $V_x \ni x_0$ every zero is in good position with $\mathrm{IsSurjLinOfIndex}(f, y, d)$, and the solution set has at $x_0$ regular data of dimension $d$ whose chart domain *is* $V_x$, every datum at a point of the solution set in $V_x$ having dimension $d$.

**Theorems 3.1.26, 3.1.22 and 3.6.11 (preprint 3.23, 3.19 and 3.57) with the index.** Theorem 3.1.26 (preprint 3.23) ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndexG · theorem_3_1_26_index⟧: for an sc-Fredholm section with tame base retractions and a zero $x$ in good position, an open $U \ni x$ and $d$ such that every zero $y \in U$ is in good position with $\mathrm{IsSurjLinOfIndex}(f, y, d)$, the zeros in $U$ are smooth, and $S(f, U)$ is a sub-M-polyfold with tame induced structure equivalent to a $d$-dimensional manifold with boundary with corners — the proof of the boundary case from the nearby good position (§III.37) with Theorem 3.6.8 (2) (preprint 3.54) with the index. Theorem 3.1.22 (preprint 3.19) ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndexG · theorem_3_1_22_index⟧: for $d_X \equiv 0$ the same, with degeneracy index $0$ everywhere (Lemma 2.4.6 (preprint 2.26)): "a smooth manifold whose dimension agrees with the Fredholm index", now with the tameness of the induced structure. Theorem 3.6.11 (preprint 3.57) ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndexG · theorem_3_6_11_index⟧: under the hypotheses of §III.37 (good position at every zero, $S \ne \emptyset$), at every $x \in S$ there are regular data of a dimension $d$, shared by every datum at the points of $S$ in their chart domain, with $\mathrm{IsSurjLinOfIndex}(f, y, d)$ at every zero $y$ of that domain.

### III.45 The kernel as tangent space, good position at interior zeros, and the filler condition as a theorem

**"$\ker f'(y) = T_yS(f, U)$", in a chart** ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndexG · theorem_3_6_8_ker_eq_tangent⟧. *Statement.* For an sc-smooth section of a strong bundle whose charts have tame base retractions and a zero $x_0$ in good position, there are the strong bundle chart $c$ centred at $x_0$ of Definition 3.6.6 (preprint 3.53), the conjugating sc-diffeomorphism $\psi : U \to U' \subseteq C'$ with inverse $\psi'$ and $\psi(0) = 0$, the finite-dimensional $K = \ker D(h + t)(0)$, the solution graph $\sigma : V' \to E'$ ($C^1$, $\sigma(0) = 0$, $D\sigma(0) = 0$, $V'$ relatively open in $K \cap C'$), an open $V_x \ni x_0$ with $\psi\varphi(\{y \in V_x : f(y) = 0\}) = \{v + \sigma(v) : v \in V'\}$, and an open $V_{x,2} \subseteq V_x$ containing $x_0$, such that at every zero $y \in V_{x,2}$, with $\psi(\varphi(y)) = v + \sigma(v)$, and every level-$1$ point $z$ over $\varphi(y)$:
$$D\psi(z)\big(\ker\mathrm{Lin}(z)\big) = N_v = \{n + D\sigma(v)n \mid n \in K\},$$
the tangent space at $v$ of the graph, which is the solution set in the chart $\psi \circ \varphi$ of its tangent germ data. *Proof.* From the kernel statement at the nearby zeros (§III.32, ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbySurjG · theorem_3_6_8_nearby_kernel⟧): $z$ lies on level $1$ of the germ of $g$, $\ker Dg(z) = D\psi'(\psi z)N_v$, $\ker Dg(z) \subseteq T_zO$, and $\mathrm{Lin}(z) = Dg(z)$ on $T_zO$ (the bridge for germs, which needs exactly $z$ in the germ and $0 \in O$, ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationChartsG · linAt_eq_germDeriv⟧; whole-chart ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · linAt_eq_scDeriv⟧), so $\ker\mathrm{Lin}(z) = \ker Dg(z)$; and $D\psi(z)D\psi'(\psi z) = 1$ on level $0$ by the chain rule for $\psi \circ \psi' = 1$ on $U'$. $\square$ The intrinsic statement, with $T_yS$ as a subspace of $T_yX$ (Definition 2.5.8 (preprint 2.38)), is not formalized: $T_yX$ has no linear structure in the formalization (Chapter 2 gap G.13); the chart form is the statement (Finding 172).

**Good position at a zero with $d_X = 0$ is surjectivity.** *Linear algebra* ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndex · isInGoodPositionIn_of_subset⟧: a subspace $N$ of $T \subseteq E_0$ is in good position inside $T$ to every cone $C$ containing $T$ — $N \cap C = N$ has interior in $N$, and any algebraic complement of $N$ in $T$ is a good complement with any constant, both sides of the condition being true. *The statement* ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndexG · StrongBundle.isGoodPositionGerm_of_linAt_surj_of_degIndex_eq_zero⟧: let $f$ be an sc-smooth section, $y$ a zero with $(f, y)$ an sc-Fredholm germ and $d_X(y) = 0$, $c$ a strong bundle chart around $y$ with tame base retraction, and $\mathrm{Lin}(z)$ onto $K_z$ at the level-$1$ point $z$ over $\varphi(y)$; then $(f, y)$ is in good position. *Proof.* The model of $c$ is a tame retract, so $d_C(\varphi(y)) = d_X(y) = 0$ by the strong form of Proposition 2.5.6 (preprint 2.36) ⟦Chapter2/TameRetracts · degIndex_eq_chartDegIndex_of_isTameRetract⟧; no coordinate of the presentation vanishes at $\varphi(y)$, so the partial quadrant at $\varphi(y)$ is the whole space, and the kernel condition of the assembly step holds by the linear algebra. The assembly step with all of $K_z$ in the range ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationChartsG · StrongBundle.isGoodPositionGerm_of_linAt_surj⟧ concludes. $\square$ For an sc-Fredholm section with tame base retractions, "$f'(y)$ onto in some chart" therefore gives good position at a zero with $d_X(y) = 0$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndexG · StrongBundle.isGoodPositionGerm_of_isLinSurjAt⟧ ($y$ is smooth, so the germ is sc-Fredholm); conversely good position gives "$f'(y)$ onto" in the chart of Definition 3.6.6 (preprint 3.53) ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndexG · StrongBundle.IsGoodPositionGerm.isLinSurjAt⟧; so at such a zero the two are equivalent ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndexG · StrongBundle.isGoodPositionGerm_iff_isLinSurjAt⟧ — the text's remark after Theorem 3.6.8 (preprint 3.54). In a non-tame chart $d_C(\varphi(y))$ can exceed $d_X(y)$, and the cone would have to be transported from a chart realizing $d_X(y)$; the standing tameness hypothesis makes that unnecessary (Finding 173).

**Theorems 3.1.22 and 3.6.10 (preprint 3.19 and 3.56) with "$f'(x)$ surjective".** Theorem 3.1.22 (preprint 3.19) ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndexG · theorem_3_1_22_of_surjective⟧: for $d_X \equiv 0$, an sc-Fredholm section with tame base retractions, $f(x_0) = 0$ and $f'(x_0)$ onto in some chart: the equivalence gives good position at $x_0$, and Theorem 3.1.22 (preprint 3.19) with the index (§III.44) applies. Theorem 3.6.10 (preprint 3.56) with the tameness and the regular data ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndexG · theorem_3_6_10_tame⟧: for $d_X \equiv 0$ and good position at every zero, $S \ne \emptyset$: Theorem 3.6.11 (preprint 3.57) with the index (§III.44) and Lemma 2.4.6 (preprint 2.26) — the solution set is a sub-M-polyfold with tame induced structure, a manifold of locally constant dimension without boundary, with regular data at every zero of a dimension equal to the index at the zeros of their chart domain. With "$f'(x)$ surjective at every zero" ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndexG · theorem_3_6_10_of_surjective⟧: the equivalence at every zero, then the previous statement.

**The converse of the matrix argument, and the filler condition as a theorem.** *The converse* ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndex · normal_injective_of_exists_range_scDeriv_eq_ρ⟧. Let $z$ be a level-$1$ point over a point $z_0$ of the retract with $\ker Dg(z) \subseteq T_{z_0}O$, and suppose $\rho(z_0)k \in Dg(z)(T_{z_0}O)$ for every $k \in F$. Then $Dr(z)b = 0$ and $\rho(z_0)Dg(z)b = Dg(z)b$ imply $b = 0$: choose $a \in T_{z_0}O$ with $Dg(z)a = \rho(z_0)Dg(z)b = Dg(z)b$; then $a - b \in \ker Dg(z) \subseteq T_{z_0}O$, so $b \in T_{z_0}O$; writing $b = Dr(z)w$, idempotence gives $b = Dr(z)b = 0$ ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · scDeriv_r_idem_at⟧. The surjectivity of $Dg(z)$ is not needed for this direction. With the direction of §III.33 ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbySurj · exists_range_scDeriv_eq_ρ_of_normal_injective⟧, under $Dg(z)$ onto and $\ker Dg(z) \subseteq T_{z_0}O$ (and $g$ agreeing with $\mathbf f$ on $O$ near a zero $z_0$ of $\mathbf f$): $\rho(z_0)F \subseteq Dg(z)(T_{z_0}O)$ iff condition (3) of Definition 3.1.4 (preprint 3.4) holds at $z_0$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndex · exists_range_scDeriv_eq_ρ_iff_normal_injective⟧ — the "iff" of Finding 100, both directions formalized. Both are linear algebra in the operator $Dg(z)$ and hold for a germ at the points of its germ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndexG · normal_injective_of_exists_range_deriv_eq_ρ_germ⟧ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndexG · exists_range_deriv_eq_ρ_iff_normal_injective_germ⟧. *The filler condition* ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndexG · isGoodPositionGermFilled_of_isGoodPositionGerm⟧: for an sc-Fredholm section with tame base retractions and a zero $x_0$ in good position, $(f, x_0)$ is in good position with the filler condition at the nearby zeros ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbySurjG · StrongBundle.IsGoodPositionGermFilled⟧. *Proof.* Take the chart and filler of the kernel statement (§III.32) and the open $V_{x,1}$ of nearby good position (§III.43); let $N$ be an open neighbourhood of $0$ on which $\mathbf f = g$ on $O$ and $O \cap N = \varphi(V_{x,2} \cap V_{x,1})$ (⟦Chapter2/MPolyfold · exists_isOpen_image_eq⟧). At a level-$1$ zero $z_1$ of $g$ over a point of $O \cap N$, on level $1$ of the germ of $g$ (the condition asks no more, §III.33): $z_0 = \varphi(y)$ for a zero $y$ of $f$ in $V_{x,2} \cap V_{x,1}$; $y$ is in good position, so $\mathrm{Lin}(z_1)$ is onto $K_{z_0}$ in the chart $c$ (§III.44); the kernel statement gives $\ker Dg(z_1) \subseteq T_{z_0}O$; and the converse gives condition (3) at $z_0$. $\square$ So the added hypothesis of the first forms of Theorems 3.6.8, 3.1.26, 3.1.22 (preprint 3.54, 3.23, 3.19) (§§III.33, III.37) holds whenever their other hypotheses do: it was never a restriction, but proving that needed the surjectivity of §III.43 (Finding 174).

### III.46 What the second addendum taught us

1. **The text's Theorem 3.6.8 (1) (preprint 3.54) is true, and the gap of its proof can be closed.** The surjectivity of $f'(y)$ at the zeros near a zero in good position does not follow from Corollary 3.5.3 (preprint 3.41) and Lemma 3.6.9 (preprint 3.55), as the text (and the book's arXiv version) says; it follows from an implicit function theorem *at the nearby zero* for the perturbed filler $g + \mu\rho(r(\cdot))k$, one smooth direction $k$ at a time, whose curve of zeros is tangent to the retract by condition (2) of the filler ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoG · theorem_3_6_8_chart_onto⟧ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoG · theorem_3_6_8_one'⟧ (§§III.41–III.43). With it, Theorems 3.6.8, 3.1.26 and 3.1.22 (preprint 3.54, 3.23 and 3.19) hold with the text's hypotheses.
2. **The uniformity problem disappears when the contraction regions are written down.** Theorem 3.3.3 (preprint 3.30) is applied at the centre to the $k$-extended contraction germ, whose regions in $a$ are those of the unperturbed germ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoGerm · ShiftedBasicData.perturbContraction⟧; only the $\mu$-interval depends on $k$, and the bounds of the perturbation scale linearly in $k$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoXi · exists_xi_radii_bounds⟧. The black-box use of Theorem 3.5.11 (preprint Corollary 3.47) for an extended problem gives neighbourhoods depending on the extension.
3. **The index raise of Theorem 3.4.2 (preprint 3.38) is what makes the perturbation $C^1$ on the shifted levels** (§III.42), and the surjectivity of the reduced finite-dimensional problem comes from a dimension count on level $0$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoCurve · ShiftedBasicData.surjective_DH_and_ker_eq⟧, not from inverting $1 - D_2B$.
4. **The index is locally constant along the solution set** ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndexG · theorem_3_6_8_index_locally_constant⟧, the special case of the text's Proposition 6.2.2 (preprint 6.2), and the dimension of the solution set is the Fredholm index at every zero, in every chart ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndexG · theorem_3_1_22_index⟧ (§III.44); $\ker f'(y)$ is the tangent space of the solution set in the chart of the proof ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndexG · theorem_3_6_8_ker_eq_tangent⟧ (§III.45).
5. **The filler condition at the nearby zeros was never a restriction** ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndexG · isGoodPositionGermFilled_of_isGoodPositionGerm⟧, and good position at a zero with $d_X = 0$ is surjectivity for a tame strong bundle ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndexG · StrongBundle.isGoodPositionGerm_iff_isLinSurjAt⟧ (§III.45), so Theorems 3.1.22 and 3.6.10 (preprint 3.19 and 3.56) hold with "$f'(x)$ surjective".
6. **Theorem 3.1.21's (preprint 3.18) "readily verified" is a construction**: translation charts of $\mathbb R^n$, the pull-back bundle as a structure, the product rule for a scalar factor, and the extension of a basic germ by inert parameters ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · IsBasicGerm.paramExtend⟧ ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbationG · theorem_3_1_21_preprint⟧ (§III.40).
7. **What is left of Chapter 3**: Proposition 3.6.5 (preprint 3.52) as an sc-Fredholm operator on $T_xX$; $\ker f'(y) = T_yS$ with $T_yS \subseteq T_yX$; Theorem 3.7.1 (preprint 3.58) for a general sc-splitting; Lemma 3.8.13 (preprint 3.70) for a general presentation of the partial quadrant; Theorem 3.2.3 (preprint 3.27) for sets whose data violate the second regularity condition; and Proposition 6.2.2 (preprint 6.2) beyond solution sets (since proved, with the text's hypotheses for paths on $\mathbb R$, §III.49). The only added hypotheses are the closedness of good complements and the regularity conditions of Theorem 3.2.3 (preprint 3.27) for general subsets.

### III.47 The tameness hypothesis repaired

The implicit function theorems of §§III.23 and III.32–III.45 were first formalized with the hypothesis, `htame` in the Lean, that the base retraction of **every** strong bundle chart compatible with the atlas is tame; the phrase "a strong bundle whose charts have tame base retractions" in those sections describes it. No strong bundle over a nonempty base satisfies it, so the statements that assumed it were vacuously true (Finding 190). This section records why, and what replaced it. The proofs told above are unchanged; the hypotheses and the charts of the conclusions changed, and the names of the theorems did not.

**Why the hypothesis fails.** Compatibility of strong bundle charts (Definition 2.6.4 (preprint 2.50)) constrains the transition maps between the retracts $K$, not the retraction whose image $K$ is. From a compatible chart with base retraction $r$ on $U \subseteq C$, the chart $(1, \varphi)$ onto $\{1\} \times O$, with model $\mathbb R \oplus E$, quadrant $[0, \infty) \times C$ and base retraction $r'(a, u) = (1, r(u))$ on $[0, \infty) \times U$, has the transition maps of the first chart composed with affine maps, so it is compatible with every chart the first one is; but $r'$ lowers the degeneracy index at $(0, u)$. The last point is machine-checked. The constant retraction $(a, w) \mapsto (1, 0)$ of $[0, \infty) \oplus W$ ⟦Chapter3/TameVacuity · ScRetraction.halfLineConst⟧ has retract $\{(1, 0)\}$ ⟦Chapter3/TameVacuity · ScRetraction.halfLineConst_retract⟧, and its product with any sc-smooth retraction $r$ of a nonempty $U$ is not tame ⟦Chapter3/TameVacuity · ScRetraction.halfLineConst_prod_not_isTame⟧: the degeneracy index of a product is the sum of the indices ⟦Chapter2/FibredProduct · degIndex_prod⟧, so it is $1 + d_C(u)$ at $((0, 0), u)$, $u \in r(U)$, and $d_C(u)$ at the image $((1, 0), u)$, against condition (1) of Definition 2.5.2 (preprint 2.34). That the chart $(1, \varphi)$ is compatible is not formalized.

**The new hypotheses.** The text asks for the tameness of particular charts: the chart of an sc-Fredholm germ has "a tame strong local bundle" $K \to O$ (Definitions 3.1.6 and 3.6.3 (preprint 3.6 and 3.50)), and the proof of Proposition 3.6.5 (preprint 3.52) starts from "a tame strong bundle chart". A *tame chart* is a compatible strong bundle chart whose base retraction is tame ⟦Chapter3/TameGerms · StrongBundle.IsTameChart⟧. *Tame germ data* at $x$ are the data of Definition 3.1.11 (preprint 3.11) (§III.6) in a tame chart ⟦Chapter3/TameGermsG · StrongBundle.IsScFredholmGermTame⟧; *good position in a tame chart* adds Definition 3.6.6 (preprint 3.53) in that chart (§III.21) ⟦Chapter3/TameGermsG · StrongBundle.IsGoodPositionGermTame⟧; and an sc-Fredholm section *with tame germ data* has them at every smooth point ⟦Chapter3/TameGermsG · StrongBundle.IsScFredholmSectionTame⟧. Each implies the notion without tameness ⟦Chapter3/TameGermsG · StrongBundle.IsScFredholmGermTame.isScFredholmGerm⟧ ⟦Chapter3/TameGermsG · StrongBundle.IsGoodPositionGermTame.isGoodPositionGerm⟧. They can be met: tameness depends only on the domain of a retraction and its values there ⟦Chapter3/TameGerms · ScRetraction.IsTame.congr⟧, and the identity retraction is tame ⟦Chapter2/Tame · ScRetraction.id_isTame⟧, so a compatible chart whose base retraction is the identity of $U$ is tame ⟦Chapter3/TameGerms · StrongBundle.isTameChart_of_eqOn_id⟧.

**The restated theorems.** The proofs used the tameness for three charts only (Finding 191): the chart of the germ data at $x_0$, for $(1 - Dr(0))E \subseteq E_0$ in the linear part (§III.23) ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTG · theorem_3_6_8_graph_proj_conj⟧; the chart $\psi \circ \varphi$ built from it, whose model is tame because $r$ is (§§III.27, III.34) ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTCenterG · theorem_3_6_8_tangentGermChart_proj_conj⟧; and the chart in which $f'(y)$ is onto at a zero with $d_X(y) = 0$ (§III.45). So each theorem now takes $(f, x_0)$ in good position in a tame chart in place of good position and `htame`; where it concludes something at the other zeros it takes an sc-Fredholm section with tame germ data; its conclusions are good position in tame charts, and the charts it produces are tame. This applies to Theorem 3.6.8 (1) (preprint 3.54) with the text's hypotheses ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoG · theorem_3_6_8_one'⟧, to its first form, whose filler condition is now stated in a tame chart ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbySurjG · StrongBundle.IsGoodPositionGermFilled⟧ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbySurjG · theorem_3_6_8_one⟧, to Theorems 3.1.26 and 3.1.22 (preprint 3.23 and 3.19) ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoG · theorem_3_1_26'⟧ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTNearbyOntoG · theorem_3_1_22'⟧, to the global theorems, with good position in a tame chart at every zero ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTTheoremsG · theorem_3_6_11⟧ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTTheoremsG · theorem_3_6_10⟧ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTTheoremsG · corollary_3_1_27⟧, to the forms with the index ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndexG · theorem_3_6_8_index⟧, and to the filler condition as a theorem ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndexG · isGoodPositionGermFilled_of_isGoodPositionGerm⟧.

**Reading the conclusion in the tame chart.** At a nearby zero $y$ the good position is proved in the chart at $x_0$ and carried to a chart centred at $y$ (§III.31). That chart was the one Definition 3.1.11 (preprint 3.11) supplies at $y$, of which nothing says that it is tame. The chart change now gives the good position in **every** chart carrying germ data at $y$ ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · StrongBundle.isGoodPositionAt_of_chart_dense⟧, the statement of §III.31 being its corollary ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationCharts · StrongBundle.isGoodPositionGerm_of_chart_dense⟧. A good position given in any chart therefore holds in the tame chart of the germ data ⟦Chapter3/TameGermsAddG · StrongBundle.IsGoodPositionGerm.toTame⟧: in the chart of the given good position $Tf(0)$ maps onto $K_0 \supseteq \rho(0)F_\infty$, $\ker Dg(0) \subseteq T_0O$ and $C_0 = C$, which are the hypotheses of the chart change at $z = 0$; in the germ reading the point $z = 0$ lies in every germ, $Tf(0)$ is onto all of $K_0$, and the form of the chart change with all of $K_0$ in the range needs no Proposition 3.6.5 (preprint 3.52) (§III.31) ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationChartsG · StrongBundle.isGoodPositionAtGerm_of_chart⟧. The same step gives good position from "$f'(y)$ surjective" at a zero with $d_X(y) = 0$: the surjectivity, given in one chart, holds in every chart ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndex · StrongBundle.IsLinSurjAt.linAt_surjective⟧, in particular in the tame chart of the germ data at $y$, where $d_C = d_X = 0$ ⟦Chapter3/GlobalIFTIndexG · StrongBundle.isGoodPositionGerm_of_isLinSurjAt⟧.

**Theorems 3.1.18 and 3.1.21 (preprint 3.15 and 3.18) keep tame germ data.** The proof of Theorem 3.1.18 (preprint 3.15) (§III.6) does not change the chart, so $f + s$ has germ data in the tame charts of $f$ ⟦Chapter3/TameGermsAddG · StrongBundle.IsScFredholmGermTame.sectionAdd⟧ ⟦Chapter3/TameGermsAddG · theorem_3_1_18_tame⟧. For Theorem 3.1.21 (preprint 3.18) (§III.40) the germ of $\tilde f$ at $(r_0, x)$ is taken in the product chart $(r \mapsto r - r_0) \times \varphi$, whose base retraction $1 \times r$ on $\mathbb R^n \times U$ is tame when $r$ is: the identity retraction of $\mathbb R^n$ is tame, so $d(a, u) = d_C(u)$, and the minimal subspaces and tangent spaces of the product are products ⟦Chapter2/FibredProduct · ScRetraction.IsTameWith.prod⟧ ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · PullbackChartData.ofProd_isTame⟧. Hence $\tilde f$ has tame germ data ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbationG · StrongBundle.IsScFredholmGermTame.param⟧ ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbationG · StrongBundle.IsScFredholmSectionTame.param⟧, and so has $F(r, x) = f(x) + \sum_i r_is_i(x)$ ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbationG · theorem_3_1_21_tame⟧, which is what the implicit function theorems need when applied to $F$.

**Not done** (Finding 199): the compatibility of the chart $(1, \varphi)$, that is, the vacuity of `htame` at the level of charts; and the passage from an atlas of tame charts to tame germ data (Definition 3.1.11 (preprint 3.11) ⟹ Definition 3.6.3 (preprint 3.50)). The text builds the tame chart into Definition 3.6.3 (preprint 3.50), and so do the theorems.

**Germ data and the charts of a tame atlas** (Findings 350–353, fourth addendum). The passage was to be made by re-centring a tame chart of the atlas at the point and transporting the filler and the conjugation to it. That strategy fails, because a filling is bound to its chart. *Condition (3) of Definition 3.1.4 (preprint 3.4) is a condition on the chart*: a filling germ in a chart with strong bundle retraction $R = (r, \rho)$ gives a topological isomorphism $\ker Dr(0) \cong \ker\rho(0)$, the linearization of the fill defect $y \mapsto [1 - \rho(r(y))]g(y)$ on $\ker Dr(0)$ ⟦Chapter3/TameGermData · IsFillingGerm.nonempty_kerEquiv⟧; so in a chart with $\ker Dr(0) \ne 0$ and $\rho(0)$ injective no map fills anything ⟦Chapter3/TameGermData · not_isFillingGerm_of_ker⟧. *A tame chart with no filling.* For the trivial bundle $\mathbb R^n \times \mathbb R^k \to \mathbb R^n$ take the model $\mathbb R \oplus \mathbb R^n$ without boundary, the base retraction $r(a, u) = (0, u)$, the product of the constant retraction of $\mathbb R$ onto $0$ with the identity ⟦Chapter3/TameGermData · TrivialBundle.Reven⟧, the chart $x \mapsto (0, x)$, and $R((a, u), h) = ((0, u), h)$ ⟦Chapter3/TameGermData · TrivialBundle.evenBundleChart⟧. It is compatible with the translation charts ⟦Chapter3/TameGermData · TrivialBundle.isChart_evenBundleChart⟧ and tame — the degeneracy index of $\mathbb R \oplus \mathbb R^n$ is $0$ everywhere and $\mathbb R \oplus 0$ is an sc-complement of $T_xO = 0 \oplus \mathbb R^n$ ⟦Chapter3/TameGermData · TrivialBundle.Reven_isTame⟧ ⟦Chapter3/TameGermData · TrivialBundle.isTameChart_evenBundleChart⟧ — but $\ker Dr(0) = \mathbb R \oplus 0$ while $\rho(0) = 1$, so no map fills the principal part of any section in it ⟦Chapter3/TameGermData · TrivialBundle.not_isFillingGerm_evenBundleChart⟧. *A tame atlas without germ data in its chart.* With the one-chart atlas $\{$this chart$\}$ ⟦Chapter3/TameGermData · TrivialBundle.bundleEven⟧ every chart of the atlas is tame ⟦Chapter3/TameGermData · TrivialBundle.bundleEven_atlas_isTameChart⟧, the graph section of a $C^\infty$ map has tame germ data — in the compatible translation charts ⟦Chapter3/TameGermData · TrivialBundle.isScFredholmSectionTame_graphSection_even⟧ — and in the chart of the atlas no section has a filling ⟦Chapter3/TameGermData · TrivialBundle.tame_atlas_no_germData_in_atlas_chart⟧. So Definition 3.6.3 (preprint 3.50) must be the existential over all compatible tame charts, as formalized; read as "in a chart of the tame atlas" it would make every section of this bundle non-Fredholm. Whether germ data in some compatible chart give germ data in some compatible tame chart, for a tame atlas, stays open: the transition between two charts is defined on the retracts only, while the filling lives off them, so it cannot simply be pushed forward.

### III.48 Definition 3.1.11 (preprint 3.11) on germs

The text states Definitions 3.1.4, 3.1.6 and 3.1.11 (preprint 3.4, 3.6 and 3.11) for germs: the filled version is "a germ $(g, 0)$", the sc⁺-section a local one, and a germ of neighbourhoods (Definition 3.1.1 (preprint 3.1)) is a sequence $U_m$ that may shrink with $m$. The first formalization asked $g$ and $s$ to be sc-smooth on the whole chart domain $U$, on the ground that a chart can be restricted. This section records why that was narrower, and the two devices on which the proofs in the text's germ reading rest: the calculus of germs with whole-chart maps, and the shrinking of the basic germ (Findings 260–334).

**Germs are not restrictions.** On $\mathbb R$ with the constant structure, $f(x) = \sum_n c_n(x - a_n)^{n+1}|x - a_n|$, $a_n = 1/(n+2)$, $c_n = (4(n+2))^{-(n+2)}$, is $C^k$ on $U_k = (-1/(k+2), 1/(k+2))$ — the terms $n \le k$ have their singular points outside, the tail is $C^{k+2}$ — and $C^{n+2}$ on no open set containing $a_n$. So $f$ is an sc-smooth germ on $(U_m)$ and sc-smooth on no open $U \ni 0$ ⟦Chapter3/GermVsChart · exists_isScSmoothGerm_not_isScSmooth⟧. Restricting a chart on level $0$ never makes the levels shrink, so the whole-chart reading is a restriction of the class of sections; for a section sc-smooth on $X$ the two readings of Definition 3.1.11 (preprint 3.11) differ exactly in the regularity of $g$ and $s$ away from their germs (§III.6). Whether every germ datum can be traded for a whole-chart one is open: replacing the sc⁺-germ by a whole-chart sc⁺-section costs one level (the index raise of Theorem 3.4.2 (preprint 3.38)), and on level $0$ no argument was found in either direction (Finding 264). No theorem depends on the answer, since the theorems are proved in the germ reading.

**The calculus with whole-chart maps.** The text forms $g + s$, $g - s$, $[1 - \rho(r(y))]g(y)$ and $\Psi_*(g)$ for germs without comment. Sums, differences, scalar multiples and compositions with sc-operators of sc¹-germs are sc¹-germs with the explicit derivative families, on $G_1 \cap G_2$ for germs on two germs ⟦Chapter3/GermCalculus · IsSc1GermWith.add⟧ ⟦Chapter3/GermCalculus · IsScSmoothGerm.add_inter⟧. The chain rule of §III.1 composes two germs *at $0$*, but $R$ and $\Psi$ are applied at $(r(y), g(y))$, which is $(0, 0)$ only when $g(0) = 0$ and $0 \in O$; so the chain rule is proved again with the outer map sc¹ on a set $V$ and the inner one a germ mapping $G_0$ into $V$, with no condition at a base point ⟦Chapter3/GermCalculus · IsSc1With.comp_germ⟧ ⟦Chapter3/GermCalculus · IsScSmooth.comp_germ⟧, and with a whole-chart inner map $\psi$, $\psi(0) = 0$, on the preimage germ ⟦Chapter3/GermCalculus · IsScSmoothGerm.comp_preimage⟧. Hence the fill defect of a germ is a germ on the same germ (§III.4); the push-forward $\Psi_*(g)$ is a germ on the germ of the points that $\psi^{-1}$ maps into the germ of $g$, with $D(\Psi_*g)(0) = \Gamma(0)Dg(0)D\psi(0)^{-1}$ (§III.23); a germ plus a whole-chart map is a germ on the same germ (Theorem 3.1.18 (preprint 3.15), §III.6); and $(a, u) \mapsto g(u)$ is a germ on $\mathbb R^n \times G$ (Theorem 3.1.21 (preprint 3.18), §III.40). The strong bundle isomorphism $\Psi$ and the basic germ $h$ remain what they were.

**A basic germ restricts.** Definitions 3.1.7 and 3.3.1 (preprint 3.7 and 3.28) pass to every smaller germ with the same $h$ and $B$: the contraction field after shrinking its radius, since every level of a germ is a relatively open neighbourhood of $0$, and the sc-smoothness by the uniqueness of derivatives in the quadrant ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermShrink · IsBasicGerm.mono⟧.

**The shrunk basic germ $G''$.** The text does not relate the germ $G$ of the basic germ to the germs $G_g$, $G_s$ of $g$ and $s$, and nothing forces $\psi^{-1}(G_m) \subseteq (G_g)_m$. For conjugation data with base map $\psi$, inverse $\psi'$ and basic germ on $G$, let $G''_m = \{x \in G_m \mid \psi'_m(x) \in (G_g)_m \cap (G_s)_m\}$, the preimage germ along the sc⁰-germ $\psi'|_G$ ($\psi'$ is sc-smooth on all of $U'$ and $\psi'(0) = 0$) ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermShrink · ConjugationData.shrinkGerm⟧. The conjugation data shrunk to $G''$ have the same $\Psi$, $h$, $B$ and conjugation identity ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermShrink · ConjugationData.shrink⟧ ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermShrink · ConjugationData.basic_shrinkGerm⟧; a point of $G''_m$ is sent by $\psi'_m$ into $(G_g)_m \cap (G_s)_m$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermShrink · ConjugationData.levelMap_mem_inter_of_mem_shrinkGerm⟧, and read on the $E$-side, $u \in U_m$ with $\psi_m(u) \in G''_m$ lies in both germs ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermShrink · ConjugationData.mem_of_levelMap_base_mem⟧. *Corollary 3.5.3 (preprint 3.41) on $G''$*: every zero $x$ of the normal form with $|x|_0$ small is $\iota x_1$ with $x_1$ on level $1$ of $G''$ — it is small on level $1$ (Theorem 3.5.1 (preprint 3.40)), and the contraction field puts a level-$1$ ball around $0$ inside $G''_1$ — so $\psi'_1(x_1)$ lies on level $1$ of $G_g$ and $G_s$ ⟦Chapter3/BasicGermShrinkIFT · ConjugationData.corollary_3_5_3_shrink⟧. This is the device of every argument at the nearby zeros: the level-$1$ lift $z_{0,1}$ of a zero near $x_0$ lies in the germ of $g$, where $Dg(z_{0,1})$ is defined (§§III.32, III.33, III.43, III.45). It relies on the lifts being small on level $1$; a point small on level $0$ only need not lie in $G''_1$. At the centre no shrinking is needed: $0$ lies in every germ, and $Dg(0)$ is the same for all germs and families (§III.4).

**What the proofs use of $g$ and $s$.** At $0$: $Dg(0)$ and $Ds(0)$ (Propositions 3.1.5 and 3.6.5 (preprint 3.5 and 3.52), Definition 3.6.6 (preprint 3.53), the linear part of Theorem 3.6.8 (preprint 3.54); §§III.4, III.21, III.23, III.30). On level $0$ near $0$: continuity of $g$ and of the sc⁺-part (Theorem 3.1.13 (preprint 3.12), §III.13; the local step of Theorem 5.2.2 (preprint 5.5), §V.6). At the level-$1$ lifts of the nearby zeros: $Dg$, through $G''$ (Theorem 3.6.8 (1) (preprint 3.54), §§III.32–III.33, III.43, III.45). Nowhere $g$ or $s$ on $U \cap E_m$ away from their germs. So every theorem of Chapter 3 holds in the germ reading with the text's hypotheses and its former conclusions, and no hypothesis is added; the statements that named the derivative of $g$ on $U$ at $0$ (Proposition 3.6.5 (preprint 3.52), the linearization statements of Theorems 3.1.22 and 3.6.8 (preprint 3.19 and 3.54)) now name the germ, a derivative family $Dg$ and $Dg(0)$, and Corollary 5.2.5 (preprint 5.8) in a chart has an sc⁺-germ on the shifted germ for its sc⁺-part (§V.7).

**The switch.** The canonical names now denote the germ reading: Definitions 3.1.11, 3.1.16, 3.6.3, 3.6.4 and 3.6.6 (preprint 3.11, 3.14, 3.50, 3.51 and 3.53) with the tame, filled and sc variants of good position ⟦Chapter3/GermFillingChart · StrongBundle.IsScFredholmGerm⟧ ⟦Chapter3/TameGermsG · StrongBundle.IsScFredholmSection⟧ ⟦Chapter3/GoodPositionGermG · StrongBundle.IsGoodPositionGerm⟧, and the theorems built on them. The whole-chart notions and theorems keep their statements under the names `…WholeChart` and `…_wholeChart`, and each whole-chart notion implies its germ twin ⟦Chapter3/TameGermsG · StrongBundle.IsScFredholmSectionWholeChart.isScFredholmSection⟧ ⟦Chapter3/GoodPositionGermG · StrongBundle.IsGoodPositionGermWholeChart.isGoodPositionGerm⟧. The non-vacuity witnesses are whole-chart data, hence germ data. Chapter 5 is stated with the germ notions: the local step of Theorem 5.2.2 (preprint 5.5) and the spanning sections changed locally, and Corollary 5.2.5 (preprint 5.8) in a chart changed its statement (§§V.6, V.12, V.7).

### III.49 Definition 6.2.1 (preprint 6.1) and Proposition 6.2.2 (preprint 6.2): the index along paths

The text's Chapter 6 opens with the index of an sc-Fredholm germ at a smooth point which need not be a zero (Definition 6.2.1 (preprint 6.1), pp. 237–240 (preprint pp. 200–201)) and proves that it is constant along sc-smooth paths of smooth points (Proposition 6.2.2 (preprint 6.2), p. 240 (preprint p. 201)). Its proof is the device that §III.44 used along the zero set: stabilize by finitely many sc⁺-sections and apply the implicit function theorem. This section retells both: first with sc⁺-sections on all of $X$, which needs bump functions (Findings 340–348), then as the text does it, with locally defined sections on the restriction of the bundle to a chart domain and without bump functions (Findings 349, 360–367).

**Definition 6.2.1 (preprint 6.1)** ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPaths · StrongBundle.IsIndexAt⟧. *$f$ has index $i$ at $x$* if there is an sc⁺-section $s$ of $P$ with $(f + s)(x) = 0$ and $(f + s)'(x)$ Fredholm of index $i$ in every strong bundle chart around $x$ (the chart-intrinsic statement of §III.44). The text's $f - s$ with $s(x) = f(x)$ is $f + (-s)$; the text's section is local, this one global; the reading with local sections, and the agreement of the two readings under bump functions, close this section. *Well defined.* At a zero of an sc-Fredholm section the index in `IsLinOfIndex` is unique, because in a chart $\mathrm{Lin}(z)$ is a bounded operator into $K_z$ (§III.44), so the quantification over corestrictions is not vacuous ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPaths · StrongBundle.IsLinOfIndex.unique⟧. Adding an sc⁺-section $s$ with $s(x) = 0$ at a zero $x$ of $f$ does not change the index: the germ datum of $f$ at $x$, with conjugation data $dc$, gives the index $\dim P_n - \dim Q$ of $f'(x)$ (Proposition 3.6.5 (preprint 3.52)), and Theorem 3.1.18 (preprint 3.15) gives germ data of $f + s$ in the same chart whose conjugation data are $dc$ up to their values on $U$, since $(g + \hat s \circ r) - (\hat s_1 \circ r + t_0) = g - t_0$ ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPaths · StrongBundle.isLinOfIndex_sectionAdd_of_isZeroAt⟧. For two sections $s, s'$ of the definition, $s' - s$ is an sc⁺-section vanishing at $x$ ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPaths · isZeroAt_sectionDiff_of_isZeroAt_sectionAdd⟧ and $(f + s) + (s' - s) = f + s'$ ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPaths · sectionAdd_sectionAdd_sectionDiff⟧, so the indices agree ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPaths · StrongBundle.IsIndexAt.unique⟧. The text argues instead that $(t - s)'(x)$ is an sc⁺-operator, hence compact on every level, and invokes Proposition 1.1.11 (preprint 1.10); the project has no lemma that the linearization of an sc⁺-section is compact, and the germ data make it unnecessary. At a zero the index of the germ is that of $f'(x)$ ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPaths · StrongBundle.isIndexAt_of_isZeroAt⟧ ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPaths · StrongBundle.IsIndexAt.isLinOfIndex_of_isZeroAt⟧. *Existence.* If $X$ admits bump functions, every smooth point has an index: $f(x)$ is a smooth point of $Y$, Lemma 5.3.3 (preprint 5.11) gives an sc⁺-section $s_0$ with $s_0(x) = f(x)$, and for $s = -s_0$ the germ data of the sc-Fredholm section $f + s$ at its zero $x$ (Theorem 3.1.18 (preprint 3.15)) give the index ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPaths · StrongBundle.exists_isIndexAt⟧.

**Along the zero set, without good position** (Findings 240–242). Let $X$ admit bump functions, $f$ be an sc-Fredholm section with tame germ data and $f(x_0) = 0$; neither good position nor surjectivity at $x_0$ is assumed. In a chart centred at $x_0$, finitely many smooth vectors span a complement of the range of $\mathrm{Lin}(0)$, closed of finite codimension by Proposition 3.6.5 (preprint 3.52), and Lemma 5.3.3 (preprint 5.11) realizes them as allowable sc⁺-sections $s_1, \dots, s_k$ ⟦Chapter3/IndexLocallyConstantG · exists_spanning_sections_germ⟧; with further sections that tilt the kernel into good position at a corner (the conditions (i)–(ii) of §V.19), the parametrized section $F(\lambda, x) = f(x) + \sum_i\lambda_is_i(x)$ of Theorem 3.1.21 (preprint 3.18) is in good position at $(0, x_0)$ in a tame chart ⟦Chapter3/IndexLocallyConstantG · exists_sections_isParamGP_germ⟧ ⟦Chapter3/IndexLocallyConstantG · isGoodPositionGermTame_paramSection_of_isParamGP⟧. So the index of $F'$ is one integer $d$ at the zeros of $F$ near $(0, x_0)$ (§III.44), among them $(0, y)$ for every zero $y$ of $f$ near $x_0$. At $(0, y)$, in the product chart, $\mathrm{Lin}_F(0)(\delta\lambda, \delta u) = \mathrm{Lin}_f(0)\delta u + \sum_i\delta\lambda_i\hat s_i(0)$, a finite-rank extension, so $d = k + \operatorname{ind} f'(y)$ ⟦Chapter3/IndexLocallyConstant · fredholmIndex_eq_of_param⟧ ⟦Chapter3/IndexLocallyConstantG · StrongBundle.isLinOfIndex_of_paramSection_isLinOfIndex⟧. Hence $f'(y)$ has index $d - k$ in every chart at every zero $y$ near $x_0$ ⟦Chapter3/IndexLocallyConstantG · index_locally_constant_of_admitsBumpFunctions⟧, also as an sc-Fredholm operator on every level ⟦Chapter3/IndexLocallyConstantG · theorem_3_6_8_index_locally_constant_general⟧. This is the text's device for Proposition 6.2.2 (preprint 6.2) in the case of a zero.

**The local form.** For $X$ with bump functions, $f$ with tame germ data and an sc⁺-section $s$ with $(f + s)(x_0) = 0$, the index of the germ is one integer at the zeros of $f + s$ near $x_0$: $f + s$ has tame germ data (§III.47) and the previous paragraph applies to it ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPaths · isIndexAt_locally_constant_of_isZeroAt_sectionAdd⟧. Hence along a path continuous at $t_0$ on which $f + s$ vanishes near $t_0$ the index is constant near $t_0$ ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPaths · isIndexAt_eventually_const_of_path⟧, and on a preconnected parameter space a locally constant index is constant: by uniqueness the index is a locally constant function of $t$ ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPaths · isIndexAt_const_of_forall_eventually⟧. What remains is a section $s$, depending on $t_0$, that vanishes along the path — the text's $s(t, x)$ on $[0, 1] \times X$ — and the passage from the product back to $X$.

**Bump functions on $\mathbb R^n \times X$.** The device works on the product of Theorem 3.1.21 (preprint 3.18) (§III.40), which must admit bump functions when $X$ does. A $C^\infty$ function on $\mathbb R^n$ is sc-smooth on the M-polyfold $\mathbb R^n$ ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPaths · isScSmoothMap_rn_real_of_contDiff⟧, products of sc-smooth functions are sc-smooth by the product rule in every chart ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPaths · MPolyfold.IsScSmoothMap.mul⟧, and $(r, x) \mapsto \beta_1(r)\beta_2(x)$ has support in $\operatorname{supp}\beta_1 \times \operatorname{supp}\beta_2$ ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPaths · tsupport_prod_mul_subset⟧; so $\mathbb R^n \times X$ admits bump functions ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPaths · MPolyfold.admitsBumpFunctions_prod_rn⟧. Then for the pull-back $\tilde f = f \circ \pi$ and an sc⁺-section $S$ of $\pi^*P$ with $\tilde f + S$ vanishing along the graph $t \mapsto (t, \varphi(t))$ near $t_0$, the index of $(\tilde f + S)'(t, \varphi(t))$ in every chart is one integer for $t$ near $t_0$: $\tilde f + S$ has tame germ data (§§III.40, III.47), and the local constancy along its zero set applies ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPaths · isLinOfIndex_paramLift_sectionAdd_eventually_const⟧. The proposition thereby reduces to two steps, the section along the path (E) and the index count on a slice (S) ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPaths · proposition_6_2_2_of_sections_of_slice⟧.

**(E): the text's section along the path** ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPathsDevice · exists_isScPlusSection_pullback_of_path⟧. For an sc-smooth section $f$ (sc-Fredholm is not needed), an sc-smooth path $\varphi : \mathbb R^n \to X$ and $t_0$, there is an sc⁺-section $S$ of $\pi^*P$ with $\tilde f + S = 0$ along the graph near $t_0$. Take a strong bundle chart $c$ around $\varphi(t_0)$. First the path is reparametrized by a $C^\infty$ map $\sigma$ of $\mathbb R^n$ equal to the identity near $t_0$ with values in $\varphi^{-1}(\operatorname{dom}\varphi_c)$, $\sigma(t) = t_0 + b(t - t_0)(t - t_0)$ for a smooth bump $b$ ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPathsDevice · exists_contDiff_eq_id_near_mapsTo⟧; $C^\infty$ self-maps of $\mathbb R^n$ are sc-smooth ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPathsDevice · isScSmoothMap_rn_of_contDiff⟧, so $\psi = \varphi \circ \sigma$ is an sc-smooth path that stays in the chart. The representative $t \mapsto \Phi(f(\psi(t)))$ is sc-smooth from the constant structure $\mathbb R^n$ into $E \oplus F$; Proposition 1.2.2 (preprint 1.17) makes it sc-smooth from $(\mathbb R^n)^1 = \mathbb R^n$ into $(E \oplus F)^1 = E^1 \oplus F^1$, and its $F^1$-component $\hat e_1$ is the curve of smooth points that the text's $s(t, x) = R(x, f(\varphi(t)))$ needs in $F^1$ — no new analysis is required. In the product chart $(r \mapsto r - t_0) \times \varphi_c$ of $\pi^*P$ the local section is $\tau(a, u) = -R[1](u, \hat e_1(a + t_0))_2$, sc-smooth into $K[1]$ because the bundle retraction is sc-smooth on $[1]$; it is cut off by a bump of $X$ equal to $1$ near $\varphi(t_0)$ with support in the chart domain (Proposition 5.5.3 (preprint 5.30)) and extended by $0$ (§V.9). Along the graph $\Phi(S(t, \varphi(t)))_2 = -R(\varphi_c(\varphi(t)), \Phi(f(\varphi(t)))_2)_2 = -\Phi(f(\varphi(t)))_2$, since $\Phi(f(\varphi(t))) \in K$ is fixed by $R$, so $\tilde f + S$ vanishes there for $t$ near $t_0$, where $\sigma(t) = t$ and the bump is $1$. The bump function and the reparametrization make the text's local section global; the text needs neither.

**(S): the slice.** The slice $S(t, \cdot)$ of a section of $\pi^*P$, read in $Y$ through the fibres $(\pi^*Y)_{(t, x)} = P^{-1}(x)$, is a section of $P$ ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPathsDevice · pullbackSlice⟧ ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPathsDevice · IsSection.pullbackSlice⟧; $G(t, x) = 0$ iff the slice vanishes at $x$ ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPathsDevice · isZeroAt_pullback_iff_pullbackSlice⟧, and the slice of $\tilde f + S$ is $f + S(t, \cdot)$ ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPathsDevice · pullbackSlice_sectionAdd_paramLift⟧. *The slice of an sc⁺-section is sc⁺* ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPathsDevice · StrongBundle.IsScPlusSection.pullbackSlice⟧: for a chart $c$ of the atlas of $P$, the product chart $(r \mapsto r - t) \times \varphi_c$ is a chart of the atlas of $\pi^*P$, and the local representative of $S(t, \cdot)$ is that of $S$ composed with the sc-operator $u \mapsto (0, u)$ ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPathsDevice · ScBanach.prodSndLeftOp⟧ and projected. *The linearization on the slice direction*: in the product chart at $(t, x)$, whose base retraction is $(a, u) \mapsto (a, r(u))$ ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPathsDevice · PullbackChartData.r_ofProd⟧, $\mathrm{Lin}_G(0)(0, \delta u) = \mathrm{Lin}_{G(t, \cdot)}(0)\delta u$, by the chain rule for $u \mapsto \hat G(0, r(u))$ ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPathsDevice · StrongBundle.linAt_ofProd_slice⟧. *The index count*: if $G$ is an sc-Fredholm section of $\pi^*P$ whose slice $g = G(t, \cdot)$ is an sc-Fredholm section with $g(x) = 0$, and $G'(t, x)$ is Fredholm of index $i$ in every chart, then $g'(x)$ is Fredholm of index $i - n$ in every chart: $\mathrm{Lin}_G(0)(\delta a, \delta u) = \mathrm{Lin}_g(0)\delta u + \mathrm{Lin}_G(0)(\delta a, 0)$ by linearity and the slice identity, the last term has rank at most $n$, and the parameter count of the zero-set case applies, the chart independence coming from the germ datum of $g$ at $x$ ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPathsDevice · StrongBundle.isLinOfIndex_pullbackSlice_of_isLinOfIndex⟧. For $G = \tilde f + S$ ($n = 1$) this is $\mathrm{ind}(f, x) = i - 1$, with $S(t, \cdot)$ as the section of Definition 6.2.1 (preprint 6.1) ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPathsDevice · StrongBundle.isIndexAt_of_pullback_isLinOfIndex⟧.

**Proposition 6.2.2 (preprint 6.2)** ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPathsDevice · proposition_6_2_2⟧. *Let $X$ admit sc-smooth bump functions, $f$ be an sc-Fredholm section with tame germ data and $\varphi : \mathbb R \to X$ an sc-smooth path. Then $\mathrm{ind}(f, \varphi(t))$ is one integer for all $t$*; in particular $\mathrm{ind}(f, \varphi(0)) = \mathrm{ind}(f, \varphi(1))$. For each $t_0$, (E) gives $S$; the index of $\tilde f + S$ is constant near $t_0$ along the graph; (S) turns it into $\mathrm{ind}(f, \varphi(t)) + 1$; and $\mathbb R$ is connected. The paracompactness of $\mathbb R \times X$ and of $\pi^*Y$ is supplied by Theorem 2.3.10 (preprint 2.16) ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPathsDevice · proposition_6_2_2'⟧. *Against the text.* (i) The path is defined on $\mathbb R$, not on $[0, 1]$: $\mathbb R \times X$ is the product of Theorem 3.1.21 (preprint 3.18) and has no boundary, and the text's "tame M-polyfold $[0, 1] \times X$" is not used; a path on $[0, 1]$ that extends sc-smoothly to $\mathbb R$ is covered, and by the reparametrization in (E) only a neighbourhood of each $t_0$ matters. (ii) The hypotheses are tame germ data, the reading of "tame strong bundle" of §III.47, and bump functions, which the text does not assume ("we do not need a sc-smooth partition of unity"): they are used for the stabilizing sections (Lemma 5.3.3 (preprint 5.11)) and for the cut-off of the section along the path, both globalizations of the text's locally defined sections. Without them the device runs on the restriction of $P$ to a chart domain, as the text intends; that is the rest of this section. (iii) Definition 6.2.1 (preprint 6.1) is proved well defined without Proposition 1.1.11 (preprint 1.10). (iv) The two points the text's proof does not address — the tilting sections for the kernel condition at a corner and the uniformity in the zero — are those of the zero-set case above.

**Without bump functions: the restriction to an open set** (Findings 349, 360). The text restricts the bundle to a chart domain without comment ("in a chart, $s(y) = R(y, f(x))$"; "we do not need a sc-smooth partition of unity", preprint p. 202). For an open $V \subseteq X$, a chart of $X$ whose domain lies in $V$ is transported to a chart of the subtype $V$ with the same model ⟦Chapter3/RestrictOpen · MChart.restrictTo⟧, compatibility is transported ⟦Chapter3/RestrictOpen · MChart.Compatible.restrictTo⟧, and the charts of $X$ inside $V$ form the atlas of the M-polyfold $V$ ⟦Chapter3/RestrictOpen · MPolyfold.restrictOpen⟧, whose levels are the traces of those of $X$ ⟦Chapter3/RestrictOpen · MPolyfold.mem_levelSet_restrictOpen_iff⟧; an sc-smooth map with values in $V$ is sc-smooth into $V$ ⟦Chapter3/RestrictOpen · MPolyfold.IsScSmoothMap.codRestrict_restrictOpen⟧. The restriction $P|V$ lives on the pull-back $\iota^*Y = \{(v, y) \mid P(y) = v\}$ of Definition 2.6.7 (preprint 2.53), as the restricted sections of Definition 5.3.13 (preprint 5.20) do (§V.23), and its fibres are those of $P$. A strong bundle chart of $P$ with base domain inside $V$ is transported with the same $F$, $U$, $R$, $K$ ⟦Chapter3/RestrictOpen · StrongBundleChart.restrictTo⟧ ⟦Chapter3/RestrictOpen · StrongBundleChart.Compatible.restrictTo⟧, and these charts form the atlas of $P|V$ ⟦Chapter3/RestrictOpen · StrongBundle.restrictOpen⟧ ⟦Chapter3/RestrictOpen · StrongBundle.restrictTo_mem_restrictOpen_atlas⟧; every chart of $P$ restricts to $\operatorname{dom}\varphi \cap V$ ⟦Chapter3/RestrictOpen · StrongBundleChart.restrictInter⟧. The restriction $f|_V$ of an sc-smooth, sc⁺ or regularizing section is again one ⟦Chapter3/RestrictOpen · StrongBundle.IsScSmoothSection.restrictOpen⟧ ⟦Chapter3/RestrictOpen · StrongBundle.IsScPlusSection.restrictOpen⟧ ⟦Chapter3/RestrictOpen · StrongBundle.regularizing_restrictOpen⟧. Two choices of the formalization: the paracompactness of $V$ and of $\iota^*Y$ is supplied by Theorem 2.3.10 (preprint 2.16), and $V$ is nonempty, for the junk values of the transported inverse charts.

**Germ data and the index on $P|V$** (Findings 361, 362). The data of Definition 3.1.11 (preprint 3.11) on a relatively open $U$ restrict to every relatively open $U_0 \subseteq U$ containing $0$: the germs of the filling and of the sc⁺-part are intersected with $U_0$, and $Dr(0)$ and $\rho(0)$ do not change, so the kernel isomorphism of condition (3) is the same ⟦Chapter3/RestrictOpenGerms · IsFillingGerm.of_subset⟧ ⟦Chapter3/RestrictOpenGerms · IsScPlusGerm.of_subset⟧; the strong bundle isomorphism $\Psi$ of the conjugation is restricted to $U_0 \triangleleft F \to \psi(U_0) \triangleleft F'$, where $\psi(U_0) = U' \cap \psi'^{-1}(U_0)$ is relatively open by the continuity of $\psi'$, and the basic germ is shrunk to $G \cap \psi'^{-1}(U_0)$ as for $G''$ (§III.48), with the same $P_n$, $Q$, $h$, $B$ ⟦Chapter3/RestrictOpenGerms · ConjugationData.ofSubset⟧ ⟦Chapter3/RestrictOpenGerms · IsConjugateToBasicGerm.of_subset⟧. So a germ datum at $x \in V$ survives in its chart restricted to $\operatorname{dom}\varphi \cap V$ ⟦Chapter3/RestrictOpenGerms · StrongBundleChart.germData_restrictInter⟧, which is tame when the chart is ⟦Chapter3/RestrictOpenGerms · StrongBundle.IsTameChart.restrictInter⟧, and an sc-Fredholm section with tame germ data restricts to one on $P|V$ ⟦Chapter3/RestrictOpenGerms · StrongBundle.IsScFredholmSectionTame.restrictOpen⟧. *The index.* At a zero $x \in V$, $f'(x)$ has index $i$ in every chart of $P$ iff $(f|_V)'(x)$ has index $i$ in every chart of $P|V$ ⟦Chapter3/RestrictOpenGerms · StrongBundle.IsLinOfIndex.restrictOpen⟧ ⟦Chapter3/RestrictOpenGerms · StrongBundle.IsLinOfIndex.of_restrictOpen⟧. The charts are not compared — a chart of $P|V$ need not come from one of $P$ —: in either bundle the index is $\dim P_n - \dim Q$ for the germ datum (§III.44), whose restriction keeps $P_n$ and $Q$ ⟦Chapter3/RestrictOpenGerms · StrongBundle.isLinOfIndex_restrictOpen_of_conjugationData⟧, and uniqueness identifies it with $i$. The same argument passes from $P|V$ to $P|W$ for an open $W \subseteq V$, where the chart restricted to $\operatorname{dom}\varphi \cap W$ is common to both and the two linearizations agree in it ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPathsLocal · sectionRestrict⟧ ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPathsLocal · StrongBundle.IsScPlusSection.restrictOpen_of_subset⟧ ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPathsLocal · StrongBundle.IsLinOfIndex.restrictOpen_of_subset⟧; Definition 6.2.1 (preprint 6.1) restricts accordingly ⟦Chapter3/RestrictOpenGerms · StrongBundle.IsIndexAt.restrictOpen⟧ ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPathsLocal · StrongBundle.IsIndexAt.restrictOpen_of_subset⟧.

**The stabilizing sections of a chart, without cut-off** (Finding 363). In the zero-set argument above, bump functions enter only through Lemma 5.3.3 (preprint 5.11), as sc⁺-sections through given smooth points of the fibres. That property — through every smooth point $e$ of the fibre over a smooth point $x$ there is an sc⁺-section $s$ with $s(x) = e$ — is isolated ⟦Chapter3/IndexLocallyConstantLocal · StrongBundle.HasScPlusSectionsThrough⟧. Bump functions give it by Lemma 5.3.3 (preprint 5.11) ⟦Chapter3/IndexLocallyConstantLocal · MPolyfold.AdmitsBumpFunctions.hasScPlusSectionsThrough⟧, and so does a chart of the atlas over the whole base, with no cut-off ⟦Chapter3/IndexLocallyConstantLocal · StrongBundle.hasScPlusSectionsThrough_of_chart⟧: $\hat e = \Phi(e)_2$ is a smooth point of $F$, with a lift $\hat e_1 \in F^1$, and the section $y \mapsto \Phi^{-1}(\varphi(y), R(\varphi(y), \hat e))$ — the text's $R(\cdot, e)$ — has the local representative $z \mapsto R[1](z, \hat e_1)_2$, sc-smooth into $K[1]$ because the bundle retraction is sc-smooth on $[1]$; it is the cut-off section of Lemma 5.3.3 (preprint 5.11) with $\beta = 1$ (§V.9), and its value at $x$ is $e$ since $\Phi(e) \in K$ is fixed by $R$. The restriction $P|V$ to a chart domain $V$ has such a chart: the chart itself, transported to $V$. The three steps of the zero-set argument — the spanning sections, the tilting sections that put the parametrized section in good position, the local constancy — are repeated with this property in place of bump functions ⟦Chapter3/IndexLocallyConstantLocal · exists_spanning_sections_of_sections⟧ ⟦Chapter3/IndexLocallyConstantLocal · exists_sections_isParamGP_of_sections⟧ ⟦Chapter3/IndexLocallyConstantLocal · index_locally_constant_of_sections⟧; the support and norm bounds of Lemma 5.3.3 (preprint 5.11), which the index argument does not use, are dropped. *The index along the zero set with the text's hypotheses* ⟦Chapter3/IndexLocallyConstantLocal · index_locally_constant_tame⟧: *let $f$ be an sc-Fredholm section with tame germ data of any strong bundle and $x_0$ a zero; then there are an open $V \ni x_0$ and $i \in \mathbb Z$ with $f'(y)$ Fredholm of index $i$ in every chart at every zero $y \in V$.* Take a chart around $x_0$ with domain $V$: $f|_V$ has tame germ data on $P|V$, which has sc⁺-sections through every smooth fibre point, so the index of $f|_V$ is one integer at its zeros near $x_0$, and at each of them it is the index on $P$. In sc form, with the sc-Fredholm property of $\mathrm{Lin}(z)$ at every zero in every chart ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationChartsScG · StrongBundle.linAt_isScFredholm⟧, $\mathrm{Lin}(z)$ is sc-Fredholm of index $i$ on every level ⟦Chapter3/IndexLocallyConstantLocal · theorem_3_6_8_index_locally_constant_general_tame⟧. This is the localization that Finding 343 described; the gap $\operatorname{ind} C(z) = 0$ of Finding 243 remains only without tame germ data.

**Definition 6.2.1 (preprint 6.1) with local sections** (Finding 364) ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPathsLocal · StrongBundle.IsIndexAtLocal⟧. *$f$ has index $i$ at $x$* if there are an open $V \ni x$ and an sc⁺-section $s$ of $P|V$ with $(f|_V + s)(x) = 0$ and $(f|_V + s)'(x)$ Fredholm of index $i$ in every chart of $P|V$: the global definition at the beginning of this section, on $P|V$. *Well defined.* Two local sections, on $V$ and on $V'$, both restrict to $V \cap V'$ (the restriction of Definition 6.2.1 (preprint 6.1) to a smaller open set, above), where the uniqueness on $P|V \cap V'$ applies ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPathsLocal · StrongBundle.IsIndexAtLocal.unique⟧. A global section gives a local one, with $V = X$ ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPathsLocal · StrongBundle.IsIndexAt.isIndexAtLocal⟧, and at a zero the index is that of $f'(x)$ ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPathsLocal · StrongBundle.isIndexAtLocal_of_isZeroAt⟧. *Existence, without bump functions.* On the domain $V$ of a chart around $x$, the section through $f(x)$ of the previous paragraph, negated, is an sc⁺-section $s$ of $P|V$ with $(f|_V + s)(x) = 0$, and Theorem 3.1.18 (preprint 3.15) gives the germ datum of $f|_V + s$ at $x$, hence an index ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPathsLocal · StrongBundle.exists_isIndexAtLocal⟧. As for the global reading, a locally constant local index along a map from a preconnected space is constant ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPathsLocal · isIndexAtLocal_const_of_forall_eventually⟧.

**Proposition 6.2.2 (preprint 6.2) with the text's hypotheses** (Finding 365) ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPathsLocal · proposition_6_2_2_local⟧. *Let $f$ be an sc-Fredholm section with tame germ data of a strong bundle $P : Y \to X$ — no bump functions — and $\varphi : \mathbb R \to X$ an sc-smooth path. Then $\mathrm{ind}(f, \varphi(t))$, read with local sections, is one integer for all $t$*; any two values of the index at $\varphi(0)$ and at $\varphi(1)$ agree ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPathsLocal · proposition_6_2_2_local'⟧. The proof is that of (E), (S) and Proposition 6.2.2 (preprint 6.2) above, run near each $t_0$ on $P|V$, $V$ the domain of a chart $c$ around $\varphi(t_0)$ ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPathsLocal · isIndexAtLocal_eventually_const_of_path⟧. The path is reparametrized into $V$ as in (E), and is then an sc-smooth path of the M-polyfold $V$. The section along the path is the one of (E) with the cut-off as a datum ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPathsLocal · exists_isScPlusSection_pullback_of_path_of_bump⟧, taken equal to $1$, which is possible because the chart $c|_V$ of $P|V$ covers its base ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPathsLocal · exists_isScPlusSection_pullback_of_path_of_chart⟧. The product chart of $c|_V$ covers $\mathbb R \times V$, so the pull-back has sc⁺-sections through every smooth fibre point without cut-off ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPathsLocal · hasScPlusSectionsThrough_pullback_rn_of_chart⟧, and the index of $\tilde f + S$ is constant along the graph near $t_0$ ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPathsLocal · isLinOfIndex_paramLift_sectionAdd_eventually_const_of_sections⟧. The slice relation (S), applied to $P|V$ unchanged, turns it into $\mathrm{ind}(f|_V, \varphi(t)) = i - 1$ near $t_0$, a local index of $f$, and the connectedness of $\mathbb R$ ends the proof. *Against the text*, what remains is the domain of the path: it is $\mathbb R$, and a path sc-smooth on $[0, 1]$ only is not covered, the project having no M-polyfold $[0, 1]$. The hypotheses on $f$ are the text's, tame germ data being the reading of "tame strong bundle" of §III.47.

**The two readings agree under bump functions** (Finding 367) ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPathsLocalBump · StrongBundle.isIndexAtLocal_iff_isIndexAt⟧. If $X$ admits bump functions, a local section of Definition 6.2.1 (preprint 6.1) gives a global one with the same index ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPathsLocalBump · StrongBundle.IsIndexAtLocal.isIndexAt⟧: the local section $s$ on $V$ is restricted to $W = \operatorname{dom}\varphi \cap V$ for a chart $\varphi$ around $x$, read in that chart as $\tau : O \to F^1$, and cut off by a bump $\beta$ supported in $W$ and equal to $1$ near $x$; the cut-off of Lemma 5.3.3 (preprint 5.11) is made here in a chart of $P$ that need not belong to the atlas ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPathsLocalBump · StrongBundle.cutoffSection_mem_oneSet_of_isChart⟧ ⟦Chapter3/IndexAlongPathsLocalBump · StrongBundle.isScPlusSection_cutoffSection_of_isChart⟧. Then $f + \beta\tau$ vanishes at $x$, and its linearization there has the index of $(f|_W + s)'(x)$: the two sections agree where $\beta = 1$, so the two linearizations coincide in the chart restricted to that set, and the germ datum of $f + \beta\tau$ at $x$ carries the index to every chart. So the global definition of the beginning of this section and the text's local one are the same notion whenever the hypotheses of Chapter 5 hold, and only the text's is available without them.

### III.50 The 2021 edition: the filling without tameness (Definitions 3.1.4, 3.6.1 (preprint 3.4, 3.48)) and a corner example

**The 2021 filling.** Definition 3.1.4 (p. 91) and its repetition Definition 3.6.1 (p. 134) of the Springer edition ask the strong bundle retraction $R(u, h) = (r(u), \rho(u)h)$ to cover an *sc-smooth* retraction $r$ with $r(0) = 0$. The 2017 text and the preprint ask for a *tame* one. Otherwise the conditions are those of §III.3:
- (1) $\hat f = g$ on $O$ near $0$;
- (2) a point $y \in U$ near $0$ with $g(y) = \rho(r(y))g(y)$ lies in $O$;
- (3) the linearization at $0$ of $k(y) = [1 - \rho(r(y))]g(y)$, restricted to $\ker Dr(0)$, is a topological linear isomorphism onto $\ker\rho(0)$.

The formal definition is a structure with $r(0) = 0$ as a field and (1)–(3) in the germ reading of §III.48 ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · IsFilling2021⟧ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · HasFilling2021⟧. The 2017 filling adds the tameness of $r$ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · IsFilling2017⟧ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · isFilling2017_iff⟧.

**The project's filling is the 2021 one.** The germ filling of §III.3 never asked $r$ to be tame ⟦Chapter3/GermFilling · IsFillingGerm⟧. A 2021 filling is exactly a germ filling with $r(0) = 0$ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · isFilling2021_iff⟧ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · IsFilling2021.isFillingGerm⟧. Conversely, a germ filling is a 2021 filling as soon as $0 \in O$, because points of $O$ are fixed by $r$ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · IsFillingGerm.isFilling2021⟧; so is a whole-chart filled version ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · IsFilledVersion.isFilling2021⟧. In a chart centred at a point of $X$, $0 = \varphi(x_0)$ lies in $O$, so the text's standing assumption $r(0) = 0$ costs nothing there. A 2017 filling is a 2021 filling ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · IsFilling2017.isFilling2021⟧. Both notions are satisfiable: over the identity strong bundle retraction, which is tame, every sc-smooth $g$ fills itself ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · isFilling2017_id⟧, for instance $g = 0$ on $\mathbb R$ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · isFilling2017_rn⟧.

**A 2021 filling that is not a 2017 filling.** Take the following data:
- $E = \mathbb R \oplus \mathbb R$, $C = U = [0, \infty) \times \mathbb R$, $F = \mathbb R$;
- $R((a, b), h) = ((0, b), 0)$ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · CornerFilling.Rc⟧, a strong bundle retraction ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · CornerFilling.isStrongBundleRetraction_Rc⟧, so $r(a, b) = (0, b)$, $O = \{0\} \times \mathbb R$ and $\rho \equiv 0$ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · CornerFilling.ρCLM_Rc⟧;
- the zero section $\hat f = 0$ of $K \to O$, and $g(a, b) = a$ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · CornerFilling.gc⟧ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · CornerFilling.isScSmooth_gc⟧.

The derivatives at $0$ are $Dr(0)(a, b) = (0, b)$ and $Dg(0)(a, b) = a$ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · CornerFilling.scDeriv_r⟧ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · CornerFilling.scDeriv_g⟧. Since $\rho \equiv 0$, the fill defect $k$ is $g$ itself ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · CornerFilling.fillDefect_gc⟧. Now check the three conditions:
- (1) $g = 0$ on $O$;
- (2) $g(y) = \rho(r(y))g(y) = 0$ forces $y \in O$;
- (3) $\ker Dr(0) = \mathbb R \oplus 0$ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · CornerFilling.mem_ker_scDeriv_r⟧, and $Dk(0)$ maps it by $(a, 0) \mapsto a$ onto $\ker\rho(0) = \mathbb R$ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · CornerFilling.kerMap⟧ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · CornerFilling.kerMap_bijective⟧.

So $g$ is a filled version ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · CornerFilling.isFilledVersion_gc⟧. With $0 \in O$ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · CornerFilling.zero_mem_O⟧, it is a 2021 filling ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · CornerFilling.isFilling2021_gc⟧. But $r$ is not tame: $d_C(1, 0) = 0$, while $d_C(r(1, 0)) = d_C(0, 0) = 1$, against Definition 2.5.2 (1) (preprint 2.33 (1)) ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · CornerFilling.not_isTame_r⟧. So $g$ is not a 2017 filling ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · CornerFilling.not_isFilling2017_gc⟧, and the 2021 notion is strictly more general ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · exists_isFilling2021_not_isFilling2017⟧.

**Sc-Fredholm germs and sections.** With the 2021 filling in place of the filling of §III.6:
- the 2021 sc-Fredholm germ (Definition 3.1.11 = 3.6.3) ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · StrongBundle.IsScFredholmGerm2021⟧;
- the 2021 sc-Fredholm section (Definition 3.1.16 = 3.6.4) ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · StrongBundle.IsScFredholmSection2021⟧;
- the 2017 germ, with the 2017 filling ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · StrongBundle.IsScFredholmGerm2017⟧.

In a chart centred at $x_0$, $0 \in O$ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · StrongBundle.zero_mem_O_of_chart⟧. So the 2021 germ is the project's germ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · StrongBundle.isScFredholmGerm2021_iff⟧ ⟦Chapter3/GermFillingChart · StrongBundle.IsScFredholmGerm⟧, and the 2021 section the project's section ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · StrongBundle.isScFredholmSection2021_iff⟧ ⟦Chapter3/TameGermsG · StrongBundle.IsScFredholmSection⟧. The 2017 germ is the germ with tame germ data of §III.47 ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · StrongBundle.isScFredholmGerm2017_iff⟧ ⟦Chapter3/TameGermsG · StrongBundle.IsScFredholmGermTame⟧, and 2017 implies 2021 ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · StrongBundle.IsScFredholmGerm2017.isScFredholmGerm2021⟧. The graph section of a $C^\infty$ map $\mathbb R^n \to \mathbb R^k$ satisfies both ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · isScFredholmSection2021_graphSection⟧ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · isScFredholmGerm2017_graphSection⟧. Whether every 2021 germ is a 2017 germ is the question of Finding 353, the passage from germ data in some compatible chart to germ data in a tame one (gap G.79).

### III.51 The 2021 edition: the theorems whose proofs do not use tameness

The project's proofs of the following results never used the tameness of $r$; each 2021 statement is the earlier theorem transported through the equivalences of §III.50:
- **Proposition 3.1.5 (preprint 3.5)** (§III.4). If $g$ is a 2021 filling of $\hat f$ with $\hat f(0) = 0$, then $0 \in O$ by $r(0) = 0$, and $g(0) = \hat f(0) = 0$ by (1). So the germ form of the proposition applies ⟦Chapter3/GermFillerLinearization · IsFillingGerm.proposition_3_1_5⟧. $Dg(0)$ maps $T_0O = \operatorname{range} Dr(0)$ into $K_0 = \operatorname{range}\rho(0)$. The restriction $T\hat f(0) : T_0O \to K_0$ is onto iff $Dg(0)$ is, and is Fredholm iff $Dg(0)$ is, with the same index ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · proposition_3_1_5⟧.
- **Theorem 3.1.13 and Corollary 3.1.15 (preprint 3.12 and 3.13)** (§III.13) ⟦Chapter3/SectionCompactnessG · theorem_3_1_13_preprint⟧ ⟦Chapter3/SectionCompactnessG · corollary_3_1_15_preprint⟧ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · theorem_3_1_13⟧ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · corollary_3_1_15⟧.
- **Theorem 3.1.18 (preprint 3.15)** (§III.6) ⟦Chapter3/FredholmSectionsG · theorem_3_1_18_preprint⟧ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · theorem_3_1_18⟧.
- **Theorem 3.1.21 (preprint 3.18)** (§III.40) ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbationG · theorem_3_1_21_preprint⟧ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · theorem_3_1_21⟧.
- **Proposition 3.6.5 (preprint 3.52)** (§§III.21, III.30) ⟦Chapter3/FredholmGermLinearizationG · proposition_3_6_5_preprint⟧ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · proposition_3_6_5⟧.

So the 2021 change of the filling does not affect these statements, and the 2021 text's version of them is verified as printed.

### III.52 The 2021 edition: Theorem 3.6.8 (preprint 3.54), the step on p. 138

The proof of Theorem 3.6.8 (pp. 137–138) starts from a germ in good position (Definition 3.6.6, §III.21). There $\ker T\tilde f(0)$ is in good position to the partial cone $C_0O = T_0O \cap C$ in $T_0O$. The proof passes to the good position of $\ker Dg(0)$ to $C$ in $E$, the hypothesis of the implicit function theorem in the quadrant (§III.17). The passage uses an sc-complement of $T_0O$ inside the subspace $E_0$ of $C$ at $0$, namely $\ker Dr(0)$. Proposition 2.5.3 (preprint 2.35) supplies it for a tame retract. In the linear part of §III.23 this is the hypothesis $(1 - Dr(0))E \subseteq E_0$ ⟦Chapter3/GoodPositionGermG · theorem_3_6_8_linear⟧. The 2021 text keeps the sentence that the retract is tame by assumption, while its Definition 3.6.1 no longer assumes it.

**The step does not go through without tameness.** In the example of §III.50, every other hypothesis of the linear part holds:
- $g$ is a 2021 filling of $\hat f = 0$, $0 \in O$, $\hat f(0) = 0$, and $\hat f$ is a section of $K$;
- $g$ is sc¹ with derivative $Dg(0)$, and $Dg(0)$ is onto;
- the germ is in good position in the chart ⟦Chapter3/GoodPositionGermG · IsGoodPositionAtGerm⟧ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · CornerFilling.isGoodPositionAtGerm_gc⟧: $T\tilde f(0) : T_0O \to K_0 = 0$ is onto, and its kernel $T_0O = 0 \oplus \mathbb R$ is all of $T_0O \cap C$. Here $\ker Dg(0) = \operatorname{range} Dr(0)$ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · CornerFilling.ker_Lg_eq_range_Lr⟧.

But $(1 - Dr(0))(1, 0) = (1, 0)$ is not in $E_0 = 0 \oplus \mathbb R$ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · CornerFilling.not_range_le_minimalSubspace⟧. And $\ker Dg(0) = 0 \oplus \mathbb R$ is not in good position to $C$ in $E$ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · CornerFilling.not_isInGoodPosition_gc⟧, for two reasons:
- a complement $P$ contains a point $p$ with first coordinate $1$, from writing $(1, 0) = n + p$;
- the first coordinate vanishes at $(0, 1) \in \ker Dg(0) \cap C$, so by Lemma 3.8.8 (preprint 3.65) every good complement lies in $\{a = 0\}$ ⟦Chapter3/GoodPosition · lemma_3_8_8⟧.

The whole is one statement ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · theorem_3_6_8_tame_step⟧. So this step of the proof does not hold as we read it for the 2021 definitions. The conclusion of the theorem is not refuted by the example: its solution set $O \cong \mathbb R$ is a manifold. Since $X = \mathbb R$ there has no boundary, even the proof of Theorem 3.6.10 (preprint 3.56), where $\partial X = \emptyset$, passes through this step.

The project states Theorem 3.6.8 and what rests on it (Theorems 3.1.22, 3.1.26, Corollary 3.1.27, Theorems 3.6.10 and 3.6.11 (preprint 3.19, 3.23, 3.24, 3.56 and 3.57)) with tame germ data, the 2017 notion (§III.47). For the untamed 2021 data they are open (gap G.79). One way to close the gap would answer Finding 353: show that germ data in a compatible chart can be traded for germ data in a tame one.

### III.53 The 2021 edition: Theorem 3.5.1 (preprint 3.40) with the sequential conclusion

The 2021 statement of Theorem 3.5.1 (p. 118) adds a second conclusion. If points $(a_k, w_k)$ of the solution set $S$ converge to $0$ in $E_0$, then for every $m$ they eventually lie in $C_m$ and converge to $0$ there. The formal statement has both conclusions ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · theorem_3_5_1⟧, in the following setting:
- $h$ a basic germ on an sc-germ $G$ of neighbourhoods of $0$ in $C = \widetilde C \times W$, with $\widetilde C$ closed;
- $U = G_0$, $s$ an sc⁺-germ on $G$ with $s(0) = 0$, and $S = \{x \in U : h(x) + s(x) = 0\}$.

*The neighbourhoods.* Take the open sets $O'(m)$ of the local Theorem 3.1.13 of §III.12 ⟦Chapter3/LocalCompactness · theorem_3_1_13_local⟧ and put $O(m) = O'(m) \cap G_0$. These are nested, relatively open in $C$, contain $0$ and lie in $U$. The closure of $S \cap O(m)$ is taken in $E_0$. It lies in $C$, which is closed and contains $G_0$, and it lies in the image of $E_m$, because it is the image of the compact set $K_m$ of §III.12.

*The sequences.* A zero of $h + s$ solves $w = B(a, w) - \mathbf s(a, w)$ with $\mathbf s$ the $W$-component of $s$ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · IsBasicGerm.solves⟧. $\mathbf s$ is an sc⁰-germ into $W^1$ with $\mathbf s(0) = 0$. So the local Corollary 3.1.15 of §III.12 applies ⟦Chapter3/LocalCompactness · corollary_3_1_15_local⟧. For every $m$ there are $y_k \in E_m$ with $\iota_m(y_k) = x_k$ for $k$ large and $y_k \to 0$ in $E_m$; these are the lifts $L_mx_k$. "Converges on level $m$" is thus stated with level-$m$ lifts, as in Corollary 3.1.15 (preprint 3.13).

### III.54 The 2021 edition: cones and Lemmas 3.8.4–3.8.6, Corollary 3.8.7 (Appendix 3.8.1.3)

The appendix on "cones and partial quadrants in infinite dimensions" (pp. 145–149) is new in the 2021 edition. Its notions:
- a *closed convex cone* $K$ in a finite-dimensional space is a closed convex set with $K \cap (-K) = \{0\}$ and $tK \subseteq K$ for $t \ge 0$ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Items · IsClosedConvexCone⟧;
- $a$ *generates an extreme ray* if $a \in K \setminus \{0\}$, and $y \in K$, $a - y \in K$ imply $y \in \mathbb R_+a$ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Items · IsExtremeRayGen⟧.

A closed convex cone is pointed ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Items · IsClosedConvexCone.eq_zero⟧. $[0, \infty)$ is one, generated by the extreme ray of $1$ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Items · isClosedConvexCone_Ici⟧ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Items · isExtremeRayGen_one⟧.

The setting is the good position of §III.14: $C = T^{-1}([0, \infty)^n \oplus W)$ for a presentation $T$ (the text's case is $T = \mathrm{id}$), and $N$ a finite-dimensional subspace in good position. Within it:
- $N \cap C$ is the cone of $N$ ⟦Chapter3/GoodPosition · quadCone⟧;
- $\Gamma$, the union of the sets $\sigma_a = \{i : a_i = 0\}$ over $0 \ne a \in N \cap C$, is the vanishing index set ⟦Chapter3/GoodPosition · vanIdx⟧;
- $P : N \to \mathbb R^\Gamma$ is the coordinate projection ⟦Chapter3/GoodPosition · sigmaCoord⟧, whose kernel is $N \cap (\mathbb R^{\Gamma^c} \oplus W)$.

**The quadrant form of a pointed cone.** If $N \cap C$ is pointed, a linear isomorphism $e : N \cong \mathbb R^{\dim N}$ carries $N \cap C$ onto $[0, \infty)^{\dim N}$ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Items · exists_quadrantEquiv_of_pointed⟧.
