It comes from the normal form of §III.14, $N \cap C \cong [0, \infty)^k \times N_0$ ⟦Chapter3/GoodPosition · IsInGoodPosition.quadrantNormalForm⟧, in which pointedness forces $N_0 = 0$. The vectors $e^{-1}(e_j)$ generate extreme rays ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Items · isExtremeRayGen_symm_single⟧. A nonzero $a \in N \cap C$ has a coordinate $e(a)_j > 0$ with $a - e(a)_je^{-1}(e_j) \in N \cap C$ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Items · exists_sub_single_mem⟧. As in §III.14, this replaces the Krein–Milman-type Lemmas 3.8.1–3.8.3 (preprint 3.62–3.64): the cone is a quadrant at once.

**Lemma 3.8.4** ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Items · lemma_3_8_4⟧. Let $N^\perp$ be a good complement and $N \cap C$ a closed convex cone. Parts (1) and (3) say that $y_i = 0$ for $y \in N^\perp$ and $i \in \sigma_a$, $a$ a generator, respectively $i \in \Gamma$. They hold without the cone hypothesis, because they are Lemma 3.8.8 (preprint 3.65) (§III.14) ⟦Chapter3/GoodPosition · lemma_3_8_8⟧. For (2), $\Gamma = \bigcup_{a \text{ generator}} \sigma_a$, the inclusion $\supseteq$ is the definition. For $\subseteq$, let $0 \ne a \in N \cap C$ with $a_i = 0$, and take $j$ and $b = e^{-1}(e_j)$ as above. Then $0 \le (a - e(a)_jb)_i = -e(a)_jb_i \le 0$ forces $b_i = 0$, so $i \in \sigma_b$. The text writes $a$ as a sum of extreme-ray generators (Lemma 3.8.1); here only pointedness is used.

**Lemma 3.8.5** ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Items · lemma_3_8_5⟧. The text's statement is: either $N \subseteq \mathbb R^{\Gamma^c} \oplus W$, in which case $\dim N = 1$ and $\Gamma = \emptyset$, or $N \cap (\mathbb R^{\Gamma^c} \oplus W) = \{0\}$. The proof assumes $N \cap (\mathbb R^{\Gamma^c} \oplus W) \ne \{0\}$, and the formal statement is the dichotomy it shows. $P$ is onto ⟦Chapter3/GoodPosition · sigmaCoord_surjective⟧.
- *$\Gamma \ne \emptyset$.* A point $x$ of $N$ with $x_i = 0$ for $i \in \Gamma$ lies in $N \cap C$, and so does $-x$ ⟦Chapter3/GoodPosition · mem_quadCone_of_vanIdx⟧, so $x = 0$.
- *$\Gamma = \emptyset$ and $N \ne 0$.* The kernel is all of $N$. If $\dim N \ge 2$, the generators $b_0, b_1$ have all coordinates positive (no index vanishes on a nonzero point of the cone). Then $b_0 - tb_1 \in N \cap C$ for small $t > 0$, but its $e$-coordinate $j_1$ is $-t < 0$.
- *$N = 0$.* The kernel is $\{0\}$, and the first alternative holds.

*Read literally, the first alternative does not hold as we read it for $N = 0$* ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Items · lemma_3_8_5_not_literal⟧. In $E = \mathbb R$, $C = [0, \infty)$, the subspace $N = 0$ is in good position, $N \cap C = \{0\}$ is a closed convex cone, and $N \subseteq \mathbb R^{\Gamma^c} \oplus W$; but $\dim N = 0$ and $N \not\cong \mathbb R$. The dichotomy holds there through its other alternative.

**Lemma 3.8.6.** (1) If $N \cap (\mathbb R^{\Gamma^c} \oplus W) = \{0\}$, then $P$ is a linear isomorphism carrying $N \cap C$ onto $[0, \infty)^\Gamma$ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Items · lemma_3_8_6⟧. This needs no cone hypothesis: $P$ is injective by hypothesis and onto. A point whose $\Gamma$-coordinates are $\ge 0$ is in $N \cap C$ by the segment argument of §III.14 when $\Gamma \ne \emptyset$; when $\Gamma = \emptyset$, $N = 0$. (2) If $N \cap C$ is a closed convex cone, $N \subseteq \mathbb R^{\Gamma^c} \oplus W$ and $N \ne 0$, then $\dim N = 1$ by Lemma 3.8.5. A linear isomorphism $N \cong \mathbb R$ then carries $N \cap C$ onto $[0, \infty)$ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Items · lemma_3_8_6_two⟧. As printed, (2) does not hold as we read it for $N = 0$, by the same example. With $N \ne 0$ its hypotheses are met by $N = E = \mathbb R$, $C = [0, \infty)$ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Items · halfLine_witness⟧.

**Corollary 3.8.7.** If $N \cap C$ is a closed convex cone, it is a partial quadrant in $N$, and $(N, N \cap C) \cong (\mathbb R^{\dim N}, [0, \infty)^{\dim N})$ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Items · corollary_3_8_7⟧. The first part is Proposition 3.1.25 (preprint 3.22) (§III.14), which needs no cone hypothesis ⟦Chapter3/GoodPosition · proposition_3_1_25⟧. The second is the quadrant form above, which the text also derives on p. 149.

### III.55 What the 2021 additions taught us

- **The 2021 filling is the formalization's.** The germ filling never asked for tameness. The 2021 text drops it, so the formal definitions and the printed ones now agree ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · isFilling2021_iff⟧ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · StrongBundle.isScFredholmGerm2021_iff⟧. The 2017 notion is strictly narrower ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · exists_isFilling2021_not_isFilling2017⟧.
- **One proof step needs the dropped hypothesis.** All the theorems of Chapter 3 that the project proved without tameness hold for the 2021 definitions as printed. The passage of Theorem 3.6.8 from good position in $T_0O$ to good position in $E$ (p. 138) does not hold as we read it without a tame chart ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Germs · theorem_3_6_8_tame_step⟧. The implicit function theorems remain verified with tame germ data, and open for untamed data (G.79).
- **Krein–Milman is not needed.** A pointed cone in good position is a quadrant ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Items · exists_quadrantEquiv_of_pointed⟧. Lemma 3.8.4 (2) follows by one subtraction, and (1) and (3) need no cone hypothesis.
- **$N = 0$.** Lemma 3.8.5, read as an implication, and Lemma 3.8.6 (2) need $N \ne 0$ ⟦Chapter3/Book2021Items · lemma_3_8_5_not_literal⟧. The dichotomy the proof shows holds for all $N$.
